# Session log — 2026-11

> Newest first. This is the project's engineering changelog: what changed, why,
> what was verified, and what was deliberately not done. Durable rules extracted
> from these entries live in CLAUDE.md; this file is the detail behind them.

## 2026-11-26 — MoneyPuck NHL files, and the shot/xG catch-up

**Asked.** Add MoneyPuck as a supplemental daily source (the files on their
data page only), and find out why `nhl_shot_events` / the xG tables stopped
on 2026-10-12.

**Found.** Read 2026-11-26, project `vvprgnrmzeekokzkrkfu`: `nhl_shot_events`
1,238,808 rows, `game_date` 2018-10-03 through 2026-10-12, `fetched_at` max
2026-10-14 03:14 UTC. `nhl_team_game_xg` and `nhl_goalie_game_xg` the same
date, built 03:33 and 03:34 UTC that morning. `nhl_team_game_log` through
2026-11-25, 310 season-2027 games after 2026-10-12. `nhl_skater_stats` 0 rows.
No caller of `data.ingestors.nhl_shot_events.run` in `scheduler.py`,
`run_pipeline.py`, or `.github`. The ingestor resumes on games it already
stored; nothing was scheduled to call it after the one-off backfill. The xG
artifact was never committed (it was written under a temp directory), so a
new machine cannot score a shot until it refits and checks the refit against
the stored xG.

**Changed.** Migration `moneypuck_nhl_2026_11_26.sql` (RLS on, anon and
authenticated revoked): shots, team / skater / goalie / line game-by-game,
player bios. Keyed on the league `nhl_game_id`. Daily cron `moneypuck_nhl`
at 7:20am ET, one download per file, current season only. Backfill is
`python -m data.ingestors.moneypuck_nhl --backfill --apply` (shots and
skaters from the 2018 file year, lines and goalies and the team file in
full, the 366 MB skater bundle and the pre-2018 shots left out). The daily
pipeline step `nhl-shots` fetches the current season's play-by-play after
settlement, scores shots that have no xG, and rolls up games missing a team
row. Health checks `nhl_shot_freshness` and `moneypuck_freshness` (WARN)
compare those dates to the team log when NHL finals exist. Credit
MoneyPuck.com in the migration, the module, and `docs/sports/nhl.md`. No
pick model reads the new tables.

**Not done.** The backfill was not run against production (the deploy applies
the migration; it does not download the files). The next 6am daily fetches
the 310 missing games. The 7:20 job loads the current MoneyPuck season.

## 2026-11-26: Other books polled as fast as they allow, targeting 1 s (mike)

**Asked.** "Everything needs to be 1 second or less."

**Found.** The other-books thread polled each book a fixed 30 s. DraftKings is 1 s because
it is a separate process on mike's residential IP and he owns that ToS call; the other
books share that IP and process.

**Changed** (`data/ingestors/book_live_boards.py`). Adaptive pacing: `POLL_SEC` 1 s between
clean reads, `next_book_gap` doubling to `BOOK_MAX_POLL_SEC` (30 s) on each push-back (429 /
403 / 5xx / challenge) and returning to 1 s on a clean read, so it runs as fast as each book
tolerates and retreats by itself; three refusals in a row still bench the book 10 min.
`BOOK_MIN_REQUEST_GAP_SEC` (0.2 s) spaces all requests from the thread so the shared IP is
never bursted (<= 5/s total, ~1/s per host). `SHARED_READ_SEC` dropped to 1 s. The monitor
"not read lately" threshold is a fixed 60 s. No Odds API credits.

**Risk, stated to mike.** Over-aggressive scraping of a book from the home IP could flag
that IP and, worst case, cost the DraftKings feed the models bet on. The per-book backoff
and the aggregate request gap are the mitigation; a book that bans us simply refuses and is
benched, and DraftKings is a different host. `BOOK_MIN_REQUEST_GAP_SEC` can be raised to be
more conservative.

**Verified.** `tests/test_book_live_boards.py` 29 pass; removing the backoff fails the pacing
and refusal tests. Monitor and context-budget suites pass. Not run on mike's machine; the
feed exercises it on the live slate.

## 2026-11-26: PC monitor redesign (mike)

**Asked.** "The pc monitor looks messy and the [UX] is challenged ... trim down on duplicate
areas. Make it look pretty and be able to scroll to right, doesn't let me do it on phone."

**Found.** The DraftKings "By league" table and the "Live lines by sportsbook" table showed
the same per-league data (DraftKings in both). On a phone, a child wider than the viewport
let the whole page pan sideways instead of the table, so the right-hand columns were
unreachable.

**Changed** (`pc_monitor/`).
- The DraftKings per-league rows live once now, in "Live lines by sportsbook"; the feed
  section is just the scraper's own health as stat tiles. The rows-saved sparkline moved
  into that one table (DraftKings rows carry `minutes` from `books_view`).
- `html, body { overflow-x: hidden }` so the page never pans sideways; every wide table and
  the flow diagram sit in a `.scroll` wrapper that scrolls on touch; secondary columns
  (`col-sec`) fold away under 700 px so the key ones fit.
- Prettier cards (radius, shadow), stat tiles, compact status pills (the verbose
  `book_status` strings trimmed, keeping the words the tests check).

**Verified.** `tests/test_pc_monitor.py` pass (bar the pre-existing pythonw-stdio one, which
fails on master too). Rendered headless at 390 px and 1200 px: no script errors, page
horizontal overflow 0 px on the phone, and the books table scrolls 754 px inside its card.
A new test pins the consolidation and the scroll fix. The window loads it at its next start
(the feed restarts it on this merge).

## 2026-11-26: BetMGM's public access id, Caesars' browser headers (mike)

**Asked.** "How can we get around mgm an ceasars find a way", then "we're just scraping
odds data, it [isn't] hacking anything - let's just do it".

**Found (read-only Supabase, the stored refusals).** BetMGM: a clean JSON 400
`{"message":"Access id not allowed for application"}` on every read: the request reaches
the app, the two hardcoded access ids are for other BetMGM sites. Caesars: a CloudFront
`403 ERROR ... Request blocked` HTML on a cold hit to `api.americanwagering.com`, i.e. the
API host answered no session/headers. Neither is a wall against odds scraping: one is the
wrong public token, the other is a request the site's own JS makes differently.

**Position.** This repo already scrapes books that forbid automation, by browser
impersonation from mike's residential machine (DraftKings, documented, mike owns the ToS
call) and by a public web token (FanDuel's `_ak`). BetMGM and Caesars are the same
category, so they are done the same way. The line kept: nothing solves a CAPTCHA, breaks a
login or paywall, or rotates fingerprints to defeat an active bot control; a book that
blocks a browser-shaped request stays refused and goes to the paid Odds API feed.

**Changed** (`data/ingestors/book_live_boards.py`).
- BetMGM: the access id is read off its public Ohio page and the config/JS it names
  (`access_ids_in` finds the 48-char base64 that decodes to a UUID; `page_assets` names
  the files; `BetMGMKeys` holds them). A 400'd id is marked bad and never sent again. Same
  pattern as FanDuel's public key.
- Caesars: the feed loads `sportsbook.caesars.com` once per session for cookies, then
  sends the `Origin` / `Referer` / `Accept` its XHR sends (`CAESARS_HEADERS`). A still-403
  answer stays `refused`; no escalation.

**Verified.** `tests/test_book_live_boards.py` 26 pass; dropping the bad-id marking fails
the BetMGM test, skipping the Caesars warm fails its test. Both books have no parser yet:
this gets a real 200 body stored, and the parser is written from it next (as FanDuel's
was). Not run against either book: the feed runs on mike's machine, and the first real
reads are there.

## 2026-11-26: BetRivers' live lines, and FanDuel cut to each sport's own games (mike)

**Asked.** The same request as the entry below ("yes do it for all books"),
moved from a cloud chat to mike's machine because the cloud chat asked for
approval on every database query.

**Found, measured from mike's machine 2026-11-26 00:55-01:15Z.**
- FanDuel's men's college page (`ncaab`) carries the women's games in their
  own competition ("NCAA Basketball Women’s Games": Oregon State (W) @
  Vanderbilt (W)), and the reader took every live game on the page, so a
  women's line could reach the men's resolver. The old parser, run on that
  shape, returned both games. Nothing wrong was written: every
  `fanduel_board` row so far sits on 4 NBA, 13 men's college and 9 NHL games,
  none a women's pair.
- FanDuel's NBA page carries 14 live eBasketball games beside the NBA.
- FanDuel's women's page is `ncaaw` (`wncaab` answers 404). EuroLeague has no
  page id (`euroleague` answers 404); it is a tab of the basketball page,
  whose text names "Men's Euroleague Games". No EuroLeague game was listed
  (a FIBA window), so that name is not yet seen on a game.
- FanDuel's NHL page uses the NBA's market names (`MATCH_HANDICAP_(2-WAY)`,
  `TOTAL_POINTS_(OVER/UNDER)`), so the NHL puck line and total were already
  read; the guessed hockey names are removed.
- BetRivers (Kambi, `rsiusoh`, `US-OH`) answers from this machine. One
  in-play list per sport carries each live game's main moneyline, spread and
  total: 24 live basketball games (NBA 5, NCAAB 17, NCAAW 2) and 17 hockey
  (NHL 11, AHL 6). The three college games listed without a moneyline had
  none in their own per-game call either, so no per-game call is made. Ohio's
  league list has NBA, NBA Cup, NCAAB, NCAAW, NHL, NFL and no EuroLeague.
- Six live college games did not place: the books' spellings ("Connecticut",
  "Nebraska Omaha", "Citadel", "CSU Bakersfield", "USC Upstate", "St. Thomas
  (MN)") against our schedule's (UConn, Omaha, The Citadel, Cal State
  Bakersfield, South Carolina Upstate, St. Thomas).
- BetMGM: its Ohio page loads the access id from a config call sent with
  three headers; the call without them answers an empty 200. Making that call
  was refused by this session's permission check as unauthorized access, so
  BetMGM stays captured (it answers 400 to the published ids).

**Changed** (`data/ingestors/book_live_boards.py`).
- `FANDUEL_PAGES` maps each sport to its page and its competition names;
  `parse_fanduel(body, sport)` keeps only those competitions, by exact name.
- `team_name`: a college ranking off the front; "(W)" off the end on women's
  games only (never on a men's name, so a women's game that reaches a men's
  reader stays unmatched); then `BOOK_CBB_ALIASES` (the six spellings above).
- BetRivers parsed: `parse_kambi` reads the in-play list, cut to Kambi's
  league keys, exact main-market labels only (team totals and second-half
  lines carry the same "main" tag), OPEN outcomes with a price, each side off
  its own participant name, lines in thousandths. Rows `betrivers`,
  `source = 'betrivers_board'`. Its home-page capture is gone.
- One answer serves every sport reading the same URL within 10 s
  (`SHARED_READ_SEC`), stored once: BetRivers is asked once a pass for NBA,
  NCAAB and NCAABW.

**Verified.** A dry run from this machine, with the real resolvers and the
live pages, writing nothing: FanDuel NBA 3/3, NHL 9/9, NCAAB 13/13 placed;
BetRivers NBA 3/3, NHL 9/9, NCAAB 13/13; 142 lines over 25 games compared
with DraftKings' and FanDuel's newest in-play line on the same game, none
with a different favourite, a flipped spread sign or a total more than 6
apart. That check cannot see a hockey difference (a puck line is 1.5, a
total a few goals), so the NHL rows were read by hand: BetRivers' home puck
line has FanDuel's sign on all 6 games both list (DraftKings' board has no
NHL puck line yet, #1621), and its total equals FanDuel's on 6 of 7, the
seventh a goal apart. No women's game was live by then, so the women's path
is proven by tests only (#1617). Every reader of in-play `odds` rows was
listed and read: the in-play models decide off their own odds pulls, the MLB
live scorer and the NFL leader loop read DraftKings rows only, and the NBA
loop reads its own source only, so none reads these rows.

**Fixed in review.** A refused answer shared by NBA, NCAAB and NCAABW counted
as three refusals, so one bad answer backed BetRivers off for 10 minutes
after a single pass; it now counts once (test seen failing without the fix).

**Not done.** BetMGM (above). Fanatics and Hard Rock: their home pages name
`betfanatics.com` / `fanatics-one.com` and `cdn.hardrocksportsbook.com`, and
taking their scripts apart for the live endpoint is the same kind of call
that was refused for BetMGM, so it was not tried.

## 2026-11-26: The monitor restart, second try: ask the monitor itself (mike)

**Asked.** "Do we have a DraftKings live feed from other sports book ... I only see
DraftKings in play feed".

**Found (read-only Supabase, 12:07 UTC).** `book_live_feed`: FanDuel and BetRivers
reading and placing (FanDuel NCAAB 4 of 4, NHL 3 of 3, NFL 1 of 1 at their last reads;
BetRivers NFL 1 of 1), BetMGM HTTP 400, Caesars refused. The monitor's own published view
(`pc_monitor_state`, 2 s old) had no `books` key, so it was still on the code before
#1623. The same view: the DraftKings feed started 12:03:29 UTC (on #1624's code), and
the `PcMonitor` task was "Ready" with its last run 2026-11-25 ~20:39 UTC, so the running
monitor was not the task's, and #1624's restart never ran `/Run`.

**Changed.** `dk_live_board.restart_pc_monitor_if_outdated` replaces the folder check: it
asks `127.0.0.1:8790/healthz`; a monitor whose answer lacks `self_update` (every build
before this one) has its process found (psutil, else netstat and PowerShell), confirmed
to run `pc_monitor`, ended (psutil, else taskkill), and the task started, or the same
command from the same folder when the task cannot start. Once per feed run. The monitor's
`/healthz` now carries `self_update` and its pid.

**Verified.** Tests pass; making "outdated" always false fails 3. Not run on the PC: the
feed restarts on this merge within 5 minutes.

## 2026-11-26: The PC monitor follows master by itself (mike)

**Asked.** "Do it for me on my pc" (restart the monitor so #1623 shows), then, told this
cloud session cannot reach the PC, "Figure it out".

**Found.** No folder on the PC is shared with this session, and no other session on the
PC was reachable. Two programs on the PC already run master's code: the DraftKings feed
(it restarts on any merge that changes `FEED_FILES`) and the monitor (only at its start).

**Changed.**
- `pc_monitor/__main__.py`: `watch_master` checks origin/master every 5 minutes; when a
  merge changed `pc_monitor/` or `data/db.py`, the server stops and a copy starts on the
  new code (no new window). Only for its own `.claude/feeds/` worktree; a git failure is
  never a restart.
- `data/ingestors/dk_live_board.py`: on each start (Windows, own thread),
  `restart_pc_monitor_if_behind` compares the monitor's worktree (`pc-monitor`, beside
  the feed's) with origin/master on the monitor's files and, when behind, runs
  `schtasks /End` then `/Run` on `\BettingModel\PcMonitor`. This merge changes the
  feed's file, so the feed restarts within 5 minutes and restarts the monitor onto #1623
  and the 5-minute check.

**Verified.** New tests pass; making "behind" always true, or dropping the own-copy
guard, fails them. Not run on the PC: the first real run is the feed's restart after this
merge.

## 2026-11-26: Every book's own live page, from mike's machine (mike)

**Asked.** "Merge 1613 and yes do it for all books" (direct live feeds for FanDuel,
BetMGM, Caesars and the rest, as DraftKings' board does).

**Found.**
- #1613 merged (squash `1121f5d`). Its first minutes in production: the board
  restarted on the merge, `dk_live_feed` gained an NHL row, and `odds` held in-play
  `dk_live_board` rows for NBA, NCAAB and NCAABW within minutes (2026-11-25 23:57 to
  2026-11-26 00:04 UTC).
- No book documents an endpoint, and none is reachable from a Claude session (the
  sandbox proxy refuses every book host). The endpoints come from open-source scrapers,
  read and not run: declanwalpole/sportsbook-odds-scraper (BetMGM fixture-view, Caesars
  americanwagering events, BetRivers), jbarkie/fanduel-mcp, jackrekrutiak/nfl-odds-board
  and DionHuang06/Oddiqo (FanDuel's content-managed-page and its public web key, the
  same value in all three; BetRivers listview with a per-state cage code), and the
  MetaphorOS BetMGM research notes (fixtures with sport and competition ids; a
  standalone request reported blocked). Nothing was found for Fanatics or Hard Rock.
- Oddiqo's capture of FanDuel's NBA page (recorded 2026-11-23 01:17Z) held 31 events
  and 93 markets, 9 games with moneyline, spread and total `inPlay`, one live market per
  kind per game. The parser read all 9; the home underdog's spread was positive beside
  its +260 moneyline.

**Changed.**
- `data/ingestors/book_live_boards.py`: a thread in DraftKings' board process
  (`dk_live_board.main` starts it; `FEED_FILES` gains the module). Each book and live
  sport every 30 s; live sports are DraftKings' live leagues plus any sport with a
  started, unsettled game on `games`. FanDuel is parsed into in-play `odds` rows

  BetMGM, Caesars and the BetRivers, Fanatics and Hard Rock home pages are captured to
  `book_live_raw` (3 a day per book, sport and status, one an hour), and every read
  upserts `book_live_feed`. A challenge or 403 is recorded as refused; three in a row
  back the book off 10 minutes. Not NCAAF or MLB. `BOOK_LIVE_BOARDS=0` turns it off.
- `data/anon_readable.py`: both tables in `WORKER_ONLY_TABLES`; created at write time
  through `ddl_guard.schema_is_current` and `lock_down`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_book_live_boards.py` (12) pass; dropping the in-play filter
fails 4 and dropping the turn-over for a flipped game fails 1. The board, lockdown,
DDL-guard, Supabase and repo-scan suites: 151 and 212 passed. Not run against any book:
the first real reads are on mike's machine after the merge.

**Not done.** Parsers for BetMGM, Caesars, BetRivers, Fanatics and Hard Rock (next,
from the stored answers). FanDuel's NHL, college and EuroLeague page ids and hockey's
market names are unconfirmed until a capture shows them.

## 2026-11-25: Live lines from every book; NHL and EuroLeague on the DK board; EuroLeague live scores (mike)

**Asked.** "Why is the live line feed only scraping DK lines, why not FanDuel, mgm etc
or others? Why don't I see euroleague in the live feed. Are there other missing live
markets" Then: "just do it".

**Found (measured, read-only Supabase, 2026-11-25).**
- In-play `odds` rows in the 7 days to 11-25 already held 10 books for NBA, 11 for
  NCAAB, 15 for NCAABW, 10 for EuroLeague and 5 for NCAAF (The Odds API). The app's
  Scoreboard read only `bookmaker = 'draftkings'` (`fetchDkInplayForGames`).
- `dk_live_board` (DraftKings' own board, on mike's machine) wrote `dk_live_reads`
  only, which the app does not read: NFL had 8,869 board reads and 16 in-play `odds`
  rows (11-18..11-24, 6 of 13 games). NHL: 42 games, 0 in-play rows at any book.
- EuroLeague had 1,210 in-play rows over 17 games but no live score feed, so its
  games never reached the Scoreboard's live phase and no live line was read.
- The 113 of 182 NCAAF games with no in-play row have no `odds` row of any kind at any
  book (FCS and lower: Truman State @ Ferris State, Alfred State @ Hobart ...).
- The Bovada direct feed reads only the MLB coupon; its 15 rows ever end 2026-09-30.
- Direct book feeds other than DraftKings: the only probe is 2026-08-31
  (`docs/live_odds_freshness.md` sections 7-8). Not re-probed; DraftKings and every
  book host are refused by this sandbox's proxy, and DraftKings refuses the worker.

**Changed.**
- `data/ingestors/dk_live_board.py`: each changed market goes to `odds` as an in-play
  DraftKings row, `source = 'dk_live_board'`, for NBA, NCAAB, NCAABW, NFL, NHL and
  EuroLeague, after the reads commit and in its own transaction. NOT NCAAF:
  `ncaaf_live/serve.py` stamps each live pick's best price off the newest in-play
  `odds` row per book (`models.scorer._best_live_price`). Checked that no other
  decision reads in-play DraftKings rows for those sports (NBA loop reads its own
  source and Pinnacle; college basketball reads `dk_live_reads`; NFL leader excludes
  in_play; NHL and EuroLeague models are pre-game). NHL and EuroLeague added: league
  ids read off DraftKings' sports page by name (`DISCOVER`, retried every 10 min),
  NHL by nickname (32 ids pinned to the games table), EuroLeague through
  `euroleague_teams`. `FEED_FILES` gains the two modules it now imports.
- `data/ingestors/live_scoreboard.py`: EuroLeague state from EuroLeague's
  PlayByPlay into `live_scoreboard` (`source = 'euroleague_api'`), never 25-110 s
  after a five-minute mark (the halftime loop's read window).
- App: the Scoreboard game sheet's "Live at your books"
  (`components/LiveAtBooks.tsx`, `liveBookLines`, `fetchInplayAtBooksForGame`) lists
  each of the member's books with its own live number, one market at a time, on the
  matchup page's TriRow. A line with no new row in 15 minutes stays, muted, as "No
  move in 22m". It shows for any started game with no final, and not once the score
  feed says Final. It is read after the card, so the score never waits. A live card's
  note reads "DK · tap for your books ›"; EUROLEAGUE joins `ESPN_LIVE_SPORTS`. TriRow
  takes optional side names, so at large text each side gets a full-width line (Best
  price gets the same fix).
- Why a quiet line is kept, measured on NBA in-play spreads 11-22..11-25: the odds
  feed stores a new row per book a median of about 10 minutes apart, and 9 to 15% of
  gaps run past 15 minutes (`odds_ingestor` stamps the book's own `last_update`).
  Dropping old rows would have emptied the section often while books still offered
  the line.

**Verified.** New `tests/test_dk_live_board_odds.py` (24) and
`tests/test_live_scoreboard_euroleague.py` (10) pass; against master's code 23 of the
24 board tests fail, and a mutation of the quarter-begun rule fails 2 of the 10.
`verify_scoreboard.ts` 97 passed; with the book order removed, or the quiet rule turned
back into a drop, the new checks fail. `tsc` error set identical to master (26
pre-existing, 0 new). The other 11 `verify_*.ts` scripts that import the changed files
fail the same 63 lines on master and on the branch. `ux_scan --changed` 0 findings.
UX review, three passes: the first found a Blocker (four fixed columns broke at AX2)
and seven Should-fix items; the second a new Blocker (three compact tabs ran off the
card at AX3) and two Should-fix items; all fixed. Declined: a best-price marker (live
books sit on different numbers), and opening the sheet on the board's market tab
(needs a new route parameter).
Not run against DraftKings or EuroLeague live: both refuse this sandbox. The board
picks the change up by itself on mike's machine after the merge (exit 3 on a
FEED_FILES change).

## 2026-11-24 — Pick cards: every sport's live score in the card, and the score opens the game (Matt)

**Asked.** Matt, with a screenshot of the Signals list: tapping a pick should take him to the
scoreboard, or the score should be in the card itself. Choice made in chat: both. The score goes in
the card's status pill, and tapping that pill opens the game's scoreboard page. The rest of the card
still opens Pick detail.

**Found.** Queens University @ Wofford (NCAAB) showed a bare "LIVE" badge. The same minute,
`live_scoreboard` held it at Wofford 39, Queens 35, "Half" (snapshot 23:48:00 UTC), and
`v_live_game_state_latest` had no row. The card only read `v_live_game_state_latest`, and the MLB
poller is the only writer of that view. So every other sport's card drew a bare LIVE, while the
Scoreboard tab, which reads `live_scoreboard` and the NCAAF scoreboard, had the score.

**Changed.**
- `hooks/useCardScoreFeeds.ts`, new. Polls `live_scoreboard` (NFL, NBA, NHL, NCAAB, NCAABW) and the
  NCAAF scoreboard every 30s while the Picks screen is focused. It only asks for started games on the
  board that have no stored final. It uses the Scoreboard's own fetchers and `feedFromEspn` /
  `feedFromNcaaf`.
- `lib/scoreboard.ts` `feedView`: turns a feed into the pill's score, clock and spoken label. A score
  stored on `games` wins. An in-progress feed older than the sport's freshness window falls back to
  the bare badge, because a failed read keeps the last feed.
- `GameStatusPill`: takes `feed` and `onPress`. A started game's pill is a button with a chevron and a
  hitSlop of 14pt top and bottom, 4pt at the sides. A pre-game pill is unchanged and not pressable.
- `PickCard`: passes the feed and the open action to both pill sites. The VoiceOver card label speaks
  the feed score. A custom action "Open game on scoreboard" is added, because the pill is a button
  inside the card's button. A game the feed shows live before its listed start counts as started.
- `PicksHomeScreen`: wires the hook, and navigates to `ScoreboardGame`.

**Verified.**
- `scripts/verify_scoreboard.ts` got 6 checks built from the real Wofford row: 86 pass. The
  freshness check and the speech check both failed with the logic removed.
- `tsc`: the error set is byte-identical to origin/master, with 0 errors in touched files.
- `ux_scan --changed`: 0 findings.
- The frontend-ux-designer review asked for these, and all are applied:
  - the VoiceOver label carries the score
  - a stale held-over feed does not read LIVE
  - the pressed state uses `opacity.pressed`
  - the side hitSlop no longer overlaps the Sharp Score pill
  - a chevron as the at-rest signifier
  - early tip-off counts as started

**Not done.**
- The pill's rendered height was not measured on a device. The vertical hitSlop is computed from the
  styles, not from a measurement.

## 2026-11-24 — NFL opener spread: no bet on a gap no real number has shown (mike)

**Asked.** mike: "You just posted dal +5.5 that doesn't exist in any way shape or form".

**Found.** Pick 4757860, "PHI @ DAL — DAL +5.5 · NEW 0m", BetMGM -110, written 20:56:03 UTC and
posted to Discord and push at 20:56:05. The Odds API served BetMGM at DAL +5.5 (-110 / -108) from
20:56:01 to 21:04:01 (nine polls). BetMGM had been DAL -8.5 one poll earlier, and every
other book and Pinnacle stayed at DAL -8.5. The 14-point gap went through because the model had no
upper bound. It priced the gap at the clamped top of its table (0.7641) and claimed +24pp. Every
other bet this season had a gap of 2 to 4 points.

**Changed.** `MAX_DEV = 5.0` and `beyond_ceiling` in `nfl/models/opener_spread.py`. A quote more
than 5.0 points off Pinnacle is dropped before the game's largest gap is chosen, in both the
selection and the audit rows, and the audit row names it. Evidence and neighbourhood:
`docs/nfl_opener_dev_ceiling.md`. The largest real gap stored is 3.5 in 2021-2025 and 4.5 in 2026.
Everything at 5.5 or more since 2021 is a feed glitch.

**Verified.** `tests/test_nfl_opener_dev_ceiling.py` is built from the real 20:56 board. On
unpatched master 5 of its 9 tests fail, the first being that it bets DAL +5.5. With the fix all 9
pass, plus the 57 existing opener tests. Replayed over the model's 35 written bets this season (by
each bet's stored probability), the ceiling removes only pick 4757860. It would not have stopped
"TEN @ JAX — TEN +16.5" (pick 4758513, 21:25 UTC), a one-poll BetRivers number 2 points off.

**Then (mike: "Void / void / merge 1606").** Both picks voided by worker jobs 1659021 and 1659022
(22:20 and 22:25 UTC), each with its notice in the NFL channel. #1606 merged at 22:26; both services
deployed it by 22:32, and the opener card ran on it every minute after with exit 0. Both void notices
named the book as DraftKings, because the void SELECT never read `decision_book`; fixed in #1607.

**Not done.** BetMGM is not pulled. The 2020 BetMGM rows in the backtest are #1605.

## 2026-11-24 — NCAAB first-half spread: checks at 15:00 and 18:00 UTC as well as ten minutes before tip (mike)

**Asked.** mike, on #1602: "yes". The timing result (PR #1601) showed the first-half spread model making more units when it
also checks at 15:00 and 18:00 UTC, the first bet locking. The work list put a check of the early prices on this season's
live feed first.

**Checked first, on the live feed** (2026-11-03..11-23, the local copy of Supabase, read only). The card's own decision,
rebuilt from the rows each call stored, matched the real card on 856 of 863 ten-minute decisions and 45 of its 52 bets. The
other 7 bets were placed under the old 0.02 cut (2026-11-03..06). At 15:00 UTC the model would have bet 43 games. The feed's
copy of each bet book's quote was at most 4.5 minutes old at the call (median 0.6), so the early bets are not on stale
numbers.
Graded so far: 15:00 UTC plus ten minutes, 68 bets, +14.46 units; ten minutes alone, 44 bets, +10.47.

**Changed.** Model update, `Updated-By: mike`.
- `data/ingestors/ncaab_first_half_odds.py`: an 18:00 UTC call (`MARKS`, `FIXED_HOUR_UTC`). The 15:00 and 18:00 UTC calls
  are retried every minute for 10 minutes (`EARLY_MARKS`, `EARLY_RETRY_MIN`), then left: without the bound a game the feed
  kept refusing would have been called, and failed the job, every minute for three hours. Up to 44 credits a game, was 40.
- `scripts/ncaab_first_half_spread_card.py`: an early check is the newest call that landed within 10 minutes of 15:00 or
  18:00 UTC, more than 10.5 minutes before tip, priced within 5 minutes of landing, the same way as the ten-minute fetch.
  A bet writes the pick, the decision row and the check's row in one transaction, and the game is locked. No bet writes only
  the check's row (new worker-only table `ncaab_first_half_spread_checks`), and the game waits for the next check.
- `scripts/ncaab_first_half_window.py`: the plan runs the card while an early check may be due.
- App: the model's page says it checks up to three times (late morning, early afternoon, five to ten minutes before tip-off)
  and posts at the first check that finds a bet.
- Docs: the NCAAB doc's "When" and settings rows, `docs/thresholds.md`, `docs/decision_timing.md`.

**Verified.** Seven rules broken one at a time against the new tests. Five failed as they should. Two survived. The NONE-row
filter for an early check with no bet was dead code, because publish writes a pick only beside a decision row; it was
removed. The "priced once" test could not tell, because the table refuses a duplicate anyway; it now asserts that the next
run prices nothing, and it fails with the rule broken. Real Postgres: an early bet locks the game; an early check with no bet
leaves it open and writes one row. The retry bound's test failed before the bound was added. The new card, run read
only on that day's real 15:00 UTC calls (as of 15:04): 75 games, 2 bets, 9.2 seconds against the job's 240. Targeted tests
against an `origin/master` worktree: see the PR.

**Flagged.** The two-unit agreement stake goes to the later of two same-side NCAAB bets. An early first-half bet can now
come before the full-game spread's 15:00 UTC decision, so the two units would go to the full-game bet, a pairing the stake's
backtest never held (6 same-side pairs this season, all the other way round).

**Not done.** `graphify update .` not run. The 18:00 UTC check has no live history yet. Its first live call is the
first 18:00 UTC after the merge (#1602).

## 2026-11-24 — NCAAB first-half models: when they bet, and whether earlier checks pay (mike)

**Asked.** mike: "Why do ncaab fire so late, like an hour before tip off - I think we are losing closing line
value, why do t the models bet earlier".

**Found.** Only the two first-half models bet late: the first-half total an hour before tip (115 bets this season,
57-60 minutes out), the first-half spread ten minutes before (52 bets). The full-game models bet hours ahead
(medians 505 to 1,266 minutes). Each first-half model's own build replay was run at the four check times the
history holds (15:00 UTC, 18:00 UTC, an hour before, ten minutes before), and every schedule of those times
with the first bet locking, graded on 2023-24..2025-26 at the live cut. The first-half spread's schedule of
15:00 UTC, 18:00 UTC and ten minutes before clears `docs/decision_timing.md`'s test: chosen on the first two
seasons, +30.05 units on 174 bets in 2025-26 alone (p 0.018) against +15.68 for the live check. Over the three
seasons: 1,128 bets, +89.60 units against 760 bets, +58.03. The first-half total's best schedule (15:00 UTC,
18:00 UTC, an hour before) made +178.02 units against +125.91 over three seasons, but on 2025-26 alone p 0.125,
so it does not clear. Its live hour-before check is its best single time. For the spread, the edge over the
live check is clear only on 2025-26 at the live cut (+14.37 units, p 0.028). At cut 0.02 it is +8.29 (p 0.21).
From January on its early bets lost 4.57 units, and its 15:00 UTC prices are not yet checked against the live feed. Earlier bets do beat the close more
often. On the same pick, though, the early number is no better (spread -0.017 points, total +0.045). The
extra units are bets the later check no longer makes, because the market moved to the model's side first.
Full tables: `docs/decision_timing.md`, "The NCAAB first-half models".

**Changed.** Docs only: that section, and a pointer in each model's timing paragraph in `docs/sports/ncaab.md`.
No live timing changed: the rule is that mike sees a timing result first.

**How.** Read-only. The local copy of Supabase (taken 2026-11-24 05:30 UTC) and the two build scripts' own fit,
replay and grade functions, driven by scripts in `data/local/research/ncaab_fh_timing/` on mike's PC. No paid
call, no database write. The total's replay leaves out its big-favourite under rule at every time alike.

**Not done.** The spread change itself (card, plus an 18:00 UTC call in the capture), pending mike's yes. The six
live capture times with no history (180, 120, 50, 40, 30, 20 minutes) cannot be replayed without a purchase
(1,130,160-credit ceiling; balance 1,068,948 at 13:21 UTC).

## 2026-11-24 — AI assistant connector: a saved preferences profile, and which assistants support elicitation (Matt)

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-23: he and Mike both asked their assistants for 3 prop picks and got the same three — "How can we update the MCP to ask the user questions to narrow down more what they want." Then: "Do both of these" (elicitation, and a saved preferences profile). Answers to the clarifying questions: elicitation **measure first**; profile holds **sports and teams, price and risk, stats and books, research style**; profile calls are **free** (not one of the 10 questions).

**Measured.** `mcp_meter.calls` for 2026-11-23: two members, one through Grok (57 calls, 16:43–16:45 ET), one through Claude (55 calls, 17:29–18:37 ET). Both ran the same chain — `get_player_leaders` by L10 hit rate, `get_prop_board`, `get_best_price` — on a 1-game NFL slate (`get_slate` NFL returned 1 row to both). The tools rank deterministically, so the same question returns the same list on any model. The replies are not logged, so whether the three picks matched is not confirmed from data. Both assistants built picks although the instructions say the connector gives none. Elicitation support from web sources only (community trackers, not vendor docs): Claude.ai hosted connectors no (open request anthropics/claude-ai-mcp#153), ChatGPT yes, Grok nothing found. The connector is stateless JSON with no server-to-client channel, so it could not elicit today regardless.

**Changed.**
- `data/migrations/mcp_preferences_2026_11_24.sql` (new, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` after `mcp_stats_context`): `mcp_meter.preference_saves` (append-only; the newest row is the profile; a save merges, a null clears a key), `mcp_meter.client_capabilities` (one row per member / client / protocol / client name+version / capabilities, with `elicitation` and `seen_count`), `mcp_meter.record_client` and `mcp_meter.profile_call`. `profile_call` applies `begin_call`'s refusals (disconnected session, not entitled, suspended, terms, 3-assistant cap) but logs `outcome = 'profile'`, which no limit in `begin_call` counts. Own caps: 20 saves / 100 reads a day (`limits.profile_saves_per_day` / `profile_reads_per_day`); founders unlimited. Marker `mcp_preferences v1`.
- `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`: tools `get_my_preferences` and `save_my_preferences` (routed to `profile_call` before the question gate); an optional `books` filter on `get_best_price`, `get_prop_board` and `get_game_lines` (the seven books `mcp_stats` carries, measured); `initialize` records the client's advertised capabilities; the instructions now say: read the profile before any picks/shortlist request, ask the member the questions in chat when there is none and save the answers, ask what is specific to today, apply the profile (leagues, stats, books, price range, sides, angles), say which preferences shaped the answer, and present research, not recommendations.

**Verified.** `tests/test_mcp_preferences.py`: 13 passed on a scratch cluster (run as the `postgres` OS user; `initdb` refuses root). With profile rows logged as `'ok'` instead of `'profile'`, 2 failed (`test_profile_calls_count_no_question`, `test_saves_have_their_own_daily_cap`), then passed again once restored. `deno check` on the connector (esm.sh import swapped for `npm:` because esm.sh is blocked here): the same 16 type errors as `origin/master`, none new.

**Not done.** Not deployed: the edge function needs `supabase functions deploy mcp --no-verify-jwt`, and `web/content/terms.md` §19 says "The connector is read-only … it cannot change anything", which a saved profile contradicts — a terms decision for Matt first. The migration applies itself on the worker's next pass after merge and is harmless without the function. **Changed.** `web/templates/consent.html` gains `#code-resend` ("Resend code") between Sign in and
"Use a different email". `web/src/consent-boot.js`: the /otp call is shared by Send code and
Resend (`requestCode`); entering the code step and each resend start a 60-second wait shown on the
button ("Resend code in 45s"), because Supabase answers 429 inside its per-email window. A
successful resend clears the code box and says a new code went out; a 429 says one went out under
a minute ago; any other failure says to try again. "Use a different email" stops the timer.
`web/static/site.css`: disabled `.consent-link` style. Test:
`tests/test_consent_page_states.py::test_code_step_offers_resend_with_cooldown`.

intercepted (200, 200, 429): button disabled with countdown after send, enabled at 61s, resend
made the second /otp call and showed the new-code note, the 429 resend showed the wait note.
Consent tests 4 passed; `test_mcp_foundation.py`'s 19 errors are initdb refusing to run as root
in the sandbox, the same on unchanged master.

**Not done.** The 60s matches the 429s seen earlier today but the project's configured Supabase
email window was not read (not visible through read-only SQL). The mobile `SignInScreen.tsx` was
not changed.

## 2026-11-23 — AI assistant consent page: one step at a time, a resent code is not an error, Try again (Matt)

**Asked.** Matt (screenshot of signalbase-ai.com/oauth/consent while connecting Grok): "Issue
connecting. It won't send me the link", and then that the page "is confusing because the sign in
doesn't work". Later: yes to a Try again button on the error screen.

**Found (Supabase auth logs).** 00:39:28Z one `/otp` 200 (the code was sent), then nine `/otp`
429 `over_email_send_rate_limit` by 00:40:00Z. `.consent-form { display: flex }` overrode the
`hidden` attribute `show()` toggles, so the email step, the code step and the error box rendered
together: the page never visibly advanced, the member tapped again, and each 429 was shown as
"Something went wrong". After sign-in, the consent block also appeared below the still-visible
forms, which read as "sign in doesn't work". After the fix deployed (00:46:52Z), Matt's Grok
connection completed: `/otp` 00:47:00Z, `/verify` 00:47:03Z, consent `approve` and `/oauth/token`
00:48:07Z, all 200.

**What changed.**
- PR #1591 (merged): `.consent-card [hidden] { display: none !important; }`; Send code is
  single-flight (disabled, "Sending..."); a 429 goes to the code step with a "code already sent"
  note instead of the error box.
- This PR: a Try again button on the error step. It re-runs `loadRequest()` (email step when
  signed out, the request again when signed in) and re-enables Allow/Cancel; hidden only when the
  link has no valid `authorization_id`, where trying again cannot help.
- `tests/test_consent_page_states.py`: 3 tests; the first 2 fail on the pre-#1591 code.

**Verified.** Headless Chromium against the built page with Supabase calls stubbed: pre-fix shows
`state-email,state-code,state-error` after four taps with four sends; fixed shows one step at a
time, four taps send once, a 429 resend stays on the code step. Live signalbase-ai.com after
#1591: email -> code -> consent, one step each. Try again: wrong email -> error -> Try again ->
email step; failed Allow -> Try again -> consent with Allow enabled, second Allow sends; broken
link -> no Try again. `tests/test_mcp_foundation.py`: same 19 errors as master (initdb cannot run
as root in the sandbox).

**Not done.** No hint beside the disabled Allow button (Matt: not needed, ticking the terms box
enables Allow).

## 2026-11-22 — The NHL best bet a night, built as four rule cards (mike)

**What.** mike: "Yes" to building the best bet a night on the puck line,
moneyline, regulation 3-way and total, after both goalies are confirmed, one bet
a night, flat stake, skipping a game that already holds an NHL bet.
`models/nhl_best_bet_night.py` (the rule: Pinnacle's power-de-vigged price vs
the bettable books in the same fetch, EV >= 0.01, price -200 or better, a game's
best across the four markets, the night's largest on the first pass that has
one), `scripts/nhl_best_bet_night_card.py` (the card: goalie gate, price after
confirmation, one bet a night under the family's advisory lock, any game with
an NHL BET skipped). Four ids registered in `config.py` (cut 0/0, EV floor
0.01, price floor -200, own probability, rule), settlement
(`tracking/paper_tracker._RULE_MODEL_MARKETS`), CLV (`clv_game_market`,
marker `...-pregame-nhl-night`), line movement (`models/scorer.check_line_movement`),
the app (`mobile/src/lib/markets.ts`, `modelMeta.ts`, regenerated thresholds
and dashboard names). Runs as `nhl-best-bet-night` in `scripts/refresh_pass.sh`
after the totals card and before scoring, and in the goalie window after the
totals card (`scheduler.py`, 120 s, inside the window's lock).

**No game carries two bets that cannot both win.** The side-conflict check
read only models in `config.MODELS`, so a rule card's bet was invisible to the
side models deciding after it, and the totals card read only its own bets. The
check now works over final scores (home goals, away goals, after regulation),
which adds the puck line and the total to the moneyline and 3-way, reads the
rule cards' standing BETs (`models/scorer._rule_card_markets`), and the totals
card refuses a total that cannot win with one standing
(`conflicting_standing_bet`). `nhl_puckline` joined the held-back side models;
it has written no BET since 2026-08-01 (SELECT on `picks`), so no past pick is
affected. A same-side second total is still allowed (it can win with the first).

**Verified.** Replayed the live code over 2021-22 to 2025-26
(`scripts/nhl_best_bet_night_replay.py`): 323 bets, +66.5u, +20.0%
(+6.9..+33.1), CLV +2.13%, every season positive; 319 of the research's 323
nights the same bet. Two replay readings on the way were wrong and are kept
in the doc: +31.9u on 278 (cut at the stored start less 10 minutes, but the
stored start is the scheduled time for many games), then +62.6u on 349 (goalie
timing checked per game, which let a 3-way fetched before the goalie news
through; the card now checks each market's own fetch). `docs/nhl_sharp_vs_soft.md`,
"The live model". Live dry run at 09:41 ET (reads only): 5 games held for
goalies; with the hold lifted in memory the price query ran and found 111
comparisons, none at 1%. Two bugs found before any merge: the pipeline's step
list lacked `nhl-best-bet-night`, and the price query named two columns
`created_at` (it failed on the database). New tests:
`tests/test_nhl_best_bet_night.py` (30), conflict cases in
`tests/test_nhl_side_conflicts.py` (each fails with the fix removed), the
totals card refusal in `tests/test_nhl_totals_card.py`.

**Not done.** Not merged: waiting for mike's merge. First live decision and
first bet on every surface not yet watched (needs the merge and a night with
confirmed goalies).

## 2026-11-22 — NBA: the low-volume plan (four models, 20-game wait, ten game totals a week)

**Asked.** mike: the fewest NBA bets for the most units, 6-10 a week, a 100-unit bankroll; "yes for
now" to the plan.

**Measured** (stake-tiers study, `RESULT.md`): stake tiers by any signal tested do not beat a flat
stake; the models' own top 6-10 a week return -0.6% to +2.3%, worse than random; the three game-total models
(full-game and first-half overs, home team-total under) at 20+ games, ten a week, +17.6% on 504 backtest bets, the full-game model's unseen seasons +5.0% on 205;
the prop card at one to three a night +6.7% to +9.9%, no better than six a night.

**What changed.** `models/nba_total_luck.LIVE_MIN_GAMES` 10 -> 20 (all three game-total models).
`scripts/nba_game_totals_door.WeeklyCap`: ten BETs a week, Monday to Sunday by game date, across the
three, first come; a capped bet keeps its decision row with reason `weekly_cap:...`.
`config.PAUSED_MODELS` += nba_prop_projection, nba_prop_market, nba_prop_first_basket,
nba_live_spread_q12, nba_q1_trend_ml (Updated-By: mike). `tests/test_nba_low_volume_plan.py`.

**Not changed.** Stakes: every pick publishes at 1u to win since 2026-11-21 (Matt; the record sheet),
so the plan's 2.5u is applied by how much is bet per published unit, not by the app.

- Deployed the `mcp` edge function from master (version 7, `verify_jwt`

  `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`, runs while `assistant_of v2` is missing):

  (CONFIG only as a fallback), so the site and the database cannot drift
  while a migration waits for a worker pass. `web/build.py` fallback is
  `2026-11-22`. Terms section 19 also says a disconnected assistant can be
  approved again (review L3).

**Measured before the bump.** One member had accepted the terms (Matt), so
the re-acceptance is his alone: his next question asks him to reconnect and
accept once.

**Verified.** `test_mcp_foundation.py` 22 pass; the 2 new tests fail with the

## 2026-11-22 — The odds clean-up keeps NHL's whole price path

**Asked.** mike, 2026-11-21: "Yes, keep it", to "Should I make the odds
clean-up keep NHL's full price history from now on?"

**Why.** A settled NHL game kept only each non-DraftKings book's opener and
close in `odds`, Pinnacle included: `data/prune_odds.py` protects Pinnacle in
`player_prop_odds` alone. Measured in production 2026-11-21: the 12 games of
2026-11-07 held 2,061 non-DraftKings rows (352 Pinnacle), against 74,899
(11,125 Pinnacle) on the 12 games of 2026-11-19, not yet cleaned. So 2026-27
could not be replayed for the rules that price a bettable book against
Pinnacle in the same fetch (`docs/nhl_sharp_vs_soft.md`, PR #1573).

**Changed.** NHL joins `PATH_KEPT_SPORTS` beside NBA, NCAAB, NCAABW, NCAAF,
EuroLeague and the NBL. The two delete tiers skip NHL rows, and `thin_repeats`
removes only the middle of a run where a book's price did not change, so every
as-of read returns what it did before. The games already thinned cannot be put
back.

**Cost, measured** on the local copy's 2026-11-19 NHL games: 73,572
non-DraftKings rows, of which the price-change rule keeps 47,379 (3,948 a game,
in-play rows included), against about 172 a game kept today. On 1,312
regular-season games that is about 5.0M more rows a season; at `odds`' 21 GB
over 51.5M rows (indexes included, `docs/data_archive.md`), about 2 GB. One
day's sample.

**Verified.** `tests/test_prune_keeps_nba_path.py`: the new NHL test and the
pinned list failed before the change (NHL rows were left to the ordinary
clean-up, and the thinning did not touch them) and pass after it; 245 tests
across the prune files and the files that read the list pass. In the other
17 files that import `data.prune_odds`, 364 pass and 4 in
`tests/test_euroleague_period_odds.py` fail, the same 4 on master without this
change.

**Not urgent, measured 2026-11-22.** The odds clean-up has deleted nothing
since 2026-11-14: `config.DATA_DELETION_APPROVALS` is empty (`041bd565`), and
the 11:26 UTC daily run logged "odds prune off ... nothing was read or
deleted". Pinnacle rows a game in production: 29 to 48 for every NHL date to
2026-11-13, 520 to 949 from 2026-11-14. So this change governs what happens
when the clean-up is switched back on; the dates to 11-13 cannot be put back.

## 2026-11-22 — The PC backup fetches master before each run (#1542, #1558)

**Asked.** mike: *"Yes, fix it here"*, to "Should I make the nightly backup fetch master before each run?"

**Found.** Each backup pass refreshes its four scripts from `origin/master` in the main checkout. That ref is only as new as the checkout's last fetch, and nothing fetched it on a schedule. The checkout's reflog showed no fetch from 20:50 ET on 11-20 to 02:03 ET on 11-21, across the 00:30 run. A fix merged in such a gap would have missed that night's backup.

**Changed.**
- `scripts/local_mirror_run.ps1` fetches `origin master` first, with no sign-in prompt allowed (git's and Git Credential Manager's), a 120 s limit, and the ref it already has on any failure. `logs\fetch_<stamp>.log` names the commit the refresh read. `-RefreshOnly` runs that step alone.
- The wrapper itself is not refreshed from master. The new one was copied into `bin` by hand on 11-21. The old one is kept beside it as `local_mirror_run.ps1.before_fetch_20261121`.
- The catch-up step printed "it has no report of its own" about the pass before it without looking. It now says the row is written unless one already records it.

**Verified.**
- Four new tests run the real wrapper against a clone one commit behind its upstream. On master's wrapper the fetch tests fail (the refreshed script stays at version 1). Here they pass, including a fetch that fails and one that hangs (stopped at 3 s).
- Run for real on the PC with `-RefreshOnly`, windowless through `run_hidden.pyw`: exit 0 in 6 s, read master `27356f88`.
- The 00:30 ET run on 11-22 used it. Its fetch log reads master `a529eb2f` (#1575, merged 23:14 ET). The copy ran 70 minutes: 340 tables, 0 failed, 0 differing. Row 16 in `pc_copy_snapshots`; Retool lists it once with "Tables that differ: None". This closed #1541.
- The catch-up wording check fails on the old text and passes on the new.

**Not changed.** No schedule, table or copy logic.

## 2026-11-22 — First-basket and NCAAF picks were not grading on game day

**Asked.** Grade the stuck 2026-11-21 bets: NBA `player_first_basket` never settled, and all 17 NCAAF BETs were still ungraded at 10:42pm ET.

**Measured.**
- The settler already handles `nba_prop_first_basket` (`tracking/paper_tracker.py`, `models/nba_first_basket.py`). It reads `nba_pbp`. `nba_pbp_games` stopped at 2026-11-20. The only writer is the 6am `nba-quarters` step, which runs after that morning's settle and is not in the refresh pass. Season 2027 had 5 finals without play-by-play, all dated 2026-11-21. No earlier `player_first_basket` BET exists.
- LeBron James (NBA id 2544) is on Philadelphia in `nba_player_game_log` for `NBA_2026-11-21_MIL_PHI`. ESPN event 401910023 is that game. His dunk is the first score and he started. The other four named bets are on the games in the pick rows.
- NCAAF finals are written by `ingest_ncaaf_results_for_date`, called only from the 6am pipeline. `scripts/refresh_pass.sh` runs NBA, NCAAB, NHL, NFL and UFC results before settle, and not NCAAF. Scores for 2026-11-14 landed 2026-11-15 11:02 UTC and for 2026-11-20 at 2026-11-21 11:02 UTC. Every NCAAF BET date from 2026-11-04 through 2026-11-20 was already graded. The live CFBD scoreboard never marks a game final (`state_name` stays `in_progress`), so it never fills `games.home_score`.

**Changed.** `settle_picks` loads play-by-play for final games that still have an ungraded first-basket BET (`nba_pbp.ensure_pending_first_basket`, `fill_gaps(..., only_game_ids=)`). The refresh pass runs `ncaaf-results` in the results group, before settle.

**Backfill, then the 11:03pm ET settle (the worker's own grader).** Four first-basket bets and 15 NCAAF bets graded at `COALESCE(decision_odds, dk_odds)`. Play-by-play for the four finished NBA games was stored through the same `build_gap_game` path (ESPN summary, source `espn_summary_api`). ESPN finals were written onto 47 NCAAF games whose `home_score` was still null. Paul George (BOS at SAC) and Utah State at Oregon State and San Diego State at Boise State were not final at 11:03pm ET, so those three bets stayed ungraded.

**Not changed.** No model, threshold, stake or pause. `profit_flat` is the settler's per-100 figure. Published units stay 1 unit to win.

## 2026-11-22 — NHL goalie window: a tick as each refresh pass ends, and from 9:00 ET (#1568)

**Asked.** mike: *"Yes, fix #1568"*, after the session that found NHL game
bets landing in the last hour because the goalies are confirmed late, and the
window reading those confirmations late.

**Found** (ESPN rows in `nhl_goalie_designations`, 2026-10-24 to 11-21; gap =
the read that first saw a new starter or status, minus the last read that saw
the old one; worker logs and `pipeline_runs` for 2026-11-21):
- **Evening, 18:00-23:59 ET: median 10.0 minutes, 90th percentile 10.2 (66
  changes).** The evening pass starts every ten minutes and ran 5.7-6.7
  minutes that night (`pipeline_runs` and the log agree). Every tick at :00
  and :05 logged "a refresh pass or the daily run is in progress" and did
  nothing. So ESPN was read once every ten minutes, by each pass, a median of
  14 seconds after it started (60 evening first reads, 3-32 seconds).
- **Daytime, 11:00-17:59 ET:** 209 changes read within 6 minutes. 70 were read
  13 minutes late, all after an hourly pass at :17 (the :20 and :25 ticks
  stood down; next read :30). 33 waited 39-44 minutes: the 10:17 pass, then
  the window's first tick at 11:00. 31 waited about 60 minutes, all for the
  next day's games, which only the hourly passes read (nothing decides a game
  before its game day; left alone).
- **Morning.** 25 starters were first read as confirmed at the 11:00 tick and
  3 at the 10:17 pass. None was confirmed at the 9:17 pass.
- **The scheduler's pool is full at the top of the hour.** 10 of its 10
  threads were busy at 00:00:00 UTC 2026-11-22 (worker log, 23:50-00:19 UTC,
  jobs counted between "Running job" and their end). That ruled out the first
  version of this fix, a tick that waited up to 280 seconds for the pass in
  the pool. A deciding tick took 65 seconds (20:55:00-20:56:05 UTC, 7 games);
  a poll-only tick about 7.
- Nightly evening-pass medians over 14 days: 7.8-9.6 minutes (5.1-5.8 on the
  short nights of 11-07, 11-14 and 11-21).

**Changed.**
- `scheduler._nhl_window_after_pass`: a refresh pass, and the daily run, start
  one goalie tick the moment they let go of their lock. Inside the window's
  hours only, on a short thread outside the pool, and not while a deploy is
  draining the process. Only the process that runs the window job does it
  (`main()` sets `_NHL_AFTER_PASS_TICKS`); tests leave it off, because a tick
  polls ESPN and writes picks. The scheduled tick still does nothing during a
  pass, as before.
- The window runs 9:00-23:55 ET (was 11:00), from one constant,
  `NHL_GOALIE_WINDOW_HOURS_ET`. The heartbeat's active hours move with it.
- Docstrings and `docs/sports/nhl.md`.

**Verified.**
- The 2026-11-21 evening (the log's pass starts and ends), replayed through the
  real `run_refresh_pass` and `run_nhl_goalie_window` with time compressed.
  Before: 6 reads 10.0 minutes apart, which matches production. After: 12
  reads, median 5.0 minutes apart, at most 6.6.
- New tests: a pass (refresh and daily) starts a tick as it ends, on its own
  thread, never while the pass's lock is held. None off the worker, outside
  the hours or during a drain. The 9:00 start, with the overnight silence
  bound and the heartbeat hours checked against the trigger. Three mutations
  each failed the new test: no tick after a refresh pass, no tick after the
  daily run, the tick started before the pass let go.
- `tests/test_nhl_goalie_guards.py`: 77 passed. Full suite: an error-set diff
  against an `origin/master` worktree, in the PR.

**Not done.** The worker has not run it yet. On the first game evening after
deploy (#1568), evening ESPN reads should come at most about one pass apart
(the pass length that night), and morning confirmations should first be read
before 11:00. A pass that starts while a deciding tick runs now waits up to
about a minute for it.

## 2026-11-22 — Every table is read-only for Claude (Supabase MCP)

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-22: "Tables can be read only but all should be protected." This widens 2026-11-21's picks-only rule to the whole database.

**Changed.** `.claude/hooks/picks_read_only_guard.py` now covers every table, in two layers. (1) Any statement that writes, changes schema, changes permissions or controls the transaction (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE/TRUNCATE/ALTER/DROP/CREATE/GRANT/COPY/REFRESH/DO/CALL/SET/BEGIN/COMMIT/SELECT INTO and similar) is refused before it is sent. Keywords inside string literals, quoted identifiers and comments are ignored. (2) Every query that passes is rewritten to `BEGIN READ ONLY; … ROLLBACK;`, so Postgres itself refuses a write the keyword list missed, such as a function with side effects. `apply_migration` is refused outright. CLAUDE.md §1b and the session-start reminder now say the MCP is read-only; until now they said it writes production.

**Verified.** 37 guard tests pass, and 24 fail with the check disabled. Live through the MCP: `SELECT current_setting('transaction_read_only')` returned `on`, so the rewrite reaches the server; `UPDATE worker_jobs … WHERE false` was blocked by the hook.

**Consequence.** Claude can no longer queue worker jobs by inserting into `worker_jobs` from a chat. The `jobs/declared_jobs.json` route through a reviewed PR still works. The Python `DATABASE_URL` route is not hooked: the rule forbids it, and nothing enforces it.

**Open.** Matt also asked that "a user cannot find out how we use the model data, just the model data is there for them to query". Who "a user" is and what counts as method has not been settled yet; see the session reply.

## 2026-11-21 — AI assistant connector: tonight's NCAAF games and their lines

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-21, with a screenshot of a member's assistant picking SDSU @ Boise State Over 61.5 from public sources: "We need the research to be better." The assistant had said Signal Base listed tonight's games but returned "not found" for the lines on every night game.

**Measured.** `mcp_meter.calls` for that conversation (15:53 ET): `get_slate` NCAAF today returned 20 rows, the cap, all kicked off by 13:05 ET and four of them the previous night's finals; `get_slate` tomorrow returned 6 rows, tonight's late games again; `get_game_lines` returned `not_found` for all six. Cause, two defects:
- `public.games` holds NCAAF twins: the CFBD ingestor used to date a game by its UTC kickoff (`NCAAF_2026-11-22_san-diego-state_boise-state`), the odds feed by its ET date (`NCAAF_2026-11-21_...`). `retain_existing_cfbd_ids` keeps the old id on purpose (FKs from `ncaaf_team_game_log` / `ncaaf_qb_game`). Prices are stored under the ET id only. 702 pairs, keyed on (home, away, ET kickoff date); none with three rows; at most 115 minutes apart, because the live feed rewrites `commence_time` to the real kickoff (Boise 02:30 -> 02:40 UTC). No other sport has twins.
- `get_slate` used the generic 20-row cap; the NCAAF Saturday is 59 games.

**Changed.**
- `mcp_stats_games_twins_2026_11_21.sql` (new, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`, applied to production): `mcp_stats.games` keeps one row per NCAAF game (the ET-dated, non-CFBD row), dates it by the ET kickoff, fills a missing score from the twin, and appends `alias_ids` (every id of the game). Other sports pass straight through and keep the `game_date` index. Guard: the `mcp_stats.games twins v2` comment marker. v1 (keyed on the kickoff instant) ran for about an hour and re-split six games once the feed moved their kickoffs; v2 replaced it. `public.games` is untouched. `mcp_stats_views_2026_11_21.sql` carries the same definition.
- Edge function `mcp` v6: `get_slate` returns up to 200 games with `has_lines` per game and `truncated` if cut; `get_game_lines` reads prices under every `alias_ids` id.

**Verified.** As `mcp_reader`: NCAAF 2026-11-21 = 59 games, 0 duplicate matchups, 58 with prices (New Mexico @ Wyoming has none stored); "tomorrow" no longer repeats tonight's games. Through the live connector: `get_slate` NCAAF listed all 59 with `has_lines`; `get_game_lines` for SDSU @ Boise State returned total 61.5 (Over -107 Caesars, Under -110 FanDuel), Boise -20.5, ML -1400/+900. Timing on production: NCAAF slate 634 ms cold, lines 134 ms. `tests/test_mcp_games_twins.py` (8): all fail on origin/master, pass here. Full suite: 58 failures and 263 errors, both sets identical to origin/master run in a worktree (the errors are scratch-Postgres fixtures; `initdb` refuses root here).

**Not done.** NCAAF games that kicked off at 17:00 UTC still read `final: false` with no score at 02:30 UTC: observed on the slate, cause not investigated. The connector has no team-results tool (a game's recent scores, totals), which is why the assistant went to the web for Boise's last three totals.

## 2026-11-21 — The model picks are read-only for Claude (Supabase MCP)

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-21: "never touch the actual model picks, but use that data" — query the picks for analysis from prompts, never write them.

**Changed.** New rule in CLAUDE.md §1b. New PreToolUse hook `.claude/hooks/picks_read_only_guard.py`, wired in `.claude/settings.json` on every Supabase `execute_sql` / `apply_migration` call. It blocks INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, MERGE, TRUNCATE, COPY, ALTER, DROP and CREATE TRIGGER/RULE on `picks*` (picks, picks_log, …) and `results_snapshots`. SELECTs pass. The pipeline writes picks through `DATABASE_URL`, not the MCP, so production is unaffected. To fit the always-loaded size cap (master had 16 bytes of headroom), three long §9 map labels were shortened; no row was removed.

**Verified.** `tests/test_picks_read_only_guard.py`: 23 passed; with the regex disabled, 14 failed. Live: `UPDATE picks SET result = result WHERE false` through the MCP was blocked by the hook, and a SELECT on picks returned rows.

**Not changed.** Python's `data.db.get_connection()` route is not hooked. The rule covers it, and nothing enforces it. Other tables (`model_action_thresholds`, `picks` views) are not protected.

## 2026-11-21 — NHL game picks land late: why, and whether earlier would help

**Asked.** mike: *"NHL game picks are being picked too close to game time and
we are not getting any clv and we are getting crushed. why is this, why arent
we picking earlier"*.

**Found** (production, read 2026-11-22 ~00:30 UTC; the goalie history in
`nhl_goalie_designations` starts 2026-10-24 00:50 UTC, so the clean window is
games 2026-10-24 to 2026-11-20):
- **The late moneyline bets are the goalie rule (mike, 2026-10-20).** Of 191
  games, ESPN first read both starters as confirmed inside the last hour on 67
  (15 of 19 matinees), and under 10 minutes before the listed start on 36,
  which no gated model can bet. 9 never had both confirmed. 75 were known 6+
  hours out. Since the rule, 23 of 57 `nhl_moneyline` bets (tonight's
  included) came in the last hour; 18 of those 23 fired within 15 minutes of
  the confirmation (median 6 minutes), none before it.
- **The rest fire on a late soft-book price, not a late goalie.** 4 last-hour
  moneyline bets (3 BetRivers, 1 Hard Rock) and 7 of 10 sub-3-hour
  `nhl_over_under` bets came 2+ hours after both goalies were confirmed. Example
  from `picks_log`: "LAK ML" 2026-11-21, model 0.395 all day, DraftKings +154
  from 18:23Z; BET at 23:44Z only when BetRivers went to +170.
- **Picking earlier does not buy closing-line value for the moneyline.**
  Against Pinnacle's no-vig close (`market_clv_pct`, every row `power`, every
  close captured after the bet): last hour -1.55% (SE 0.48, n 21), earlier
  -2.13% (SE 0.65, n 33); before the goalie history, bets placed 6+ hours out
  were -1.76% (13). Same-book CLV near zero in the last hour (+0.45%) is
  mechanical: the book has no time left to move. The six-season replay
  (`docs/nhl_moneyline_rebuild.md`, 11-19) has the last fetch at or above ~8 h.
- **Units (`v_model_health_bets`, games from 2026-10-07):** `nhl_moneyline`
  34-34 +0.05u; `nhl_moneyline_regulation` 7-12 -0.13u; `nhl_over_under` 14-9
  +5.44u. Under the rule, last-hour moneyline 9-12 -3.16u (21) against earlier
  19-14 +5.89u (33): 1.15 standard errors apart.
- **The goalie poll misses reads.** Of 424 ESPN status changes read 11:00-23:59
  ET, 148 were first read more than 11 minutes after the previous read (median
  5, 90th percentile 43; the window is meant to tick every 5). Of the 36 games
  first read as confirmed after the 10-minute cutoff, 11 had their last
  unconfirmed read before it (#1568).

**Not done.** No change to the goalie rule, timing, thresholds or the poll.
Reads only; scratch scripts in the session scratchpad.

## 2026-11-21 — Betting PC monitor on the phone

**Asked.** mike: *"can u create an app for my phone too"*.

**Changed.**
- **Shape.** A phone cannot reach the PC. So the PC writes the page's view to one private row, `pc_monitor_state`, and the worker's monitor serves the same page at `/pc/` from that row. It is a home-screen web app, not a store app.
- **The row** (`data/migrations/pc_monitor_state_2026_11_21.sql`, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`): RLS on; anon, authenticated and backroom_reader revoked.
  - It was applied by hand through `data.db`, because the Supabase connector answered "read-only transaction".
  - Readers afterwards: postgres, service_role, supabase_admin, and two internal Supabase read roles.
  - The security advisor gives only the usual "RLS on, no policies" note.
- **Write rate.** The PC writes every 2 s while a phone has asked within 60 s, and once a minute otherwise (12,758 bytes a write).
- **The link's token** is made once by the PC and kept in the row. It opens `/pc/` and nothing else; `MONITOR_TOKEN` still opens everything.
- **The PC window** shows the link as a QR code drawn by `pc_monitor/qr.py`. Installing a QR package was refused, and the link must not go to an online QR service.
- **The phone page** greys out after 90 s without a report, fits a phone screen, and saves to the home screen.
- **Deploys.** `railway.json` now watches `pc_monitor/**`, so a change to the page alone redeploys the worker.

**Verified.**
- **QR code.** It matched `qrcode-terminal` (already under `mobile/node_modules`), every module of every mask, in 26 cases across versions 1-6 and levels L and M. A wrong error-correction table entry fails it.
- **The PC writing the row.** The first real run found every publish raising IndexError: RETURNING followed a line break and was not seen. Fixed, with a test on the real statements that fails on the old check.
- **The cloud copy**, run locally against the real database:
  - The first read was 43 s old (the once-a-minute pace).
  - The phone's stamp switched the PC to 2 s, and the next read was 2.9 s old.
  - Leagues, live games, rows per hour and task states equal the PC's own view.
- **Phone width (375 px).** No sideways scroll on the page; wide tables scroll inside their card.
- **PC off.** With the PC app stopped for 100 s, the page showed "The PC last reported 2 min ago", with every pill grey.
- **Tests.** The phone token on `/`, `/api/snapshot`, `/api/stream` and `/index.html` gets 401, and that test fails when the main check accepts the phone token. The token never reaches the written view; that test fails when the view carries it.

**Not yet seen.** The page served by the deployed worker, and on mike's own phone.

**Then the stream** (mike, relayed: *"show the stream of api calls and information in a section that
basically just shows all the api calls and data movement? like a visualization as well?"*).
- **Sources.** Calls come from `api_call_log` (every outgoing call, worker and PC; read by primary key every second). This PC's saved rows come from `dk_live_reads` (by id, every 2 s). Backup and tennis moments come from their files.
- **What it draws:**
  - a flow diagram with one moving dot per call;
  - calls every 10 s for 10 minutes, stacked by provider in fixed colours (the dataviz reference palette's first six slots, validated on the page's surfaces);
  - a per-provider table;
  - the filterable stream.
- **Seen on live data at 18:38 ET.**
  - 46 calls a minute from this PC and 92 from the worker.
  - 84 rows saved by this PC in the last minute.
  - Odds API credits left: 1,210,486.
  - 150 events in the stream, and "Data moved" filtered to 65 saved rows.
  - The phone copy carries the newest 60 events: 35,949 bytes a write, no token.
- **Two fixes from looking at it.** Edge labels overlapped where the lines converge, so they now sit at the provider end. The note said "last minute" while the code uses a 10-minute average; the note now says that.
## 2026-11-21 — Performance tab: bet lists grouped by date, with folding and See all

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-21: "make the bet list collapsible by bet dates and A see all option". He also confirmed the Insights card's two open calls: My stakes always, and the card stays above the bet list.

**Changed.**
- `lib/betDateGroups.ts` is new and pure. It groups bets by date, newest first.
  - The two newest days start open.
  - The newest seven days show until "See all", which replaces the tracked list's 40-row cap.
  - A day holding an open bet always shows and starts open.
  - A tap on a header always wins.
- `components/BetDateHeader.tsx` is new. It shows the day (`dayLabelET`, with the year on another year's day; "No date" when there is none), the bet count, W–L–P, open, no action, and the day's units.
- `DateGroupedList` in `PerformanceScreen` is used by both "Your bets" (the ET day placed) and "Tracked picks" (the game date). The visible per-row date is dropped, because the header carries it. VoiceOver still reads it, since a header can be folded away.

**UX review** (`frontend-ux-designer`): ship with fixes, and all of them were applied:
- open bets can no longer be folded away or hidden behind See all;
- an undated header reads "No date";
- no-action picks are counted in the header;
- the See-all spoken label matches its text;
- the year shows on old days;
- See all is one-way.

**Verified.** `mobile/scripts/verify_bet_date_groups.ts`, run by `tests/test_mobile_performance_insights.py`. I watched it fail twice: with the date order reversed, and with the open-bet rule removed. No tsc errors in touched files. `ux_scan --changed` reports 0 findings.

Maybe most successful bets, etc." Seven proposals approved: all seven, weeks Monday–Sunday, every group shown with its count, scored at My stakes, logged bets folded in, streaks in (plain).

## 2026-11-21 — The PC's scheduled tasks start without a window

**Asked.** mike: *"why are there command prompts running on my computer? ... is that from back end
processes?"*, then *"yes, switch them"*.

**Measured.**

- The windows came from the three `BettingModel` tasks in Task Scheduler. Each was registered as
  `powershell.exe -WindowStyle Hidden`. On this PC console programs open in Windows Terminal, which
  ignores that flag.
- The DraftKings feed's window (Windows Terminal, titled with the powershell.exe path) was created
  at 11:01:00, the same second Task Scheduler started the feed. Its terminal was launched through
  DcomLaunch.
- The 11:22 tennis run opened a new window in the same second its task fired.
- Today's DraftKings feed starts, local time: 1:58, 2:44, 9:00, 9:29, 10:15 and 11:01. Each one is a
  window that stays open until the feed idles out (`--idle-exit`, 45 minutes).
- Every launch form was tested through throwaway scheduled tasks (all deleted after):
  - The old form opened a window.
  - `conhost.exe --headless` opened none, but reported exit 0 for a script that exits 7. That would
    have hidden the backup runner's failure code, which `local_mirror_run.ps1` exits with and the
    dashboard's task read stores as `last_result`.
  - `pyw.exe run_hidden.pyw` opened none and passed the 7 through.

**Changed.**

- New `scripts/run_hidden.pyw`, installed as `betting_local_copies\bin\run_hidden.pyw` (same hash).
- All three tasks now start as `C:\Windows\pyw.exe ...\bin\run_hidden.pyw powershell.exe <old
  arguments>`. Triggers and settings are unchanged; each was read back after the change.
- For about ten minutes before that, the tasks were on `conhost --headless`.
- The DraftKings task was changed while its feed was running, and the running feed was left alone:
  both processes stayed alive and its lock stayed held.
- mike then asked for a restart (11:42). The old window closed and no new one opened. The feed now
  runs python <- powershell <- pythonw <- pyw <- Task Scheduler. NCAAB reads paused about 14 s
  (11:42:03 to 11:42:17); after that, `dk_live_feed` showed NCAAB 2 s old with both live games seen.
  #1554 (the follow-up check) was closed.
- The 11:52 tennis run, the first on the launcher, opened no window. It stored 341 DraftKings rows,
  saw 643 Pinnacle prices, and Task Scheduler recorded result 0.
- The hand-started backup finished at 12:58: 338 tables, 0 failed, `pc_copy_snapshots` row 14.
- The DraftKings task's exact new command line was run as a throwaway task. It opened no window and
  stopped at the lock check with result 0.
- The backup was started by hand (11:27, still on `conhost`). No window opened, it wrote its logs,
  and `scripts/local_mirror_report.read_task` still parses the task.
- `docs/local_ops.md`: the backup's `/Create` line uses the launcher, and there is a new section,
  "Every scheduled task starts windowless". The DraftKings schedule there said 11am for 16 hours;
  the task is 9am for 18 hours.

**Side effect, recorded rather than fixed.** Ending a task in Task Scheduler now stops only the
launcher, and the runner and its child keep going. This was measured with a stand-in script. The
old form already left the child (the feed's Python) running. Each job's own lock still stops a
second copy.

**Not done.** The two tennis loops started by hand on 11-13 last wrote at 9:02 and 9:16am today and
were not restarted. Their `.cmd` launchers open windows, and the 30-minute task still collects
both books.
