## 2026-11-21 — Betting PC monitor: one window for the jobs running on mike's PC

**Asked.** mike: *"is there a way to create a desktop app / monitoring dash so I can see the data
throughput in real time for all these processes running locally?"*, then, asked whether it should
be a tab in the cloud monitor, *"I want something on my computer as I told you"*.

**Changed.** New package `pc_monitor/`, separate from `monitoring/` (the worker never imports it).
`pythonw -m pc_monitor` serves http://127.0.0.1:8790/ and opens it as an Edge app window. Three
collector threads: local reads every second (psutil, the feed's log and launcher log, the backup's
pass files, `progress.json`, `STATE.json`, the tennis logs), Task Scheduler every 30 s (one hidden
PowerShell call for the whole `\BettingModel\` folder), and the database on its own thread
(`dk_live_feed` every 2 s, `dk_live_reads` per minute every 15 s, `tennis_pinnacle_prices` and
`pc_copy_snapshots` every 60 s; both range reads are index-only scans, 14 ms and 3 ms). Desktop
shortcut "Betting PC monitor" made by `scripts/pc_monitor_shortcut.ps1`, running from its own copy
`.claude\feeds\pc-monitor`; each start moves that copy to origin/master once master contains the app.
`docs/local_ops.md` has the operator section, and its DkLiveBoard schedule paragraph was corrected to
what Task Scheduler holds (every minute from 09:00 for 18 h, run through `run_hidden.pyw`, lock
port 47232; it said 11:00 for 16 h and 47231).

**Verified.**
- Numbers read at one moment (14:05 ET) against the sources:
  - Task Scheduler: all three tasks' state, result code, last and next start match.
  - Processes: the feed (pid 16256) and its launcher (22140) match `Win32_Process`.
  - The feed's last log line matches (read 8101 at 14:05:04).
  - The rate is 0.98 reads a second, over 900 reads.
  - Live and matched games per league equal the SQL.
  - Hourly saved rows: NBA 258 = 258 and NCAABW 82 = 82. NCAAB 742 vs 748 and NCAAF 2,072 vs 2,086, because that count refreshes every 15 s.
  - Tennis: 403 = 403 Pinnacle prices in the last hour.
  - Backup: row 14 = 14.
- Launched from the `.lnk` itself it runs as `pythonw.exe -m pc_monitor`. Two window-watcher runs (80 s and 75 s, each covering two or more Task Scheduler reads) saw the Edge windows and no console window.
  - The first run also saw a File Explorer window, 47 s after launch. It did not recur on the second run, and nothing in the app opens Explorer.
  - A second click opened another window onto the running app and exited.
  - With no window open, a copy exits after its idle time (seen at 2 min).
- With `DATABASE_URL` pointed at a closed port, tasks, processes, logs, the backup and tennis all filled, and the database showed as unreachable.
- The connection lands on port 6543 (the transaction pooler), and each read leaves no open transaction.
- `tests/test_pc_monitor.py`: 41 tests on lines copied from today's logs. Each of these fails with its fix removed:
  - a tennis exit line with a blank code, whose time had been read as code 2026;
  - a UTF-16 byte-order mark left on the first line;
  - the read counter resetting on a restart;
  - the 8 s and 30 s constants drifting from the feed and the models;
  - a child process started without `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`.

**Found and fixed on the way.**
- **Old data judged as current.** After a database error, or a window reopened after the collectors paused, the last rows kept ageing in memory. Every league with a live game would have turned red although the feed was fine. Each panel now has a limit of a few read intervals; past it, it shows grey "not read for N s". Seen on a copy reopened after 75 s: all five leagues grey, "not read from the database for 15 s", none red.
- **The restart after an update died silently.** A pythonw started by another pythonw is handed an output pipe whose reader is closed. Its first print raised OSError (errno 22), so it exited before logging. Measured with a probe. Under pythonw, output now always goes to the log, and startup errors are logged and shown in a message box.

**Seen live.**
- **Backup.** The backup task was started by hand at 15:08 ET.
  - At 15:09:51 the backup card matched `progress.json` exactly: table `public.clv_pinnacle_mainline_once_2026_10_14`, 1 row copied in 4 s, 6 of 338 tables done.
  - At 15:13:17 it matched again: table `public.job_heartbeats`, 707 rows, 220 s, 3 rows a second, 103 tables done.
  - The pass also writes a `fetch_` log first, which the reader now counts.
- **Tennis, during the 15:22 pass.** The card matched `Win32_Process` (`scripts.tennis_dk_probe` under `tennis_feeds_run.ps1`) and the pass log (started 20:22:01Z, no source finished yet).
  - It showed one wrong thing: the launcher empties a source's summary file when it starts it, so the DraftKings row read "no summary, finished 15:22" while it was still reading.
  - Fixed. Seen at 15:26: "This pass, by source", DraftKings "reading now", and the other two marked as the previous pass.
- **The update on start.** With the copy one commit behind and the target set to this branch (`PC_MONITOR_UPDATE_REF`), a pythonw start logged "moved e840d9cd -> c71a6c68". It then restarted itself on the new commit and served on 8790 within 5 s.
- **The page as shipped.** No errors in the browser console. "No exit recorded" shows for launcher runs that ended without an exit line.

**Sign-in start** (mike: *"Yes, at sign-in"*). Task `\BettingModel\PcMonitor` was registered by
`scripts/pc_monitor_shortcut.ps1 -SignInTask`: 2 min after sign-in, as mike, Interactive and Limited,
IgnoreNew, no time limit. It runs `pythonw -m pc_monitor --stay --no-window` in the copy.
- Started once by hand at 15:34:41, it ran as pythonw with `--stay` (pid 23896) and served at 15:34:43.
- A `--stay` copy with a 6 s idle limit was still running after 25 s; the same copy without `--stay` had exited.
- The trigger itself fires only at a real sign-in; MJACode/betting-model#1559 stays open until that is seen.

**Also fixed here.** `mobile-tsc` had failed on master since MJACode/betting-model#1556: TS7022 at
`mobile/scripts/verify_no_use_before_declare.ts:69`, found by running tsc on this PC (node from the
pip wheel, TypeScript from the main checkout's `node_modules`). Master merged the same one-line
annotation in MJACode/betting-model#1561 while this branch waited, so the rebase keeps master's copy.

**Not done.** The feed still records no per-league read count. The window says so rather than
editing the feed, because a merge that touches the feed restarts it mid-game.

## 2026-11-21 — AI assistant: the connector server and the website approval page

**Changed.**
- **`supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts`**, deployed as edge function `mcp` (verify_jwt off; it checks the token itself). It speaks MCP over Streamable HTTP, stateless JSON-RPC.
  - Seven tools: `get_slate`, `find_player`, `get_player_recent_games`, `get_player_hit_rates`, `get_player_leaders`, `get_best_price` and `get_game_lines`.
  - Every call is metered first through `begin_call` as `mcp_meter_role`. Reads run only against `mcp_stats` as `mcp_reader`, read-only with a 3 s timeout.
  - A request without a token gets a 401 with `WWW-Authenticate` pointing at the protected-resource metadata.
- **The website approval page, `signalbase-ai.com/oauth/consent`** (`web/templates/consent.html`, `web/src/consent-boot.js`, built by `web/build.py` into `dist/oauth/consent/` and `dist/consent.js`). The member signs in with an email code, sees the app's name and its return host, and ticks the AI Assistant Terms (§19). On Allow, `mcp_accept_terms` runs, then Supabase's consent call, then the page follows the redirect. Trial and App Review accounts get a plain "not available" message instead.
- **`web/vercel.json`** rewrites `/mcp` and `/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource[/mcp]` to the function.

**Verified.**
- The deployed function, from outside: no token gets 401 with the header, a fake token gets 401, GET gets 405, and the metadata JSON is served.
- The approval page in headless Chromium against a mocked Supabase:
  - a missing request shows the error;
  - paid sign-in leads to approval, with Allow disabled until the box is ticked, then a redirect back to the client;
  - the founder line shows no limit;
  - trial and reviewer accounts see the ineligible message;
  - a client name containing HTML is shown as text;
  - there are no console or CSP errors.
- `tests/test_web_site.py` passes.

**Not done yet.** The app screens (Settings › AI Assistant, setup, connected, trial and onboarding). A live end-to-end connection from Claude, ChatGPT and Grok.

## 2026-11-21 — AI assistant: the Stats views the connector reads

**Changed.** `mcp_stats_views_2026_11_21.sql`, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` after the foundation. It is one DO block that runs only while `mcp_stats v1` is missing.
- **Views in `mcp_stats`, owned by postgres (BYPASSRLS), SELECT for `mcp_reader` only:**
  - `player_games`: long format, all ten logs. Stat names follow the prop markets. MLB innings convert to outs.
  - `players`: for name search.
  - `games`.
  - `prop_prices` and `game_prices`: pre-game only (`snapshot_at <= commence_time`), the seven `BEST_LINE_BOOKMAKERS` books under display names, main lines only.
  - `prop_markets` and `books`.
  - No source, provider, pick or model fields.
- **Name search.** `pg_trgm` in `extensions`, plus a GIN trigram index on the player-name column of each of the ten logs.

**Measured on production**, in rolled-back transactions, as `mcp_reader`:
- Name search: `ILIKE '%joki%'` on `nba_player_game_log` took 3,192 ms with no index, and 1,330 ms with the index when DISTINCT ON stayed in the same query, because the planner kept the player_id index walk. With the filter in a MATERIALIZED CTE first, it took 3 ms. The connector must query that way.
- With the views: find_player 5 ms (Nikola Jokic, DEN, 203999), last 10 rebounds 4 ms, today's NBA slate 1 ms (6 games), rebound prices 14 ms, season points leaders 62 ms.
- `mcp_reader` reading `public.player_game_log` directly got 42501, as intended.

**Found and fixed before merge.** The first draft's skip check used `obj_description('mcp_stats.player_games'::regclass)`, which raises 42P01 before the view exists, so the first worker pass would have failed. It now uses `to_regclass`, and `test_stats_views_file_shape` pins that. Also, MLB market names came from memory in the draft. Checked against `latest_prop_odds`, they are the `batter_*` and `pitcher_*` keys. NHL has no goals over/under (only anytime scorer), so that mapping was removed.

**For the connector.** Take "current season" as the latest season in each sport's data, not a date rule. The leaders check ran on NBA season 2026, which is 2025-26 under the ending-year label.

## 2026-11-21 — The first monthly full and nightly diff of the PC backup; three reporting bugs fixed (#1494, PR #1539)

**Asked.** mike chose "Stop it and fix" on 11-20 (entry below): fix the slow check, merge it, run the full again. This entry is what followed. It is plumbing, not a model change, so no `Updated-By`.

**The monthly full** (started by hand, 19:44 UTC on 11-20).
- Finished at 23:30 UTC: 331 tables, none failed, 16,871,400 rows copied, exit 0.
- `nfl_odds_history` took 891 s on the fixed check. Per chunk, the fixed check takes about 0.26 s, where the old whole-table read took 33 s (11-20 entry below).
- The local database copy (DuckDB) rebuilt in 74 s.

**The first nightly diff** (00:30 ET on 11-21, pass `20261121T053003Z`).
- Ran 05:30 to 06:41 UTC. 337 tables, none failed, 4,441,045 rows copied. On 11-19 it was 35,347,926.
- It ran the OLD check: the scheduled task's copy of the script (`bin\local_mirror.py`) was refreshed at 00:30 ET, and the fix reached master at 02:00 ET.
- `nfl_odds_history` still took only 124 s. It fingerprinted 40 chunks (`last_reconcile` in the table's saved record on the PC), against 154 on 11-20. 40 whole-table reads at 33 s would take 22 minutes alone, so these chunks did not hit the slow plan. Which chunks hit it depends on where they sit in the key (11-20 entry below). The fixed check removes that dependence; the monthly full, which checks every chunk, is where it matters most.

**How the fix reached master.**
- #1518 went in with batch PR #1525 at 02:00 ET, merge `8bebbc4b`.
- Both cloud services redeployed to SUCCESS at 07:02 UTC.
- The in-play loops logged "no game in progress" just before the restart.

**Three reporting bugs, fixed in PR #1539** (merged 09:00 UTC, `760ad534`; both services SUCCESS at 09:02).
- **The full was recorded twice on the dashboard.**
  - Row 10 was my report by hand. Row 11 was the next pass's catch-up step.
  - The catch-up only guarded on its own pass stamp, and a report by hand carries another.
  - It now writes nothing when a row already records that finished copy run.
  - Neither row was deleted.
- **A cache table would have shown as "differs" every night to Dec 1.**
  - A diff leaves `latest_odds` and `latest_prop_odds` as they are, so their files kept the full's verdict. `latest_prop_odds` changed while the full copied it.
  - A report now leaves out any table its copy run did not check.
- **The log's total line said Supabase had 13,237,404 fewer rows than the PC.** That was the two skipped caches counted as missing, less 8 real rows. The difference is now taken over the tables counted in Supabase.
- **Retool.** The runs list already showed each copy once, but picked the entry with no exit code. It now picks the one with an exit code, so the 11-20 full reads "OK" on the live page. Published in Retool (`rr-publish-1795247455494-4a77f0a`) and copied back to `retool/models-ops/` the same day.
- **The "Tables that differ" caption.** Once a diff's report leaves the caches out, "All 337 tables matched" would claim more than was checked. On a diff it now says every table that diff checked matched, and that the two caches are checked only by the monthly full. Published in Retool (`rr-publish-1795252781462-92c58a5`) and copied back here: 22,967 bytes, 465 lines, matching Retool's published file. The earlier thread would not publish its second edit ("no commits found"), so this went through a fresh thread from the published app.

**Verified.**
- Each new test was watched failing on master's code, for the reason it targets.
- Full suite, 6 processes: 16 failed, 13,568 passed, 58 skipped. The same 16 fail on a copy of `origin/master` at `8bebbc4b`, none in a touched file.
- After the merge:
  - The guard's query on production finds row 10 for the 11-20 full, row 12 for the 11-21 diff, and nothing for an unknown run.
  - A dry run of the merged report on the PC's real copy lists only `tennis_dk_selections` as differing, without `latest_prop_odds`. It wrote nothing.

**Not done.**
- The nightly wrapper takes its scripts from the main checkout's `origin/master` without fetching it first. So a merged fix reaches the next run only if something fetched in between. Nothing fetched from 20:50 ET on 11-20 to 02:03 ET on 11-21 (the ref's reflog). The fetch at 04:10 ET on 11-21 covers the 11-22 run. #1542.
- The 11-22 run is the first on the fixed code. Its check is #1541.
- Retool AI credits for the three dashboard edits: 13.39 used (902.88 before, 889.49 after, of 1,000 a month) (resets 2026-12-14).

**Review follow-up.** Two of the PR review's findings were fixed. Select all now covers only untracked picks, so one tap can't put a placed bet back in. A late load of tracked state now unticks only picks that are newly tracked, so a pick you ticked back in on purpose stays ticked. The test was extended and fails without the fix.

## 2026-11-21 — AI assistant (MCP): the database gate

**Asked.** Matt: "Yes build that DB."

**Measured first.** `postgres` has BYPASSRLS and CREATEROLE. Subscriptions carry `status` `trialing` or `active`, plus `current_period_end` and `trial_end`. Each sport keeps its own player log (`player_game_log` is MLB only), so the Stats views go in with the connector's tools, not here.

**Changed.** `mcp_foundation_2026_11_21.sql`, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` after `app_founders`. It is one DO block that runs only while the `mcp_meter v1` marker is missing.
- **Schemas.** `mcp_stats` holds the connector's views (empty for now) and gives `mcp_reader` SELECT on them by default privileges. `mcp_meter` holds `limits` (one tunable row: 10 / 30 / 5 / 200 rows, burst 120 s / 5 calls / 300 s, terms version `2026-11-21`), `terms_acceptances`, `connections` (one active per member, by partial unique index), `calls` (the request log) and `suspensions`.
- **Roles.** `mcp_reader` and `mcp_meter_role`, both NOLOGIN and granted to postgres, so the server runs `SET LOCAL ROLE`. Role-level settings apply at login, so the server also sets read-only mode and the 3 s timeout per transaction.
- **`mcp_meter.entitlement`.** Returns founder, then paid (`active`), then comped, then trial, else none. App Review accounts do not get the connector.
- **`mcp_meter.begin_call`.** Takes an advisory lock per member, then checks entitlement, suspension, current terms and one app. A lookup counts toward the current question when it comes within 2 min of the last one, the question has fewer than 5 lookups, and it is no more than 5 min old. Limits are 10 questions, 30 lookups, 200 rows a day and 5 a minute, and founders skip them. The day resets at midnight ET. Every call is logged, refused ones included.
- **`finish_call`** records the row count.
- **For members (authenticated only):** `public.mcp_accept_terms`, `public.mcp_my_status` (feeds the Settings screens, including `trial_converts_at`) and `public.mcp_disconnect`. A disconnect sets a timestamp; nothing deletes a row.

**Verified on scratch.** `tests/test_mcp_foundation.py` (11 tests) applies the file twice through `_apply_one` with the worker's grant rewrite. It covers trial and no-access refusal, terms required, a burst counting as one question, the 10th question allowed and the 11th refused, the next ET day starting over, 5 a minute, the 30-lookup cap, founders unlimited, one app with disconnect keeping the row, suspension, and the member functions (anon refused, a wrong terms version refused). It also checks that anon, authenticated, `mcp_reader` and `mcp_meter_role` cannot read `mcp_meter.calls`, and that none of authenticator, anon or authenticated can become either role. With trial accounts let through, the trial test failed. 321 passed across the related files.

**Flagged.** The Privacy draft says request logs are kept 90 days. CLAUDE.md "no data is ever deleted" allows removal only by archiving, and only mike can override that. Nothing here deletes, so the two must be reconciled before launch.

## 2026-11-21 — site_public_record is security_invoker; anon still reads it through the definer function

**Asked.** Supabase Security Advisor flags `public.site_public_record` (security definer view). The view has to stay readable by anon. The 2026-11-19 break of this view refused the website record for about 26 minutes.

**Changed.** `data/migrations/site_public_record_invoker_2026_11_21.sql` is in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`. One DO block. It recreates the view `WITH (security_invoker = true, security_barrier = true)` selecting the same ten columns from `site_public_record_rows()`. It does not revoke anon. Anon SELECT is granted only while that grant is missing, or while `picks` is missing or anon can still read `picks`. The hand file stays out of `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS` and still creates the view with `security_invoker = false`; its header now names the worker file.

**Skip.** Reloptions contain one of `security_invoker=true|on|yes|1` (the linter's list) and `pg_get_viewdef` contains `site_public_record_rows` and `refreshed_at`. Anon grants are not the skip. A missing function returns a notice and does no DDL, so a database that never had the hand file does not fail the refresh pass.

**Other views.** The only public view in `data/migrations` created with `security_invoker` false is this one. The others are created with `security_invoker = on`, or the migration that rewrites them sets that option in the same body. `internal.ncaab_margin_*` is not in an exposed schema and anon and authenticated have no grant. `mv_scored_pick_outcomes` is a materialized view; this lint is `relkind = v`. `clv_standard_v2_2026_10_14.sql` replaces `v_public_track_record` without setting the option, and only on the pass that adds `clv_game_settled`. On Postgres 18.6, `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` without `WITH` clears reloptions (measured: `security_invoker=on` became null). That file was left as it is: its body does not run once the column is present, and the advisor item named `site_public_record`.

**Verified.** Scratch Postgres 18.6. Before: reloptions `security_invoker=false, security_barrier=true`, anon reads NBA 2-1 and NFL 1-1, anon is refused on `v_public_track_record`. After: reloptions `security_invoker=true, security_barrier=true`, the viewdef calls `site_public_record_rows()` and has `refreshed_at`, the same two rows, anon still refused on the inner view. Second pass: DDL counter 0. After a step A revoke of anon on `picks` and the inner view: DDL counter 0, same rows. A database with no function: DDL counter 0. `format_type` after `CREATE OR REPLACE` is the production list: text, integer, integer, integer, integer, numeric(12,2), numeric(12,2), date, date, timestamp with time zone. The function return type stays `units numeric, risked numeric` (Postgres drops a numeric typmod on a function result) and `proconfig` is `search_path=public, pg_temp`. `tests/test_site_public_record_invoker.py`: 7 passed. No SQL was applied to production. Master was merged in (no force-push).

**Not done.** No pause, threshold, stake, or model change. `graphify` is not installed here, so the graph was not rebuilt.

## 2026-11-20 — The PC backup's chunk check scanned the whole NFL odds history table; it now uses the index (#1494)

**Asked.** mike: *"why was nfl odds history so slow last night"*. Then, once the cause was found and today's full backup was hitting it too, he chose "Stop it and fix".

**Why it was slow** (database log, `auto_explain` lines; backup logs and the table's saved record on the PC).
- The night of 11-20 the backup spent 7,296 s reading `nfl_odds_history`, after taking out the pauses. On 11-19 it spent 1,861 s.
- The work was the same size both nights: 154 chunks fingerprinted on 11-20 (`last_reconcile` in the saved record) against about 150 on 11-19. It pulled 748,792 rows on 11-20 against 954,748.
- The worker was not busier: 0.40 s of pipeline steps per second during the pass on 11-20, against 0.50 on 11-19. No worker job ran, and no large table was vacuumed during either pass.
- The database log recorded 104 fingerprint statements on that table on 11-20, every one a whole-table read (`Seq Scan`), at 33.4 s on average. That is 3,478 s. The backup then rests as long as any statement over 10 s took, which doubles it to the measured time. On 11-19 there were 18 such scans.
- The cause is how Postgres prices a range on a key of several columns. It prices the row comparison `(snapshot_at, game_id, bookmaker, market) > (...)` by its first column alone, and prices the two ends as unrelated. A 50,000-row chunk was priced at 2.7 million rows, so it chose to read the whole 4.4 GB table.
- Which chunks hit this depends on where they sit. Each night fingerprints the newest 40 chunks plus up to 115 of the chunks checked longest ago. On 11-19 those were the oldest seasons, near the start of the key, where the mispricing is small. On 11-20 they were 2026-09-11 to 09-25, where it is large.

**Today's full backup.**
- It started 04:54 ET, paused for the morning, and resumed at 07:28.
- `latest_prop_odds` failed at 09:23 ET after six reconnects. The connection dropped three more times between 11:11 and 11:36. The PC logged no sleep, and its only Wi-Fi events were roams at 05:53 and 12:05, so the drops are not explained.
- `ncaab_pbp` took 5,690 s; the night before it took 102 s. Its checks use the index (0.38 s a chunk, measured afterwards), so that time was not database time. Not explained yet.
- From 11:40 ET it was scanning the whole `nfl_odds_history` table for each chunk: 31 scans at 32 s. Stopped at about 12:35 ET on mike's word. At that pace it had about 13 more hours on that table alone.

**Changed** (`scripts/local_mirror.py`, `keyset_where`).
- On a key of two or more columns, each bound now also bounds the first column (`a >= x`, `a <= y`). The row comparison already implies it, so the rows that match are unchanged.
- **Plans before and after** (`EXPLAIN`, nothing read), on 63 chunks across seven tables:
  - before: 6 whole-table scans, all on `nfl_odds_history`;
  - after: none.
- `odds`, `player_prop_odds`, `picks_log` and `kalshi_game_markets` have one-column keys and were never affected.
- **A real check after the fix** (`EXPLAIN ANALYZE`): an `nfl_odds_history` chunk took 0.26 s through the index, against 33 s for the whole-table scan.

**Verified.**
- The new test and the updated old one were watched failing on the old code. The backup's 113 tests pass.
- Full suite, side by side with a copy of `origin/master` at 251a2720: the same 29 tests fail on both, none in a touched file; the branch passes one more (the new test).

## 2026-11-18 — The test suite no longer writes job check-in rows (#1456, PR #1462)

**Asked.** The EuroLeague session found it (entry below). mike, in that
session: *"push the heartbeat fix and open its PR"*, then *"merge"*. PR #1462
was merged at 20:05 UTC (merge commit `c08d003c`), and #1456 was closed. This
is test plumbing, not a model change, so no `Updated-By`.

**Changed.**
- `tests/conftest.py` replaces the check-in writer
  (`tracking.job_heartbeat.beat`) once, before any test file loads. Every
  caller looks the writer up when it runs, so the one replacement covers
  everything that writes a check-in:
  - job registration and the "switched off here" marks;
  - the scheduler's job wrapper;
  - the in-play loops.
- A test that needs the real writer asks for the new `real_heartbeat` fixture,
  and must give it a stand-in database.
- New `tests/test_suite_writes_no_heartbeats.py` (5 tests), added to the PR
  check's list. It traps every database connection, through both
  `data.db.get_connection` and `psycopg2.connect`. It checks four things:
  - building the scheduler opens no connection;
  - running one job opens no connection;
  - with the real writer put back, the same trap catches one connection per
    check-in, so the guard cannot pass blind;
  - the real writer still writes its row to a stand-in database.

**Measured.**
- **The guard on master** failed. With the pipeline role and the NFL in-play
  worker off, the build opened 27 connections and running one job opened 1
  more. A build with every setting at its default opened 32, the figure in
  the entry below (counted the same way, after the merge).
- **The guard on the branch** passed, 5 of 5.
- **The 43 test files that build the scheduler or touch the check-in code:**
  1,260 passed, 0 failed, in 6:00.
- **`tests/test_overnight_polling.py`** was run with no database address set,
  from a copy with no `.env`. It took 9:29 on master and 0:45 on the branch.
- **The full suite, master vs branch,** was run with the bad-address URL from
  the entry below. Master had 19 failed and 13,280 passed. The branch had 19
  failed and 13,285 passed. The failing set is the same 19 tests (compared
  test by test from the junit files). The only test names on the branch and
  not on master are the 5 in the new file.
  The wall times were 3:53:41 and 3:16:13. Other full suites were running on
  this PC at the same time, so those two times are not a comparison.
- **The full suite on the branch with no database address set,** from this
  copy with no `.env`: 3:16:15, with the same 19 failures. The run with the
  bad address, at the same time, took 3:16:13. So the 14-hour stall is gone.

**Found, not fixed here (#1465).** At least five tests still try to open a real
database connection, through three other routes. With no address set, each
refused connection costs about 4 s, 24.3 s in all. They were found by
comparing per-test times between the two branch runs, which ran at the same
time: a test under 1 s with a bad address that gained 3.5 s or more with
none. That only catches tests that are otherwise fast and that swallow the
refusal. A slower test that dials could hide in the load noise. Each route
was then traced with a trap on `psycopg2.connect`:
- `test_cbb_pairs.py::test_the_set_aside_under_is_still_written_as_a_priced_none_row`
  reads `model_auto_pauses` (`models/scorer.py` `_auto_paused_models`).
- `test_eggz_picks.py::test_the_main_recap_waits_while_a_game_on_that_date_is_unfinished`
  calls the grader (`tracking/paper_tracker.settle_picks`) twice, through
  `tracking/discord_notifier.record_held_for_grading`.
  - The test's stand-in database returns the same row for every query.
  - The grader reads the row's fifth field as the date, and that field is
    the price, -110. So it grades a "date" of `-110`.
- Three tests in `test_nfl_live_pick_writer.py` run the default announcer.
  The trap caught one connection in each, from the live push notifier
  (`tracking/push_notifier.notify_live_signals`). It runs its query for the
  test's date, 2026-09-13, against the `.env` database. From the main
  checkout, that database is production.
  - Production holds 0 unsettled live bets on 2026-09-13 (read-only count,
    2026-11-18). So today the query finds nothing and no push goes out.

## 2026-11-20 — "0 measured" said nothing was measured; it now says every bet matched the close

**Asked.** mike: *"Euroleague said 0 measured in the records last night"*.

**Found.** The record was right and the sentence was wrong. Of the 10 EuroLeague bets settled on 2026-11-19, 1 was in-play (not measured, by design), 1 had no close and 8 were measured: all 8 closed at exactly the price taken. Each close was a real later pull, 8 minutes after the bet and 2 before the start (the books' own update times moved, the prices did not). An all-push set has no beat rate to divide, and `clv_line` printed it as `0 measured, 8 push`. The bet with no close is "ZAL ML" at BetMGM +105: BetMGM had taken its full-game moneyline down, absent from both the 17:56 closing pull and the 17:58 pull while its spread and first-half lines were there. EuroLeague all-time, pre-game: 28 priced, 24 at the price taken, 3 beat, 1 trailed, 1 no close; Pinnacle's fair close on 11 of them averages -5.23%.

**Built.** `discord_notifier.all_matched_text`: `all N matched the close` (`1 matched the close` for one) for the all-push case of `clv_line`, which the Discord recap, the X posts and eggz all use. The app says the same through `clvBeat.allMatchedText`: the Record hero cell, its spoken label, the closing-line sheet and its per-split rows, the model page's Beat close tile, the opening comparison and the share text. The mixed case is unchanged (`75% beat close (3/4, 24 push)`).

**Design review** (the app's UX reviewer, before the PR): six fixes taken. Where the words stand alone they start with a capital ("All 8 matched the close"). The opening comparison no longer prints "Beat close all 8 matched the close", which wrapped after the count so its first line read as a win. The share text for an all-matched set opens "At the book we bet, all 8 matched the close" rather than "Beat the closing price ...". VoiceOver pauses after "Beat the closing line,". The model page's tile drops its "8 push" caption when the value already says it. The Record hero cell may wrap to three lines when it stacks at the largest text sizes. The mixed case keeps "N push".

**Checked.** The new expectations in `tests/test_clv_push_beat_rate.py`, `tests/test_discord_notifier.py` and `mobile/scripts/verify_record_hero.ts` (run by `tests/test_record_hero.py`) fail on master and pass with the change; with the X, eggz and record-units tests, 322 passed.

**Not done.** Last night's post is not re-posted or corrected (no restated records); the new wording starts with the next recap after this reaches the worker.
## 2026-11-20 — NBA first basket graded at real prices: nothing passes; the blend fails only the interval

**Asked.** mike: "yes buy the history".

**Bought.** `player_first_basket` and `player_first_team_basket`, one snapshot 10 minutes before each
tip, 2024-01-10..2026-11-19: 246,350 rows on 3,094 games in the final run, 50,698 credits billed.
Balance after, all spending included: 1,286,602.

**Graded** (`docs/nba_first_basket.md` section 6; each test written down before it was computed): the
model alone +5.9% on 2,924 bets, and the books price it better; the books' median alone +3.2% on 1,953;
a blend of the two +7.5% on 1,930 (first basket) and +9.5% on 1,056 (team first basket), five bars of
six, every interval reaching below zero. Nothing shipped. The team market is quoted by only DraftKings
and FanDuel this season, so its rule cannot fire live.

## 2026-11-20 — Tennis bets get their closing price

**Asked.** mike: *"currently have zero measured CLV fix it"*.

**Found.** Every surface already measured closing-line value except tennis: the published record had 1,365 measured pre-game bets of 1,674 (the Key Metrics query, run through Retool's own database connection), the daily recap's all-time line 854 graded, the app's record view 1,207. The models with nothing measured were the in-play ones (unmeasured by design) and `atp_moneyline_shop`. Its one settled bet, Alexander Zverev ML at Hard Rock -275 on 2026-11-19, was stamped "no pull of this book after the bet and before the start", although Hard Rock was polled at 19:13 and 19:23 UTC, before the 19:30 listed start. The final close (`tracking/clv_close.py`) read only `odds`, `player_prop_odds` and `clv_close_quotes`; the tennis card writes its prices only to `tennis_odds_quotes`.

**Built.** `clv_close.tennis_quotes`: the bet book's match-winner quote at each of the card's live polls (`tennis_odds_fetches`) after the bet and at or before the start, read as the book's newest change-only row at or before the poll. The match is the feed event the card decided (`tennis_moneyline_decisions`). `scripts/clv_close_history remeasure-none --model ...` re-measures only the named models.

**Checked.** Four new tests in `tests/test_clv_close.py` (a held price, a moved price with other books, markets and in-play rows around it, no pull after the bet, the worker's stage); three fail without the fix and all 28 pass with it. Re-measured on production: the Zverev bet closed at Hard Rock -300 (its 19:23 UTC pull), +2.27%, a beat; read back from `picks` and from `v_model_health_bets` (status price, verdict beat).

**Not done.** Pinnacle's fair-close figure for tennis (`market_clv_pct`): its writer reads `odds` only (#1505). A tennis bet settled before this reaches the worker is stamped with no close again; the command above re-measures it.

## 2026-11-20 — Pre-request hook lost anon EXECUTE through the worker's grant rewrite; fixed

**Found at the 17:25 UTC check.** `authenticator` had `pgrst.db_pre_request=public.premium_pre_request` and `app_founders` existed, but anon lacked EXECUTE on the hook. The cause: once anon cannot read `picks`, `view_migrations._without_combined_anon_grants` rewrites any `TO anon, authenticated` to `TO authenticated`, and the hook's grant line read `TO anon, authenticated, service_role, postgres`. With the hook set, PostgREST would have refused every anon request, including the website record and the crash logger.

**Impact, measured.** Edge logs show 138 REST requests from 16:30 to 16:34 UTC (all authenticated 200/204) and none after that until the fix. The hook was set at the post-merge restart, about 16:40. No anon request arrived while anon lacked the grant, and `postgres_logs` show no hook error.

**Fixed.**
- `GRANT EXECUTE ... TO anon` was applied directly at about 17:57 UTC, and anon and authenticated both executed the hook.
- The migration now grants anon on its own statement and re-grants it on any pass where it is missing.
- `test_premium_gate_matview_hook_through_the_worker_path` now applies the file the way the worker does: with step A in place and through `_without_combined_anon_grants`. It failed with 42501 for anon before the fix.
- `test_worker_migrations_do_not_reopen_anon` now exempts grants on functions in `ANON_KEEP_FUNCTIONS` only.
- 301 tests pass.

**Lesson.** A test that applies a migration file directly skips the worker's own rewrite. Apply what the worker runs.

## 2026-11-20 — Premium gate, part 2: a PostgREST pre-request hook closes the scored-picks matview

**Both earlier migrations are live.** At 16:10 UTC production had the `tracked_bets` owner column and three owner policies, 66 policies gated on `has_app_access()`, the ops tables closed to authenticated, and `site_public_record` at 10 rows. `pipeline_log` showed no `dispatch:apply-view-migrations` row after the 15:17 failure, so the post-merge worker restart most likely applied them. That is inferred, not measured.

**Correction: no tracked bets were hidden.** `tracked_bets` holds 149 rows on 26 devices, and none has a `user_id`. `device_push_tokens` maps one device, which holds 111 rows. Nothing else maps. The app keeps tracked bets in AsyncStorage (`useTrackedBets`) and never SELECTs the table. It only inserts, untracks through `untrack_tracked_bet`, and clears through `clear_my_tracked_bets`, and both helpers handle a NULL-owner row. `tracking/push_notifier.py` reads it as postgres. So the earlier "invisible in the app" was wrong, and the planned claim RPC and app change were dropped.

**Changed.** `premium_gate_matview_2026_11_20.sql`, in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`:
- It creates `public.premium_pre_request()`, an invoker function with an empty search_path and a `v1` marker, and sets `pgrst.db_pre_request` on `authenticator` when it is not already set.
- For `current_user = authenticated` without `has_app_access()`, it raises 42501 on the six endpoints over `mv_scored_pick_outcomes`: the matview, `v_model_full_outcome_picks`, `v_model_full_outcome_record`, and `rpc/custom_model_backtest`, `custom_model_backtest_posted` and `custom_model_picks`. Every other path, and anon, returns at once.
- Anon keeps EXECUTE, because PostgREST calls the hook as the request role. The function was added to `ANON_KEEP_FUNCTIONS`, `RPC_ANON_CALLABLE`, step B's read-back and the stray check.
- The undo is `premium_gate_matview_2026_11_20_undo.sql` (`ALTER ROLE authenticator RESET`).
- It does not wrap the views and functions, because about ten active migrations redefine them behind their own skip guards.

**Verified.**
- On production, in a rolled-back transaction, for the first account without access: the six endpoints were refused, and `/games`, `/picks` and `/rpc/my_access` were allowed by the hook (part 1's RLS returns 0 rows there).
- Matt was allowed on three of the six.
- Anon was allowed on `/site_public_record`.
- Afterwards the function was absent from production.
- `test_premium_gate_matview_hook_through_the_worker_path` applies the file twice on scratch with an `authenticator` role. It failed when the file was a no-op, and passes with the real file: 299 tests across lockdown, anon-readable, string-literal and DDL-guard.

## 2026-11-20 — Bet all: a Placed tick on each pick replaces the per-book "did you place" question

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-20 (screenshot of the send phase with "Stop Bet all?" stacked on the DraftKings card): "I don't like this set up that confirms what I get for tracking. Find a better way." Offered three options; he chose a tick on each pick, and no capture of the actual price taken.

**Changed.** `mobile/src/components/BetAllSheet.tsx` send phase: each pick row is a checkbox; ticking calls `trackMany([pick])`, unticking `untrackPick`. The tick reads `useTrackedBets.isTracked`, so it is the tracked state itself (a pick already tracked shows ticked). A book with more than one pick has "Placed all N", which tracks only the untracked ones. Removed: the return-from-book question card (Placed · track / Not yet / Skip, all-or-nothing per book), "Mark as placed", and the "Stop Bet all?" warning. Header is "Bet all · X of N tracked"; a Done button is always there. The backdrop stays inert mid-send so a stray tap does not drop the member back at Choose; X and Done close with no warning, since nothing is pending.

**UX review** (frontend-ux-designer): no blockers; three should-fix copy items all applied ("Tick what you placed" status after opening a book, one-sentence subtitle, footer no longer repeats the count). "Placed all" moved up under the picks.

**Verified.** `verify_bet_all.ts` ALL PASS; the five new checks fail against the old sheet. tsc: 0 diagnostics outside the baseline. `ux_scan`: 0 findings. Not verified on a device.

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-20: "start the premium gate. But allow the founders. Matt and Mike have unlimited searches with the MCP." Comped accounts count as premium. There is no free tier.

Of the 14 accounts, 12 are comped and 2 have no access. Which account is Mike's is not recorded here. The MCP's unlimited allowance for Matt and Mike is a requirement for the MCP plan, not for this gate.

- Every permissive SELECT policy in public that said `USING (true)` for authenticated (66 at measure time) becomes `USING ((SELECT public.has_app_access()))`.
- The authenticated grant is revoked on 14 ops, telemetry and retired-golf tables that nothing under `mobile/src` reads.
- A pass with nothing left to change issues no DDL.
- A read-back raises if any open SELECT policy remains.
- The undo is `premium_gate_2026_11_20_undo.sql` (not active).

**Verified before merge, on production, in rolled-back transactions.**
- As the first account with no access: `picks`, `games`, `model_action_thresholds`, `v_public_track_record`, `player_game_log` and `latest_odds` all read 0. `odds_api_quota` has no grant. `site_public_record` reads 10.
- As Matt: `picks` 198,242, `games` 264,777, thresholds 87, record view 60, `player_game_log` 492,657, `latest_odds` 3,475,341.
- After both rollbacks, a catalog check still showed 66 open policies and 0 gated.
- `test_premium_gate_through_the_worker_path` applies the file twice on scratch. It failed with 67 open policies when the file was replaced by a no-op, and passes with the real file.

**tracked_bets owner migration failed its first worker pass, fixed here.** At 15:17 UTC `dispatch:apply-view-migrations` reported 130 of 131 applied, and `tracked_bets_owner_2026_11_20.sql` failed with `column "user_id" does not exist`. Production's `app_timings` has `device_id` but no `user_id`. The step B file assumed both, and so would have failed the same way. The DO block rolled back, so `tracked_bets` was unchanged. The two helper functions read the same column at call time, so untracking would have raised 42703. The backfill and both helpers now check `information_schema.columns` for `user_id`, not just `to_regclass`. The scratch test creates `app_timings` the way production has it. It failed with the production error before the fix and now passes, including a mapped row from `feedback_threads` and a call to each helper.

## 2026-11-20 — Anon-key lockdown step B, part 1 applied; part 2 (tracked_bets) waiting on Matt in the SQL editor

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-20: "Continue with B". Premium gate yes, and comped accounts count as premium. Comped accounts get MCP. MCP allows 10 questions a day.

**Before.** Snapshot `pre_step_b_20261120` (413 relacl, 115 proacl, 81 policy, 6 defacl, 3 attacl). The anon counts were 73 readable / 19 grant-only / 55 functions / 67 sequences. That is step A's end state: 98−25, 20−1, 82−28+1 (`site_public_record_rows`). `tracked_bets` was copied to `ops.tracked_bets_pre_step_b_20261120`: 145 rows, md5 of the ordered rows equal.

**Part 1, applied through the MCP.** These are the revoke, policy and function-grant statements of step B, plus its read-back guard, in one transaction. The guard passed. Afterwards anon cannot read `games` and cannot write `tracked_bets`, authenticated still reads `games`, and `site_public_record` returns 10 rows.

**Found.** The prepared step B file named `save_device_push_token(text, text, text, boolean, text)`. Production has `(text, text, text, boolean, text, uuid)`, which `notification_inbox_2026_11_19.sql` replaced, so the file as written fails with 42883. The spec, step B and undo B now use the six-argument signature. The scratch test calls it with six arguments. The new `p_user_id` must equal `auth.uid()` or the call raises 42501, so anon keeping EXECUTE cannot attach a token to another account.

**Part 2, not applied.** This is the `tracked_bets` owner column, the backfill, the owner policies and the two SECURITY DEFINER helpers. Each attempt through the MCP stopped on the server's confirmation prompt, which got no answer in 60 s, three times. It is now `data/migrations/anon_lockdown_step_b_part2_tracked_bets_2026_11.sql`, for the Supabase SQL editor. It renames and retargets the two legacy anon policies instead of dropping them, which gives the same three owner policies. Its guard refuses to commit unless all three policies are owner-scoped, anon has no write or execute access, and the table still holds 145 rows.

**Part 2 moved to the worker.** Matt, 2026-11-20: "you need to fix this. I shouldn't need to." The MCP prompt failed six times (five `execute_sql`, one `apply_migration`). Part 2 is now `tracked_bets_owner_2026_11_20.sql` in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`: one DO block, which returns at once when the owner column and the three owner policies are in place and nothing is open to anon. Functions are created through `EXECUTE $ddl$`. It has no row-count guard, because the worker runs it after live inserts. `test_tracked_bets_owner_migration_through_the_worker_path` runs it twice through `view_migrations._apply_one` on a scratch cluster after step A. The scratch test failed on a copy whose insert check was `true`, and passes on the real file. The standalone `anon_lockdown_step_b_part2_tracked_bets_2026_11.sql` is kept as the SQL-editor fallback. 1 pass hourly at :17 after merge.

**Hourly migrations to watch after B.** `add_live_scoreboard_2026_10_29.sql`, `add_scoreboard_splits_2026_10_29.sql` and `member_sportsbooks_2026_10_19.sql` skip only while anon holds its old grant. Step B removes that grant, so they run their bodies again. None of them re-grants anon, because every anon GRANT is gated on anon reading `picks`, which is false since step A. They do re-issue REVOKE/GRANT and, for member_sportsbooks, CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION each pass.

## 2026-11-20 — View migrations: byom_official_legs stops failing after step A

**Asked.** `dispatch:apply-view-migrations` failed every refresh pass from
2026-11-20 03:20 UTC. `pipeline_log`: 129/130 applied, FAILED
`byom_official_legs_2026_11_04.sql`, cannot change the return type of an
existing function. Postgres detail: row type defined by OUT parameters is
different. Hint: drop `byom_bets(integer,jsonb,text)` first.

**Cause.** Hand-applied anon-lockdown step A at 03:12:34 UTC
(`anon_lockdown_step_a_2026_11.sql`: the logged statement starts with the
same `REVOKE ALL ON public.picks FROM anon` list and includes
`REVOKE ALL ON public.byom_official_legs FROM anon`). The 03:10 pass had
succeeded. PR 1475 (`site_public_record` anon) is an unmerged draft; it did
not change this function. The skip guard still required anon SELECT on
`byom_official_legs`. After the revoke it did not skip, and
`CREATE OR REPLACE` hit `byom_bets`, whose OUT list was widened on 11-17
(`actual`, `home_score`, `away_score`) by `byom_bets_actual_2026_11_17.sql`.
Production at check time: anon cannot read `picks` or `byom_official_legs`;
authenticated and `service_role` can execute `byom_bets`; no other function
source and no view depends on `byom_bets`.

**Changed.** The skip also accepts the step A grant (authenticated SELECT,
no anon grant, anon cannot read `picks`), so the file does not rebuild
functions a later migration already replaced. If the body does run,
`byom_bets(integer, jsonb, text)` is dropped only when nothing else uses it,
then recreated, and the existing anon/authenticated EXECUTE grants run again.

**Verified.** On a scratch cluster the pre-fix file, after the same revoke,
raised `42P13` / `cannot change return type of existing function`. With the
fix, `tests/test_byom_official_legs.py` and the BYOM side/total/actual
migration tests: 63 passed. Two other `RETURNS TABLE` widenings
(`byom_backtest` in `byom_backtest_rpc_2026_10_16.sql`,
`player_h2h_stat_values_nfl` in `add_player_h2h_stat_values_rpcs.sql`) skip
on a function marker or a function count, not on the anon grant, so step A
does not send them back through `CREATE OR REPLACE`.

**Review.** A rebuild under Supabase-style default privileges (EXECUTE
granted to anon on a new function) left anon with EXECUTE, because
`REVOKE FROM PUBLIC` does not remove a direct grant. The seven functions
are now revoked from `PUBLIC, anon`, then granted to authenticated, to
anon only while anon can read `picks`, and to `service_role` when that
role exists. `byom_bets_actual_2026_11_17.sql` drops and recreates
`byom_bets`, so its revoke is the same. The caller check is
`prosrc ~ '\mbyom_bets\s*\('`. A scratch test with those default
privileges, `picks` present, and anon revoked failed on
`byom_risk_units` while the revoke was `PUBLIC` only (`anon EXECUTE`
was true). With the revoke, the same test passes, and re-applying the
ten later BYOM files ends on the widened `byom_bets` (`actual`,
`home_score`, `away_score`) with anon still off. Three other active
files still require an anon SELECT to skip
(`byom_facts_schema_2026_10_16.sql`, `open_picks_view_2026_11_04.sql`,
`discord_publish_state_2026_09_23.sql`). Measured after step A, anon
cannot read those objects, so those files re-enter. Their grants are
gated on anon reading `picks`, and the worker rewrites a combined
`TO anon, authenticated` to authenticated, so they do not hand anon
the grant back. `live_scoreboard` still has anon SELECT, so its guard
still skips. Not changed in this pass.

**Not done.** No pause, threshold, stake, or policy change. Step A itself
was not re-applied or undone. `graphify` is not installed here, so the
graph was not rebuilt.

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-19: "Fix the data leak first", then come back with the MCP plan. There is no free tier, only a free trial, and MCP is paid only.

**Applied, project vvprgnrmzeekokzkrkfu, 2026-11-20 03:12:33 UTC (10:12 PM ET on 11-19, from `postgres_logs`).** A fresh ACL snapshot `pre_step_a_20261119` (taken 01:07:40 UTC) went into `ops.acl_snapshot` (413 relacl, 114 proacl, 81 policy, 6 defacl, 3 attacl). The pre-apply counts read 98 readable / 20 grant-only / 82 functions / 67 sequences against the 97 / 20 / 82 / 67 baseline. The one extra relation is `site_public_record`, which is meant to be anon-readable. The other three new objects since 11-18 (`site_retired_models`, `game_first_play`, `v_public_track_record_model_daily`) had no anon SELECT. Then `anon_lockdown_step_a_2026_11.sql` ran as written and its own guard passed. As anon, `picks`, `model_registry` and `custom_model_picks` are now refused. As authenticated, `picks` still reads 198,138 rows and `model_action_thresholds` 87. From 03:12:35 to 04:06 UTC the edge logs show 551 signed-in REST requests, all 200/204, and no anon REST traffic.

**Broke, then fixed.** Right after step A, anon reading `site_public_record` got `42501: permission denied for view v_discord_published`. The definer view reads `v_public_track_record`, which is `security_invoker`, and an invoker view nested in a definer view still checks the caller. The fix went in at 03:38:24 UTC, so `site_public_record` was refused to anon for about 26 minutes. The edge logs show no request for it in that window, so no visitor was served the error. The fix: the view now reads a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, `site_public_record_rows()`, owned by postgres with a fixed search_path. It returns the same ten columns. Anon needs EXECUTE on it, because Postgres checks a function inside a view against the caller (measured: with EXECUTE revoked in a rolled-back transaction, anon got 42501 on the function). As anon, the view now returns 10 sports.

**Why the tests missed it.** The scratch fixture's `v_public_track_record` was a plain VALUES view with no `security_invoker` and no locked dependency. It now matches production (invoker, reads `push_sent`). The scratch test failed against the old site view and passes with the fix. It now also checks the site record right after step A, not only after B.

**Repo.** `data/anon_lockdown_spec.py` generates the new site SQL, and both migration files were regenerated from it. Byte-identical: site (25 retired ids) and step B. `site_public_record_rows` was added to `ANON_KEEP_FUNCTIONS`, `RPC_ANON_CALLABLE`, step B's read-back and `scripts/lockdown_anon_stray_check.sql`, so step B keeps it.

**Also caused by step A, fixed on master by another session.** `dispatch:apply-view-migrations` failed 6 times, 03:20–04:10 UTC (the 11-20 entry). It has run green every pass since 04:30. At 11:28 UTC anon still cannot read any step A relation or execute any step A function, so the hourly migrations have not re-opened anything.

**Not done.**
- Step B (stats, odds, scoreboard off anon; `tracked_bets` owner policies). The runbook says 24 h after A unless Matt says otherwise. Its pre-apply baseline (97/20/82/67) is the pre-A one, so B needs a fresh snapshot and its own expected counts.
- The premium gate (old steps C and D). With no free tier, any signed-in account without a trial or subscription can still read model data and Stats over REST.

sheet: filter by model, only the sport's models, with how many units each is

both All and Signals; after Market; the new view, yes.

  2026-11-19): `v_public_track_record_daily` split by model. The migration
  reads the daily view's own definition and adds `model_id` to SELECT and
  GROUP BY, so it cannot drift from the record; it rebuilds when the daily
  definition's md5 changes and creates nothing if the edit does not apply.
  SELECT for anon and authenticated, everything else revoked.

  bets over the 7 days ending yesterday ET, cold the mirror),

  the selection impossible.

`v_public_track_record` (59 models; the 60th has 0 picks in both), per
(date, sport) it equals the daily view (0 mismatches). 550 rows in all, at
most 19 on one day. `verify_model_heat.ts` and the new model checks in
`verify_pick_categories.ts` pass, and each was watched failing with the cut
or the bet floor removed. `tsc` error set byte-identical to master (26, none
in touched files).

the selection's overlap with the listed models, and none when that is all or
none of them (`effectiveModelCut`), so ticking every listed model no longer
hides the All board's paused rows with no pill. The migration drops and
recreates the view inside a fail-soft block, so a later column change in the
daily view cannot raise and stop the pass; watched failing ("cannot drop
columns from view") on a real Postgres with the old CREATE OR REPLACE. The
view is authenticated-only (master's anon-key lockdown prep takes anon off
the record views); production revoked to match.
## 2026-11-19 — The PC backup copies only what changed each night, checks everything once a month, and shows its schedule on the dashboard; the other four odds indexes rebuilt (#1493, #1478)

**Asked.** mike: *"run the other four indexes now. how is this going have we migrated everything? and why is the nightly backup so big, we should just be running a diff and a full backup like once a month, ensure the backup schedule and runs are captured in the retool app"*.

**The four odds indexes** (19:14 to 19:50 ET, `REINDEX INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, one at a time).
- The disk allowance's latest daily point read 99% before the first index and before the largest.
- Results: `idx_odds_source_inplay` 0.30 to 0.14 GB, `idx_odds_game` 0.86 to 0.46, `idx_odds_date` 0.89 to 0.46, `idx_odds_book_snap` 6.70 to 4.30.
- The table's indexes went from 10.02 to 6.64 GB, freeing 3.38 GB. Database plus write-ahead log went from 81.13 to 77.82 GB. All 5 indexes are valid.
- During it, the worker ran 238 pipeline steps, all successful, 26 of them odds writes (15,135 rows). There were 0 app errors and 0 app reads.
- #1478 is closed; the result is on #1345.

**Migration status** (`archive_batches`, 2026-11-19 evening).
- Only the prop repeats have moved: 49,532,968 rows in 2,666 batches, games 2026-05-13 to 2026-11-12, all removed by 11-17 17:59 UTC.
- Nothing else has moved. The game odds repeats are blocked by their readers, and rows older than 90 days by the opener/close table (`docs/data_archive.md` section 8). The database is 79.22 GiB.

**Why the backup was big.** The night of 11-19 pulled 35,347,926 rows. 29.8M of them were whole copies:
- 16.8M from the 312 tables under 2M rows;
- 13.0M from the two latest-price tables.

**Changed** (`scripts/local_mirror.py`).
- `--kind auto` picks the run's kind.
- **The nightly diff:** every keyed table catches up, whatever its size, and an unchanged table copies nothing. The two latest-price tables wait for the monthly full.
- **The monthly full:** the first pass on or after the 1st of each month, Eastern time. It fingerprints every chunk against Supabase, reads back every file, and copies again only what differs. It records `LAST_FULL.json`.
- **The report** (`scripts/local_mirror_report.py`) now writes `copy_kind`, and `schedule`, read from Task Scheduler. The migration adds both columns.
- **The Retool PC copy section** gains a Next backup card and a Backup runs table.
- **The GitHub copy** retries a cut-off download. On 11-18 and 11-19, two of the four repositories' issue export stopped on `IncompleteRead`, and the report counted 0 failed.

**Verified.**
- 14 new tests. Each was watched failing on the old code.
- On the real copy:
  - A diff of 8 tables copied 289,295 rows in 2 min 18 s. The night before, those tables were copied whole: 6,395,137 rows. All counts matched afterwards.
  - The full on `injuries` (2,074,172 rows) checked every chunk and file in 47 s and copied the 18,817 new rows.
  - The report read the real task: daily at 00:30, plus 2 minutes after sign-in.

**Not done.**
- No whole pass has run with the new code yet. Tonight's 00:30 pass is the first, and it runs the full.
- The dashboard change could not be type-checked here, because there is no TypeScript on this PC.
## 2026-11-19 — EuroLeague: one bet a game, moneyline first

**Asked.** mike, after the first card run under the 2026-11-17 cuts still put three bets on ZAL and on MIL: *"I dont understand what was done, still betting 3 bets on the same game?"*, then *"i need all options on the table in clearly explained english"*. Ten options for the three side models and five with the other two models were replayed on the same games and the posted stake. He chose *"Strict, moneyline first"*.

**Built.** `models.euroleague_pregame.one_bet_a_game`: a game carries one EuroLeague bet across all five models. After every other gate, the moneyline keeps it, else the spread, else the first half, else the first-quarter total. The halftime spread bets only a game with no pre-game bet. A standing bet keeps its game whatever its order. VOID and paused rows keep none. The card (`scripts/euroleague_pregame_card.py`) applies it in `pick_rows`, so a moneyline a gate refused leaves the game to the spread. It applies it again in `publish` under the locks, on `standing_bets` (all five models; was `standing_full_game`, two). The halftime loop declines with the same reason (`models/euroleague_live_loop.standing_bets`). A dropped bet is a NONE row at its side and price. Its reason, `one bet a game: <label> keeps it`, is on the card's decision row (`euroleague_pregame_decisions.detail`, after `; refused`), not on the pick row; the halftime loop writes it as the reason of a "declined" row in `euroleague_live_decisions`. The artifact, uplifts, EV floors and cuts are unchanged. App copy: the five model pages state the rule. The Spreads and First Half pages say to expect few bets. The 2026-11-18 same-team sentence is gone.

**Verified.** `scripts/euroleague_stacking_check.py --live`, new section 5, through the shipped function: 361 games all five priced, every bet 455, +82.88 units, +18.93%; one bet a game 205, +46.25, +23.78% [10.4, 36.3], 0.95 at risk a game (2.16), deepest drawdown -8.73 (-20.16), no game with two bets. That reproduces the scratch replay mike chose from. The spread and first half keep no game: each of their 125 and 131 replayed bets from 2025-26 on sits beside a moneyline bet. The 2026-11-19 card's rows through the rule: 10 bets written, -1.66 units; kept TEL ML, ZAL ML, MIL ML, -0.60. Tests: 16 new or changed EuroLeague tests failed with the rule switched off (the real-database card tests among them). The write-time recheck has its own race test, which failed with that recheck removed. Two copy tests failed on the old copy. Full suite, 4 workers by file, branch and master at f632370c: the same 27 failures on both, none in a touched file. The 9 branch-only failures each named the copy of master that sat inside the worktree for the comparison, and pass with it removed. The 4 master-only failures (`test_overnight_polling.py`) pass on both trees run alone. The EuroLeague tests, plus the files #1492 changed, also pass on origin/master f6e2c487 with this change applied (178 passed). The app's All board lists no-bet rows, so a dropped bet shows there as a grey NEUTRAL card, as every no-bet row does. The first real run under the rule is tracked in #1495.

**Not done.** Other sports were not measured for same-game stacking; mike's choice was EuroLeague's. Picks already written stand, including 2026-11-19's.

No audience group: 0 app subscribers and 0 Whop members.

The record's totals come from `v_model_health_summary`'s rows, which equal `v_public_track_record` (1,636 bets, +71.11u). Live figures at the build: beat the close 716 of 1,337 measured (615 on price, 101 on the number), matched 340, trailed 281; Pinnacle 275 of 672 beat, -0.67% average; deepest drop -29.64u (2026-09-01 to 09-18), now 4.08u below the 11-15 high; 59 live models, 28 paused, 34 of 58 in profit, 5 of 9 with 50+ bets in profit, 19 beating the close by the verdict and 0 trailing; odds credits 1,396,345 with a 55,159 daily median. Query time from this machine: the slowest read 864 ms including about 270 ms of network round trip.

## 2026-11-19 — NCAAF bets after kickoff: in-play on Model Health, and no pre-game pick in the last 10 minutes

**Asked.** mike: *"The 3 college football bets should be moved to live bets"*, *"leave the 13 missing blank"*, *"merge 1485 / 1483 / 1484"*, and *"out of the ~1600 bets if only 100 moved our way is that bad?"*

**Merged.** #1485 (16:26 UTC; deploy success 16:39) and #1483 (16:31; the session-log overlap with #1485 resolved by keeping both entries). #1484 is the issue, not a pull request; this is its fix.

**Moved to live, on Model Health.** `v_model_health_bets` reads a bet placed at or after its game's first play as `in_play`. Hand-applied it read so (1,180 priced, 288 in-play, 101 line our way, 53 against, 13 no close; the dashboard's resource reads as the table owner and sees the first plays), but the worker re-runs master's file each pass, so it holds only once this merges. Not set as `is_live` on the pick rows: the record views count an in-play bet only from a model whose id carries `_live_` and only with a live-channel lock, so the flag would drop the three (1-2, -1.34 units) from the published record. That is a restatement and needs mike's word; asked.

**The cause.** `config.PREGAME_START_BUFFER_MIN = {"NCAAF": 10}`, read at the pick insert (`models/scorer._drop_pregame_after_cutoff`). The feed listed the start 1.8 to 6.4 minutes after kickoff in 4 of 4 NCAAF games checked; NHL's listed start came 8 to 18 minutes before puck drop in 30 of 30. 4 of 115 published NCAAF pre-game bets fell inside 10 minutes, 3 of them after kickoff. The in-play loop's first stored state landed 1 to 10 minutes after kickoff, so it is no start signal. Tests: the insert-gate file (13) and the Model Health file (12) pass; each new test watched failing with its change removed; 218 scorer, config and Model Health tests pass.

**How many moved our way (the question).** Of the 1,334 measured pre-game bets: 1,180 closed at our number (we beat the close on 615, matched it on 337, trailed it on 228; average +3.34%), 101 saw the line move our way (average 2.05 points) and 53 against (1.28). Against Pinnacle's fair close, on the 661 that have it: 267 beat it (40%), average -0.78%.

## 2026-11-19 — The older bets' closes bought and measured; same-book CLV against Pinnacle's fair close

**Asked.** mike: *"yes"* (buy the older bets' closes), *"yes"* (leave line moves as line moves), and: *"When measuring the closing line, are you just looking at the number, or are you taking into account odds? ... if you bet a game at plus 120 and it's the same number, but the closing line is 115, then that's beating closing line value ... Look into that and understand if it's a best practice."*

**Bought and applied (#1470).** 1,011 game-and-book calls to the feed's history, all ok, 17,380 credits, 27,947 quotes (`clv_close_purchases`, kind `history`); balance after 1,407,929. Two runs died on "server closed the connection unexpectedly" from the session pooler (the database had not restarted, no idle timeouts set); `scripts/clv_close_history.py` now stores a fetched game's quotes again on the reconnected connection instead of dying, and the resumed run finished with no drop. `history --only-bought --apply` on the 1,191 published pre-game bets of games before 2026-11-15: 1,037 priced (899 main line, 138 alternate ladder), 131 whose book no longer offered our number, 23 with no snapshot after the bet; 399 changed value.

**The record after both eras** (`v_model_health_bets`, 1,635 bets): 1,157 priced, 285 in-play (not measured, by design), 87 line moved our way, 42 against, 64 no closing price.

**The 64 found (mike: "how do ~60 have no closing price? find it").** Two bugs and two real causes. Bug 1: the feed's history drops accents ("Jose Soriano", "Walbert Urena", "Eury Perez", "Cristopher Sanchez"), so bought quotes for those pitchers never matched our labels; now matched through `clv_close.player_key`. Bug 2: a prop pick row with no `prop_market` (the single-market prop models) never had its line move recorded, so an Under the book no longer offered at our number (its alternate ladders list Overs only) showed as "no closing price" instead of a line move. `remeasure-none --apply` on every bet measured with no close: 6 newly priced, the moved props now line moves. Left: 32, of which 22 were placed in the last ten minutes before the start, after the book's final pull (13 recent: NCAAB first-half spreads and NHL), 8 whose player or market the book no longer listed at the start (Hard Rock shots on goal, one William Hill game with no quotes), 1 with no main line in the last pull. The closing pull now pulls a game again when a bet lands after its last closing pull.

**The 13 recent late bets bought.** The games from 2026-11-15 had not been bought (their pulls are stored), but our own last pull came before these bets. The feed's history keeps a snapshot every 5 minutes (at :55:36 before a :00 start), which lands after a bet placed at :50. Bought with `buy` aimed at those 13 game-and-book pairs (one-off target list; same purchase code): 13 ok, 31 quotes, 166 credits, balance 1,405,032. `remeasure-none --apply`: 10 newly priced (9 NCAAB first-half spreads, 1 NHL saves prop); the 3 NHL moneyline bets placed at :56 still have none, the snapshot predates them. The 9 older late bets' bought snapshot also predates the bet. Now (`v_model_health_bets`): 1,173 priced, 102 line moved our way, 53 against, 22 no closing price, 285 in-play. The 22: 12 placed after the last price anyone stored or sells, 8 Hard Rock and William Hill shots-on-goal props whose player the book stopped listing, 1 NFL prop with no Fliff pull after the bet, 1 NBA prop whose FanDuel last pull did not carry our number.

**Price, not just the number.** `clv_pct = (taken decimal / close decimal - 1) x 100` at the same book and the same number: +120 taken, +115 close = +2.33% (`tracking/clv_math.same_book_clv`, run 2026-11-19). The common practice in the sources is the price against a sharp book's no-vig close (Pinnacle), at the exact number; that is the `market_clv_pct` column. On the 614 priced bets that have both: same-book +3.77% average, 59.3% beat; against Pinnacle's fair close -0.85%, 40.1% beat; correlation 0.78. By model (20+ such bets): `nhl_prop_shots_on_goal` +6.28% (SE 0.83, n 97) and the two market-relative prop cards positive; `nhl_moneyline` -1.61 (0.40, 60), `mlb_prop_pitcher_hits` -3.48 (0.73, 42), `nba_team_total_luck` -3.79 (0.59, 21), most others negative. The Pinnacle column is still the earlier writers' read (its close is not yet required to be pulled after the bet).
