## 2026-11-19 — Anon-key lockdown prepared, not applied

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-18 8:46 PM ET: prep steps A and B with the CPO's defaults. He will OK the production apply. Nothing was applied to project vvprgnrmzeekokzkrkfu.

**What the migrations do.** They follow the CPO spec of 2026-11-18, not a premium gate. Authenticated keeps the grants it has today, except `tracked_bets`.

- `site_public_record_2026_11.sql` creates `site_retired_models` (no anon or authenticated grant) and `site_public_record` (security_invoker off, one row per sport, no model id). The website reads that view.
- Step A revokes anon on 25 model/record/BYOM/ops relations plus `picks_log`, moves 15 policies to `authenticated`, and revokes PUBLIC and anon EXECUTE on 28 functions, then grants those functions to postgres, authenticated and service_role. The matview loses anon and keeps authenticated.
- Step B revokes anon on the other 72 relations and 19 grant-only relations, moves 53 policies to `authenticated`, revokes anon on sequences, and revokes PUBLIC and anon EXECUTE on 52 functions. `tracked_bets.user_id` defaults to `auth.uid()` with owner-only policies. The 135 existing rows stay null and stay visible to the worker.
- Anon keeps `site_public_record`, `log_app_error` and `log_app_timings`.
- None of these files are in `ACTIVE_MIGRATIONS`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_anon_lockdown.py` on a scratch Postgres: anon denied on step A objects while still allowed on games; after B, anon denied everywhere except the record view and the two loggers; eight personas; tracked_bets cross-user, legacy null row, and cascade; undo B then undo A. The manifest tests that this change touches passed (219). The two `test_threshold_review` failures are `ModuleNotFoundError: scipy` in this environment, not this change.

**Review, same day.** The draft stays unmerged. A plain `apply_anon_grants --apply` keeps today's anon grants. `--lockdown-b` is the only sweep that revokes the rest, and it refuses until `tracked_bets.user_id` exists and no anon policy remains. Worker migrations that re-grant anon now do so only while anon can still read `picks`. Step B also revokes default privileges on new tables, sequences, and PUBLIC execute; undo B puts those three back. `scripts/restore_acl_from_snapshot.py` diffs `ops.acl_snapshot` and prints or applies the GRANT, REVOKE and ALTER POLICY statements. A NULL ACL is `acldefault` for the owner. Sequences use lowercase `s` (`rwU`), not uppercase `S` (a foreign server, USAGE only). PUBLIC EXECUTE on new functions is revoked globally, because a per-schema revoke does not remove the built-in grant. The PR pytest subset runs `tests/test_anon_lockdown.py` and fails if that file skips. Matt ran the default-privilege query read-only on 2026-11-18. No global rows. In `public`, postgres grants anon only on sequences. Functions and tables go to postgres and `service_role`. `supabase_admin` grants anon, authenticated and `service_role` on sequences, functions and tables in `public`. `storage` (postgres) and `graphql` / `graphql_public` (`supabase_admin`) also grant anon. Those three schemas are not altered. A non-member cannot change `supabase_admin`'s defaults (`permission denied to change default privileges`, measured on scratch Postgres) and cannot revoke grants on a table that role owns. Step B tries the revoke and continues with a notice when it is not a member. `scripts/lockdown_anon_stray_check.sql` raises on any other public object anon can use. `--lockdown-b` revokes that grant when it owns the object and fails the run when it does not. A REVOKE by a role that holds the privilege without grant option returns success and the warning "no privileges could be revoked"; the sweep re-checks the privilege and names the owner instead of logging a revoke. The same pass includes partitioned and foreign tables. The stray-check keep names are asserted equal to `ANON_KEEP_RELATIONS` / `ANON_KEEP_FUNCTIONS` and to `ANON_READABLE` / `RPC_ANON_CALLABLE`. Every `CREATE FUNCTION` in this repo is in `public`. Step B's global PUBLIC revoke is paired with a per-schema PUBLIC grant on every other schema that exists at apply time, so a function postgres creates outside `public` keeps PUBLIC execute. A pre-step snapshot has no `postgres:f:global` row. The restore grants PUBLIC execute back when that live row exists and the snapshot does not name it. Undo B does the same grant. A missing per-schema default-privilege row is an empty list. Legacy tracked bets are filled when one account owns the device, and `untrack_tracked_bet` deletes a null row otherwise. The website falls back to `v_public_track_record` until `site_public_record` exists. The three app hooks refetch when sign-in changes. Account deletion calls `clear_my_tracked_bets`, which removes a legacy null `user_id` row when no other account used the device. If another account has used that device, the null row is left. No owner can delete it through the policy. A schema created after step B does not get that per-schema grant. It needs `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA <name> GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTIONS TO PUBLIC`. The snapshot restore leaves the per-schema PUBLIC grants step B added. They repeat the restored global default. Undo B's per-schema REVOKE would also remove a PUBLIC function default that already existed outside `public`. Before the prod apply, check the pre-A snapshot for a per-schema `=X` function default outside `public`. If one exists, roll back with the snapshot restore, not undo B.

**Runbook SQL.** `lockdown_acl_snapshot.sql` casts `defaclobjtype` to text. `text || "char"` is 42725 on Postgres 17.6 and 18.6. The object is still `role:type` or `role:type:global`, which `_split_defacl` splits. `lockdown_preapply_check.sql` calls `has_sequence_privilege` only inside `CASE WHEN relkind = 'S'`. With the filter alone, Postgres calls it on a table first and raises 42809. The same CASE wraps the table-privilege scans in that file, in step B's post-apply check, and in `lockdown_anon_stray_check.sql`. Step A's check names relations. The undo files do not call `has_*_privilege`. `test_lockdown_runbook_sql_runs_on_scratch_postgres` runs the snapshot, the pre-apply check, step A, and undo A on a scratch cluster. pytest-subset already starts that cluster from `initdb`. A service container would not put `initdb` on PATH, so no new job was added.

**Deliberately not done.** Merged `master` at `ba6e66e3` (notifications inbox) so the branch is not behind. No production apply, no merge of this PR, no EAS, no OTA, no threshold or pick-math change. Steps C, D and the MCP stay held. `supabase_admin` default privileges that still grant anon cannot be altered by postgres. Sequence object grants are not in the hand undo; the snapshot restore is that path. After undo B, run the plain grant sweep if the 2026-09-03 table default (anon revoked on new tables) should stay, because undo B grants that default back.

## 2026-11-19 — A failed push opt-out does not block the login delete

**Asked.** Chief Engineer re-review 5968597046 on #1473 at 2d717735. One Medium, and Lows 10 and 11.

**Changed.** `continueAccountDeletion` logs a failed push opt-out and still calls `delete_my_login`. The opt-out is outside the registration queue, and it gives up after 5 seconds so a hung token write cannot hold the login delete. `deletionBanner` returns the success sentence only when the login delete succeeded. A failed login delete, including an opt-out failure that happened first, returns the sign-in-still-active sentence. `pushWritesClosed` stops a registration that was already waiting on the permission prompt from enabling the token afterwards. The delete-account order check reads the calls, so a comment that names `delete_my_login` does not count.

**Verified.** `verify_delete_account.ts` (banner cases plus the opt-out does not return before the login RPC). `verify_push.ts` (deletion calls `unregisterForPushNow`, and a closed registration does not write enabled).

**Not done.** No SQL change. The migration is not applied. The PR stays a draft.

## 2026-11-19 — Account delete opts out before the login is gone

**Asked.** Chief Engineer re-review 5968536183 on #1473 at a1f59152. One High, one Low.

**Changed.** Account deletion calls `forget_my_notifications` and the push opt-out before `delete_my_login`. `save_device_push_token` stores a null user when `auth.uid()` is no longer in `auth.users`, so a save that still runs after the login is gone does not fail the foreign key. Turning notifications off is queued behind an in-flight registration, so the last write matches the toggle.

**Verified.** Scratch test: the delete sequence succeeds and the token is disabled with a null user. `verify_push.ts` checks the opt-out runs after a registration that was already in flight. `verify_delete_account.ts` checks the opt-out and the forget sit before the login delete.

**Not done.** The migration is not applied. The PR stays a draft.

## 2026-11-19 — Inbox review: the token owner is the session

**Asked.** Chief Engineer review 5968413613 on #1473. Both Highs and all four Mediums.

**Changed.** `save_device_push_token` stores `auth.uid()` and rejects a `p_user_id` that is not that session, so anon cannot attach a token to a user. Sign-in and sign-out re-save the token; a signed-out save stores a null user. The bell does not clear the badge while auth is still loading, and it reloads when the user id changes, when a push arrives, and when the app becomes active. Tapping a phone notification marks that inbox row read. `forget_my_notifications()` deletes the caller’s inbox and sport prefs and unlinks their tokens; account deletion calls it. Inbox ids are deterministic (`uuid5` of user, kind, and lock keys, or pick and price, or the reply id) and the row is inserted with the `push_sent` ledger after the send, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_notification_inbox.py` (mute fan-out, live, line change, feedback id, unread count, conflict insert, scratch RLS plus the anon and mismatched-user refusals). `verify_push.ts` 70, `verify_notification_inbox.ts` 30, `verify_a11y.ts` 86. `tsc` added no errors in the touched files.

## 2026-11-19 — Notifications inbox (Option C)

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-18: Option C. Keep 7 days. Show all alert types. One inbox for all sports. Started picks stay openable. One row per alert. Bell on every tab. Clear phone alerts and use the app badge. Per-sport on/off, all 12 sports, gold switches. Muted sports are hidden from the inbox and the badge. A new server table is OK.

The push producers filter muted sports before the title is built, skip the push and the inbox row when nothing remains, and stamp `data.inboxId` plus a `badge` count. ## 2026-11-19 — Closing-line value: one final close for every pre-game bet, a closing pull, and the older bets' closes bought

**Asked.** mike, on the 2026-11-18 findings: *"don't need clv for in play bets
ensure that is mentioned"*; *"yes I want it to be accurate please correct, so
will the norm exclusion be in play bets ? If no we need to correct all others"*;
*"Merge 1470,1471,1472"*. Branch `claude/clv-final-close`, issue #1470. Not a
model update: no cut, stake, grade or pause changed.

**The rule** (`tracking/clv_close.py`, `docs/clv.md` "The final close"): the
bet book's quote at our exact number in its last pull before the start, pulled
after the bet; the main line, or the alternate ladder when the main line moved;
otherwise no closing price (NULL, never 0.00). "Pulled" is the row's
`created_at`, not the book's `snapshot_at`. It runs in the settle step after
the two earlier writers, once per bet, and keeps what the row held before in
`clv_close_measurements`.

**Measured.**
- The live feed's events list is free and one game's event odds cost one credit
  per market returned (NFL spread and its ladder: 2, x-requests-last,
  2026-11-19). The history: an events list is 1 credit, one game at one book 10
  per market returned (the first 10 games: 241 credits; 15 of their 16 bets
  priced, snapshots 20 seconds to 4 minutes before the start).
- Applied to the 145 published pre-game bets on games 2026-11-15..18, from
  stored rows: 111 priced (13 from the alternate ladder), 24 whose book no
  longer priced our number at its last pull, 10 with no pull between the bet
  and the start. 21 of the 145 changed value.
- The 1,191 published pre-game bets on games before 2026-11-15 need the
  history: 1,011 game-and-book calls, about 24,000 credits at the measured
  rate (1,441,236 left). The full run was refused by the session's permission
  check, so it waits on mike.

**Built.** The rule; the settle-step stage; `data/ingestors/clv_closing_pull.py`
and its every-minute worker job `clv_closing_pull` (the bet books, markets and
ladders four minutes before each start, into `clv_close_quotes`);
`scripts/clv_close_history.py` (plan, buy, measure); three worker-only tables
(`data/migrations/clv_close_2026_11_19.sql`, applied to production 2026-11-19,
RLS on, app roles revoked, on the PC copy because it lists tables from the
catalog); the Model Health page says in-play bets are not measured, by design.

**Verified.** `tests/test_clv_close.py` (14): the pure rule and the real reads
and write on a scratch Postgres; seven deliberate breaks, six caught first
time, the seventh (filtering on the book's change time) caught by a sharper
break. The registry tests pass one file at a time;
`tests/test_anon_readable.py` was run in a clean checkout because the main
folder holds private repo copies under `.claude/` that the scan reads.

## 2026-11-19 — Signals Slip and Track pills: the icon and label were 4pt high

**Asked.** Matt, 2026-11-18: the Swept (+ Slip) and Track bubbles on the
Picks > Signals card are off center. Check the other icon+label pills too.
Alignment only.

**Measured.** His screenshot (`slip-track-offcenter.png`, 1206×2622, 3×).
Both pills are 132px = 44pt tall. Ink center versus the pill center:

| | ink | dy |
|---|---|---|
| + Slip, the plus | 12.3–21.7pt | −5.0pt |
| + Slip, the word (the p reaches lower) | 12.3–24.0pt | −3.8pt |
| Track, the bell | 10.3–24.0pt | −4.8pt |
| Track, the word | 12.3–21.7pt | −5.0pt |
| Best FD −115, the label | — | +0.2pt |

Horizontal on those two pills is within half a point. The Best control is a
row with `alignItems: 'center'`, which is why it already looks even.

**Cause.** `btnTall` is `minHeight: 44` with 8pt vertical padding and a 1pt
border, so the content box is 26pt. The icon+label row is ~18pt. The
Pressable is a column and starts at the top, so the spare ~8pt sits under
the row and the row's center is 4pt above the pill's. The bell and the plus
are centered in their own boxes (Ionicons advance width; bell ink 0.5px left
of its box on the web render). Not a glyph baseline and not Android font
padding — the screenshot is an iPhone.

**Changed.** `alignItems: 'center'` and `justifyContent: 'center'` on
`btnTall` in `AddToPlayButton` and `TrackButton`. Same 44pt, same padding,
same words. `verify_pick_card.ts` now requires both properties inside
`btnTall`. Compact and detail pills size to their content and were already
even; left alone. Same for the Best control, filter chips, the Filters
buttons, Sharp Score, the signal badge, PrimaryButton, the betslip bar, the
Bet all sheet, and the Settings / Discord tiles — those rows already center.

**Verified.** After render at 3×, 44pt: Slip plus dy 0.0pt, Track bell dy
0.0pt, Track word dy −0.2pt. Slip's word ink center is +1.4pt because the
p's descender hangs below a centered line box. tsc error set unchanged.
`verify_pick_card.ts` passes, including the new center check (it fails if
either property is removed).

## 2026-11-18 — Closing-line value: why 622 of 1,595 published bets had no number, and a Model Health tab

**Asked.** mike: *"Is this being measured correctly? Why do we have nearly 1500
bets and clv not measured for nearly half?"* and *"need a rolling clv tracking
per model in 25 bet increments for last 500 bets and 50 and 100 bet
increments ... a dedicated page in the retool app called model health"*.
Branch `claude/model-health-clv`. Not a model update: no cut, stake, grade or
pause changed.

**Measured (production, read-only, 2026-11-18/19).** The published record
(`v_public_track_record`, from 2026-09-01) holds 1,595 graded bets; 973 carry
a closing-line number. The other 622, by cause (`v_model_health_bets.status`,
which matches the hand query exactly):

| Cause | Bets | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| In-play | 274 | No pre-game close exists; excluded by the standard (`docs/clv.md`). |
| The line moved off our number | 300 | The book's main line left the number we bet before the start, so there is no closing price for our exact bet. 190 moved our way, 110 against. 227 game lines, 73 props. |
| Not measured | 42 | All `ncaab_first_half_spread`. The card bets on the tip-minus-10 pull, which was the last pull, and the close must be a pull after the bet. |
| No closing quote | 6 | Stamped, nothing found. |

The line moves are concentrated: `ncaaf_over_under` 81 of 106 bets,
`nfl_wind_totals` 29 of 38, `ncaab_spread_market` 22 of 28,
`nfl_opener_spread` 19 of 21. Those models have almost no CLV average at all.

**Found, three ways a number was wrong or missing.**
- **The Retool Picks & CLV tab called every line move "Worse"**, including the
  190 that moved our way (`getPickLedger`: any non-zero `line_clv_pts` was
  `WORSE`). Its "CLV beat rate" was beats over everything measured, flat closes
  included: 470 / 1,273 = 37%. The record's own rate, beats over beats plus
  misses, is 470 / 634 = 74%; with line moves counted by direction it is
  660 / 934 = 71%. Fixed in the dashboard (below).
- **NCAAB first-half spreads had nothing to close against** (42 bets).
  Fixed going forward: a tip-minus-3 pull (`CLOSE_MARK`), retried every minute
  like a decision pull. The card cannot decide on it: it needs a pull that
  landed 5 to 10.5 minutes before tip. One more call per game (at most 4
  credits).
- **A close can be older than the bet.** For game lines and props the capture
  takes the bet book's newest pre-start row, with no condition that it was
  fetched after the bet. On games from 2026-11-15 (after the odds prune
  stopped, so the table still holds every row): 4 of 73 measured bets had no
  row fetched after the bet, 3 stored as 0.00 and one as +2.70 (NHL moneyline
  bets placed 4 to 8 minutes before the start, against a last quote 10 minutes
  before it). Older games cannot be checked: the prune removed later rows.
  Not fixed here: both writers would change (`tracking/paper_tracker.py` and
  `clv_standard_v2_2026_10_14.sql`), and re-stamping stored rows moves the
  published CLV line. Issue opened.

**Built.**
- `data/migrations/model_health_2026_11_18.sql`: `v_model_health_bets` (one
  row per published bet, with its status and verdict), `v_model_health_windows`
  (blocks of 25, 50 and 100 over each model's newest 500 bets) and
  `v_model_health_summary` (one row per model: all bets, newest 25, 50, 100).
  The bets are the record's: the filter is read out of
  `v_public_track_record`'s own definition when the file runs, and the views
  carry its hash, so a change to the record's filter rebuilds them on the next
  pass. Applied to production 2026-11-19 (two passes, the second a catalog
  read): 1,595 bets and 973 closing prices in both the record and the summary,
  all 58 models equal on bets, measured count, beats, pushes, average CLV,
  Pinnacle count and units (the one record row with no graded bet,
  `nfl_prop_rush_yards`, has no health row). anon and authenticated hold
  nothing on the three.
- The ops dashboard's **Model Health** tab (Retool): what each bet's close is
  measured against, every model (average CLV with its 95% margin, newest
  25 / 50 / 100, beat rate with and without line moves, against Pinnacle, units,
  a verdict and a last-50-against-prior-50 trend), the selected model's blocks,
  and a rolling 25 / 50 / 100 chart with its 95% band. Its arrangement is for
  the session only, like the Database tab: the saved-layout table's check does
  not allow a new tab yet.
- Picks & CLV tab: line moves are their own two outcomes, and the ledger reads
  its bets from `v_model_health_bets`, so it no longer shows the one
  `ufc_total_rounds` bet the record leaves out.

**Verified.** `tests/test_model_health.py`, 10 tests on a scratch Postgres
(the migration through `_apply_one`, the worker's path), with production's
record filter verbatim. Eight rules broken one at a time, each caught: the
record filter dropped, the push rule changed, a line move against counted as a
beat, the filter's hash left out of the mark, a REVOKE removed, newest-first
reversed, a retired stamp's number carried, the 500 cap removed. NCAAB
first-half: the tip-minus-3 tests, and the close retry removed is caught.
`tests/test_ncaab_first_half_odds.py` has 4 failures that fail identically on
master (the fetch-run tests); not touched here.

**Not done.** No in-play measure (no pre-game close exists for those 274
bets); no conversion of a line move into a price (the record keeps price
closes only, and the health page shows line moves beside it).

## 2026-11-18 — NBA reassessment: threes overs are no longer bet, and the luck totals wait for 10 games again

**Asked.** mike, 2026-11-18: *"NBA is bleeding money and we've made no changes
to improve the models or find other edge, I don't care what you have to do,
just do it"*, and that a week of work had produced no short summary of what
was tried. Both changes are model updates (`Updated-By: mike`).

research files under `data/local/research/nba_research`, not in git.)
- The live record, settled NBA bets 2026-10-20 to 11-17: 221 bets, 100-121,
  -9.63 units. By model: `nba_prop_projection` 44-65, -4.74; `nba_prop_market`
  5-10, -4.75; `nba_team_total_luck` 16-18, -4.07; `nba_total_luck` 15-15,
  -1.40; `nba_first_half_total_luck` 12-7, +4.06; `nba_live_spread_q12` 7-6,
  +0.27; `nba_q1_trend` 1-0, +1.00.

  books (`scripts/card_variants_2026_11_18.py`, re-run today): 287 card bets,
  -23.08 units, -12.6%; the card without them +209.60 units against +190.26
  (+19.34, 95% interval -19.58 to +60.75). Off the card +0.93% on 6,828 bets,
  interval -1.5% to +3.4% (`scripts/proj_by_market_7books.py`, re-run today).
  Live: 26 of the model's 109 settled bets, 9-17, -0.64 units, median price
  +158.5.
- The wait. 50 of the three luck models' first 56 settled bets had a team
  under 10 games: 21-29, -11.10 units (`reassess/live_diagnosis.md`). Every
  team had 10 games before 2026-11-12 (fewest 10 of 30 teams); from then to
  11-17 the three went 15-6, +8.38 units.

**Changed.**
- `models/nba_prop_projection.py`: the threes cell bets unders only, so a
  threes over is never offered. `CARD_AS_GRADED` keeps the card as shipped on
  2026-10-16 for the three closed searches that replayed it
  (`scripts/nba_s_postseason.py`, `nba_s_propoffline.py`, `nba_s_q1props.py`).
  No artifact refitted; the percentile table in `models/nba_prop_rank.py` is
  unchanged, because the replay ranked with it.
- `models/nba_total_luck.py`: `LIVE_MIN_GAMES` back to 10 (it was 1 from
  2026-11-02). The home team total and first-half models import it. A held
  game is a recorded decision, `early_season`. The closed first-quarter
  team-total search keeps the one game it was graded with
  (`scripts/nba_s_q1tt.py`, `B_MIN_GAMES = 1`).
- Docs: `docs/nba_reassessment_2026_11.md` (new: the whole reassessment in

## 2026-11-18 — The odds table: a rewrite frees nothing yet, its duplicates have readers, and the prop move cost 4,647 "At pick" prices

**Asked.** mike (2026-11-18): "now do the odds table the same way" as the props table.

**Measured.**
- **The table file is full.** `odds` is 13.21 GB of data and 10.50 GB of indexes, with 59.5M live rows and 107,448 dead.
  - A 0.2% block sample holds 34.2 rows a page, against 34 for a packed page at its 228-byte average row.
  - So a `pg_repack` rewrite today would give back about nothing. Props shrank because 49.5M rows had left first.
- **Duplicates.** On the PC copy, 28,198,332 of 59,301,354 rows (47.6%) are exact repeats of games over 3 days old. 23,828,919 of them fall outside the four readers the 11-16 audit named.
- **Readers.** A fresh audit of every reader of `odds` found more that need those rows. In production these are the app's line-history card ("At pick" within 6 hours) and the NCAAB first-half CLV close. There are also the NCAAB spread and moneyline builds, the NFL cache import, the NCAAB history replay, the NCAAF derivative buyer, the re-buy planner, and about eight research scripts.
  - Protecting them in whole groups (every game with a pick, all NCAAB and NBA pre-game, and others) leaves 1,279,329 movable repeats (2.2%). A finer list would free more, not measured.
  - 596,699 of those are NFL cache-import rows, which the keep list now holds.
- **The props move (11-16 to 11-17) cost "At pick" prices.**
  - The same app rule reads `player_prop_odds`. Of 77,435 pre-game prop picks on archived games, 74,913 had a row at the pick's book within 6 hours before the pick. Now 70,266 do, so up to 4,647 cards lost the "At pick" price. Five sampled picks were replayed with the app's own read on live Supabase, and all five showed it.
  - The prop CLV close reads the newest row before the start, and is unaffected.
- **Parity.** The new keep list picks the same rows in Postgres as on the copy (count and fingerprint) on 10 games of MLB, NBA, NCAAF, NFL and NHL, among them games holding paid in-play, NHL paid-history and NFL cache-import rows.
- **Index sizes, 09:33 ET:** `idx_odds_book_snap` 6.62, `odds_pkey` 1.85, `idx_odds_date` 0.88, `idx_odds_game` 0.85, `idx_odds_source_inplay` 0.30 GB. All are valid.
  - The size estimator matched the rebuilt props primary key exactly (0.81 GB). It overstates non-unique indexes 1.7 to 6.7 times, so only the primary key's 0.6 GB is a firm figure.

**Changed.**
- `scripts/archive_rows.py`:
  - `_ODDS_KEEP` holds the game-odds rows a reader needs whole. The odds move stays in `REFUSED`, with the audit's readers as the reason.
  - Prop moves are refused too (`REFUSED`), because they took the app's "At pick" price (#1458). Nothing may move today.
  - `prepare_range` (the `bulk` step) prepares every date from one pass of the copy. It never rewrites a date already in Storage.
  - `--batch-rows` removes small games together, each still checked on its own. It has never run against Postgres.
- `data/ingestors/odds_rebuy.py`: the game, extra-market and in-play plans count archived game-odds rows (`odds_history`), as the prop plan already did.
- `docs/data_archive.md`: the audit, the prop "At pick" count, and the note that a rewrite of `odds` returns nothing yet.
- `#1454` (the props rewrite record) merged at 08:47 ET. The worker and pollers deploys reached SUCCESS.

**Tests.** 64 pass in `tests/test_archive_rows.py` and `tests/test_odds_rebuy.py`. Four deliberate breaks of the new code failed the new tests: deleting a game Postgres disagrees on, rewriting a stored date, no batching, and the re-buy planner without the archive.

**Not done.**
- No game-odds row moved; the table was not rewritten.
- The `odds` indexes are rebuilt overnight, 02:28 ET on 11-19 (`REINDEX CONCURRENTLY`, one at a time, outside the refresh pass).
- The "At pick" loss on prop cards (#1458) is fixed in the app by #1460. The archive tool now refuses any further prop move until that fix is live.

## 2026-11-18 — pytest-subset: refresh_pass no longer bare-waits

Master pytest-subset job 134975785320 (run 44353641273, commit 2218fc8, Ubuntu 26.04, bash 5.3.9) failed one test: `tests/test_deploy_drain.py::test_refresh_sigterm_finishes_the_open_group_and_marks_deploy_cutoff`. `par_wait` called a bare `wait`. On bash 5.3.9 that builtin loops inside `wait_for_background_pids` once a job pid is not a child of the shell, printing `wait: pid N is not a child of this shell` (bash53-010 fixes it; 5.3.9 does not have that patch). The deploy-drain test's 30s wait then expired, and the log was the spam. `par_wait` now waits only the pids `par` recorded, and retries one of those only while it is still this shell's child. The pass still runs the same steps, still collects the failure markers, and a SIGTERM still finishes the open group before later steps are skipped. Each parallel step also drops the inherited EXIT/TERM traps and job table so the subshell cannot wait on the parent's jobs.

`tests/test_yesterday_discord_units.py::test_the_unpriced_pnl_verifier_passes` passes on this master. PR #1293 retired `ufc_moneyline`, `ufc_total_rounds` and `ufc_method_of_victory` and shipped `ufc_open_moneyline`. They are not paused. `verify_paused_catalog.ts` expects UFC to be moneyline only, written by `ufc_open_moneyline`. The unpriced-PnL harness (a6e271c4) lifts that retirement and the record strike for its own sample, so the daily recap in that sample still has the UFC row. No fixture change.

Local: the workflow's pytest-subset command, 2880 passed, 16 skipped, and the push-token re-run passed. mobile-tsc's tsc baseline check and the nine verify scripts it runs passed, including the UFC catalog checks.

## 2026-11-18 — The props table rewritten online: 26.05 GB of data down to 10.73 GB, 37.19 million rows checked

**Asked.** mike (2026-11-17): rebuild the props table (`player_prop_odds`) to give back the space left by the archived duplicate rows. He agreed to a temporary Railway helper using the worker's database login, and to the start staying at 2:28 am.

**Measured.**
- **Start check, 02:27 ET:** no other session held the table, no vacuum was running on it, and no transaction was older than 60 s. The dashboard's disk speed allowance for Nov 18 read 99%.
- **Run:** `pg_repack` 1.5.2 ran from 02:29:34 to 02:55:18 ET and exited cleanly. The copy took about 10 minutes and the 5 indexes about 16.
- **Space:** the table's data went from 26.05 to 10.73 GB, and its indexes from 4.37 to 4.33 GB. The database went from 93.34 to 78.01 GB. The dashboard read 81.55 GB used of 135 at 03:21.
- **Guard:** database plus log peaked at 112.14 GB, against a cancel line of 118. It polled 57 times. There were no app errors and no failed app reads in any 5-minute window.
- **Rows:** a count and checksum of the rows up to ID 121,946,715 matched across three runs (14:59 and 17:49 on 11-17, and after the swap): 37,193,430 rows, checksum -24846386042104179470461.
  - The 1.15 million rows written after the 17:49 baseline (IDs up to 123,097,605) were not checksummed before the rewrite.
- **Not tested:** no prop rows arrived during the run. The last ID stayed at 123,097,605 from 02:28 to 03:17, so rows landing mid-copy were not exercised.
- **Table setup:** triggers, the anon read rule, permissions, constraints and all 5 indexes were the same as just before the run.
- **New rows:** the 03:15 refresh pass wrote 21,218 rows. All 21,218 reached `latest_prop_odds`.
- **Helper:** the Railway service `pg-repack` was deleted at about 02:58.

**Changed.**
- `docs/data_archive.md`: the "space comes back only after a rewrite, not yet measured" section now holds the measured run and how to repeat it.
- The compute line there now carries the dashboard's reading (Medium, 120 connections).
- New `scripts/pg_repack_helper.sh`: the helper's start script. It pins the client to the database's 1.5.2 and builds it from source when apt has another version.
- New `scripts/pg_repack_guard.py`: the disk guard. It cancels from SQL above a set size and never writes a row. Two read-only polls ran from the repo copy at 03:23.

**Before the night (11-17), on the 5,333-row `nfl_live_prop_snapshots`:**
- A dry run.
- A real rewrite: same count and checksum, new storage file.
- Two aborts from SQL, one by hand and one by the guard with its line set to zero. `pg_repack` removed its own trigger and copy tables both times.
- A 1.5.3 client refused to run against the 1.5.2 extension. That is why the version is pinned.

**Not done.** `archive_rows.py reclaim` (`VACUUM FULL`) was not used and is unchanged. #1345 stays open for the rest of the archive design.

## 2026-11-17 — The full test suite no longer hangs on Windows (#1445)

**Asked.** Fix #1445: a full `python -m pytest -q tests/` run on this PC never
finished.

**Measured.**
- `tests/test_nhl_stats_views.py::test_the_views_run_as_anon_on_a_scratch_database_and_windows_skip_nulls`
  hung for the whole four-minute limit when run alone on master (4eb63419).
- The cause: it ran `pg_ctl ... start` with `capture_output=True`, and on
  Windows the server inherits that pipe and never closes it. Until #1430
  (2026-11-16) the test could not find a server here and skipped itself.
- `tests/byom_scratch.py` had the same start. With the server on PATH,
  master's helper hung at the 90-second limit on
  `tests/test_byom_facts_schema.py`. Folders it left on 2026-11-11, 11-13 and
  11-14 are still in `%TEMP%`, and one still has a server running.
- That helper's own finder only looked on PATH and in the Debian layout. On
  this PC, where only the embedded-postgres wheel is installed, 88 tests that
  ask it skipped. With the server findable, all 237 tests in the 22 files
  that use the helper passed (746 s).

**Changed.**
- The NHL views test and the BYOM helper now start their database through
  `tests._pg.scratch_cluster`, and the BYOM helper uses `tests._pg.pg_bin`.
  The NHL test passes here in 14 s, and the BYOM schema file in 11 s.
- New guard, `tests/test_scratch_pg_start.py`. It fails if any file under
  `tests/` other than `tests/_pg.py` writes out a `pg_ctl ... start` command,
  or if `tests/_pg.py`'s own start stops sending its output to a file. On
  master's files it fails and names exactly the two starters (byom_scratch.py
  line 52, test_nhl_stats_views.py line 189). It reads files as `utf-8-sig`,
  so a file saved with a byte-order mark is not an error (watched failing
  with plain `utf-8`). It is added to the PR check's file list, because a
  captured start passes on Linux.
- `tests/_pg.py` retries the folder delete for up to about five seconds. A
  single delete left `log` and `pg_ctl.out` in three folders on 2026-11-17.
  That the server still holds them is inferred, not measured, and the
  leftover did not recur in 35 tries, so the retry is tested only against a
  stand-in delete.

**Full suite on this PC** (`python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -rfE
tests/`, the fix before master was merged in, 2026-11-17). It finished in
2:48:34 while six other runs shared the PC: 19 failed, 13,280 passed, 56
skipped. Master's tree that morning: 20 failed, 13,185 passed, 144 skipped,
with the NHL test's database stopped by hand so the run could end. The 19
are exactly master's 20 minus the NHL test. The 95 extra passes are the 88
BYOM tests, the NHL test and the guard's six; the 88 fewer skips are the
BYOM tests. After master (#1432) was merged in, the guard, the NHL test and
the files #1432 touched passed (115), and so did the PR check.

**Reviewed.** Three independent reviewers (Linux PR check, cleanup paths,
the guard), each finding checked by a separate skeptic. Kept: the guard was
not in the PR check, the byte-order mark, the leftover files. Rejected as
not defects: start commands built from pieces (no file has ever done that,
`git log -G pg_ctl`), and `tests/_pg.py` being exempt by name (now checked
anyway). On the Linux side the reviewer found no caller that reads the old
`.data` folder or parses the connection string, and a `-m fast` stop with an
open transaction returned in 2.19 s.

**Not done.** The leftover folders from 2026-11-11 to 11-14 are not removed
here, and the server still running in one of them is not stopped: none of
them came from this session's runs.

## 2026-11-17 — The 19 tests master failed in a full run (#1447)

**Asked.** A session tidying the project folder ran the full suite on master
`0eb85c49` and found 19 failures, the same 19 on its own branch (#1447). The
full local run is the only gate for most of the suite (§5), so every pull
request was being compared against a red baseline. Started as a side session
of that one.

**Found.** All 19 were tests, or their harness, that had stopped matching
code that was right. No app, model, pick or publishing code changed. Four
causes:

- **Code changed on purpose and a text-matching test did not follow (8).**
  Daily steps moved into the deploy-drain `try:` (#1186, 2026-11-05), so the
  health-check and model-quality tests looked for a 4-space line that is now
  8. The betslip counts only the legs still in the parlay (`pricedLegs`,
  #1115). The NFL prop card's insert grew past the test's fixed
  1,000-character window (the `decision_*` columns). The CLV backfill scan's
  NCAAB first-half "is there a close yet" check names its book as the pick's
  own bet book, written in SQL rather than as a `%s` (#1167); it picks the
  same book `_bet_price_and_book` does. `_post_picks` takes `conn=` (#1379)
  and the NBA test's stub did not. The Stats legend names the season through
  the shared `teamSeasonLabel` (#1194). The player page's opening stat moved
  into `mostFilledChip` (#1241).
- **The calendar (5).** The four NCAAB first-half fetch tests pin a tip-off
  of 2026-11-11 00:00Z while the fetch re-reads the real clock before each
  call, so from that date every fixture game read as tipped and nothing was
  bought. They have been red since 2026-11-10 23:59:30Z (tip minus the
30-second last-call lead). The session-start
  outstanding test wrote its cache at a fixed 2026-11-13 14:00Z, which the
  real clock calls over 24 hours old from 2026-11-14.
- **This PC (4).** Git here checks files out with Windows line endings, and
  the Back-button verify script matched a plain newline. The site's record
  math test called a bare `node`; this PC has only the pip package's node
  (`tests/_node.py`). Windows cannot deliver a catchable SIGTERM
  (`send_signal(SIGTERM)` is TerminateProcess), and the Linux PR CI already
  runs that test (master run 44166042023: 2,868 passed, 0 failed, not among
  its 16 skips). `.env` is ignored by git, so it exists on the main checkout
  and in no worktree.
- **Test order (2).** `test_service_role_split.py` ends by reloading
  `scheduler` as the poller service and never put it back, so every later
  file that built the scheduler saw only the poller's jobs. Both social
  tests pass alone and failed after it (reproduced, then fixed).

**Changed.** Tests only, plus the verify script's reader:
`tests/test_{health_check_observe,model_quality,market_coverage_probe,
picks_unique_row,multi_book_odds,nba_prop_projection,mobile_board_season,
mobile_player_stat_groups,ncaab_first_half_odds,outstanding,service_role_split,
web_site,deploy_drain,claude_rules_split}.py` and
`mobile/scripts/verify_stack_back.ts`. The NCAAB tests freeze the module's
clock at the fixture's `now`; the role-split file reloads `scheduler` after
each test; the rules test lets a path through only when `.gitignore` names it
line for line; the season test now also runs `teamSeasonLabel` under node.

**Verified.** Full runs on this PC, with #1445's hanging
test deselected. A copy of master at `4eb63419` (`git worktree add
--detach`) had 20 failed, 13,184 passed and 144 skipped. This branch at
`04fe39ae` had 0 failed, 13,204 passed and 145 skipped. Every master
failure is gone on the branch, and the branch has no failure master lacks.
The extra skip is the SIGTERM test on Windows. The extra test is the new
season-formatter test, which ran. The 20th master failure is not one of
the issue's 19: `test_monitoring.py::test_exit_waits_a_bounded_time_for_a_database_that_never_answers`.
That run shared the PC with the branch run (3h27m and 3h02m, against
the 1h11m runs that found #1447). The test's 20-second limit covers the
child's start-up as well as its exit. Alone it passed 3 of 3 on each tree;
timed, the child took 2.25 to 2.66 s, of which the exit was 0.61 s. The
cause is not established, and the test is not changed here (#1453). Each check rewritten here was watched failing: the code it
guards was broken on purpose (19 breakages), the test went red, and the file
was restored byte for byte. The role-split reset was seen failing before the
fix and passing after. master then moved by #1432 (the folder clean-up,
which touches none of these files) and was merged in. A full run of the
merged branch at `706b2b1a`, with nothing else running, had 0 failed,
13,204 passed and 145 skipped (1h13m), the monitoring test included. The
pull request's Linux CI had 2,868 passed and 16 skipped, the same as
master's last run. Neither the record-math test nor the SIGTERM test was
among the skips, so both ran and passed there.

**Judgement calls.** Skipping the SIGTERM test on Windows, and letting the
rules test pass a path `.gitignore` names exactly (today only `.env`), are
both choices someone may want reversed. The health-check and model-quality
tests still pin the exact indent, so a re-indent of the daily body will trip
them again; that keeps "unconditional" in the check.

## 2026-11-17 — X posts the day's best four picks, each with a rationale reply

mike, 2026-11-17: post picks to @SignalBasePicks, one text post each, no
graphic, and reply under the post with a short rationale. The same day he
narrowed it: not every pick, the 3–4 best of the day. Live picks stay off X.

**Why posting had stopped.** The last real `x_pick` rows are 2026-11-10.
`push_sent` also has `kind = 'x_pick'`, `lock_key =
PAUSE:x_pick_alerts:approved-by-mike-2026-11-10`, `sent_at =
2099-01-01T00:00:00-05:00`, `message_id` null. The gap clock takes the
newest `sent_at`. That stamp is in the future, so `now - last` never
reached 10 minutes and every later pick was held. Railway `worker` has
the four `X_*` keys. It does not set `RUN_X_PUBLISHER` or
`X_POST_ALL_PICKS` (both default on). `pollers` has no X keys. No code
change and no row delete were required to name the cause. The clock now
skips a `PAUSE:` key and any stamp more than a day ahead, so the row can
stay. Nothing was deleted.

**What posts.** At most 4 pre-game picks per ET date, from the same
released set Discord uses. Rank is `decision_edge` (else `edge`), then
expected value at the price taken, then model probability. One per game.
The best of each sport not already on the card is taken before a second
from one sport. One tweet per call. The 10-minute gap and the 1:00–8:00am
ET quiet window stay, and both are waived when holding would push the
tweet to or past the game's start. A started game is not posted and not
ledgered. The tweet says "All picks are in the free Discord and the stat
sheet." with no URL; the reply carries that sentence only when the tweet
cannot. The reply is 2–4 sentences from stored probability, the price's
implied probability, the stored edge, EV from those two numbers, and
stored public, stake, or injury text. A link or a hashtag in a stored
clause is left out. No number is filled in. The reply is ledgered as
`x_pick_reply` only after a confirmed id. A failed reply does not resend
the pick. A retry uses `message_id` and only for a pick tweet from the
last 36 hours, so the Nov 10 posts are not replied to now.

**Verified.** `tests/test_x_publisher.py`, the X cases in
`tests/test_pick_integrity.py`, and `tests/test_free_pick_names_the_cheaper_book.py`.
The 2099 row was read from production. Railway variable names were read
on `worker` and `pollers`; values were not.

**Not done.** The pause row was not updated or removed. No Railway
variable change. Live picks stay off X: the live loops run on `pollers`,
which has no X credentials, and a live price is not the pre-game card.
Thresholds, stakes, and pause state were not changed.

`execute_sql` and `apply_migration` on both Supabase prefixes.
`tests/test_claude_settings_allowlist.py` fails on those four entries.

## 2026-11-17 — Header buttons 4pt low in the iOS glass bubble: 36pt header items

**Reported.** Matt (screenshot, Pick Detail, iOS 27.3): the Back arrow is not
centered. The #1424 chevron-only fix was already running on that phone
(`app_timings`, device on iOS 27.3, update `01a1e6d2-…-c98a11836adb` =
the #1424 OTA, 00:13Z), so a rebuild would not have changed it.

**Measured (3x pixels, 1206 x 2622).** Bubble 52 x 44pt, center y 251.5px;
chevron center y 263px: 11.5px = 3.8pt low. Horizontal: 1px. The title's
center (251px) matches the bubble's, so the bubble is placed right and the
content is low. The Ionicons chevron glyph is centered in its own box to
0.01pt (font metrics, @expo/vector-icons 15.1.1), so the glyph is not it.

**Cause (source, not device).** react-native-screens 4.16.0 (Expo SDK 54's
pin) gives `UIBarButtonItem initWithCustomView:` our header view directly.
On iOS 26+ UIKit sizes that custom view itself, and 4.16 leaves our content at
its origin. Upstream added a centering wrapper with hugging and compression
resistance (#3449, #3548; read in 4.23.0's `RNSScreenStackHeaderSubview.mm`,
absent from 4.16.0's). The numbers fit UIKit giving the view a 36pt-tall slot
with the bubble 4pt outside it: (44 − 36) / 2 = 4pt low, 44 + 8 = 52 wide. That
36 is inferred from the screenshot, not read from UIKit.

**Changed.** `stackBack.HEADER_ITEM_HEIGHT = 36`: the Back pill (every pushed
screen), BYOM Builder "Cancel" and BYOM Detail "Edit" are 36pt tall, with
a declared hitSlop. Chevron fixed at 22pt; Back, Cancel and Edit show the
Large Content Viewer; Cancel/Edit text stops growing at accessibility Medium
(above it the text spilled out of 36pt). Title width still reserves 44pt
(36 + 4 a side). UX review (ship with fixes) found, from RNS 4.16 source:
header taps are forwarded only inside the bar and only to the first
left/right view, so the Back target is ~52 wide x bar height and BYOM "Edit"
(second view on its screen) is likely a 36pt-tall target. Accepted until RNS
4.23; noted in UX_REVIEW.md §4.
`verify_stack_back.ts` gained the 36pt checks; watched them fail on
origin/master.

**Verified.** `verify_stack_back.ts` and `verify_settings_home.ts` ALL PASS;
`tests/test_mobile_back_button.py` + paused-catalog + context-budget 22
passed; tsc error set byte-identical to the unchanged tree (26, none in
touched files); ux_scan 0 findings.

**Not verified.** On a device. The fix is right only if UIKit's slot is 36pt
on every iOS 26+ version; Matt's screenshot after the OTA is the check. The
root-cause fix is react-native-screens >= 4.23 (Expo SDK 55's pin), which is a
native build.

becomes a circle); VoiceOver label still "Back". One component, so it covers

the word (player / Settings titles: 162 → 254pt at 390pt, 1x).
`verify_stack_back.ts` gained chevron-only / centred / width checks; watched
the two shape checks fail against master's button.

## 2026-11-17 — PR CI `pytest-subset` red on every PR: the Postgres finder was pinned to 16

**Symptom.** Every PR's `pytest-subset` failed with 1 failure and 5 errors,
each "initdb is missing in CI", in `test_nhl_stats_views` and
`test_clv_pinnacle_mainline`. Seen on #1426 and #1424 (16 Nov).

**Cause (measured from the run log).** The workflow's install step reported
`postgres provisioned via apt` and `initdb: /usr/lib/postgresql/18/bin/initdb`
(18.6). The runner's apt now ships Postgres 18. `tests/_pg.py` looked only in
`/usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin`, and `test_nhl_stats_views` kept its own list
that stopped at 17. The workflow's own check scans every version, so it
passed while the tests could not find the binary.

**Fixed.** `tests/_pg.py` now searches `/usr/lib/postgresql/<major>/bin`,
newest major first by number. `test_nhl_stats_views` uses that shared finder.
New `tests/test_pg_finder.py` (added to the CI list) tests the search on a
fake 18 layout and fails if any test file hard-codes a major. All four of its
tests fail on the old code.

**Verified.** CI's whole `pytest-subset` list plus the new file, run as a
non-root user with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=1`: 2906 passed, 15 skipped (mobile node
modules were absent in that copy), 0 failed. initdb refuses to run as root,
which is why the sandbox has to use a separate user.

## 2026-11-16 — Dark mode, and a Settings > Appearance control (System / Light / Dark)

**Asked.** Matt: add a dark mode toggle in Settings and build a dark mode. Matt
approved, in this order: a 3-way System / Light / Dark control; **Light by
default (opt in)**; a **brand-navy** dark palette; and the theme applying at
the **next app open**. The first proposal was a reload on toggle, but
`Updates.reloadAsync` is banned (lib/otaUpdate.ts, the 2026-10-19 TestFlight
crash), so that was dropped.

**Why not live.** 126 StyleSheets read `colors` once, at import. A live switch
would mean rewriting each of them to read the theme on render.

**Built.**
- `mobile/index.tsx` is the new entry point (`package.json` main). It reads
  `appearance.v1` from AsyncStorage, sets the boot scheme, then `require`s App,
  so `theme.ts` picks its palette before any StyleSheet is built. A 1.5 s
  timeout falls back to Light so the app always opens. An explicit Light or
  Dark also sets `Appearance.setColorScheme`, so native alerts, pickers and the
  keyboard match.
- `lib/theme.ts`: `lightColors` (unchanged values) and `darkColors`. Dark uses
  bg #0B1220, cards #16213A, and the amber brand colour as tint. `textInverse`
  now means content on a tint fill, so it is near-black in Dark.
- New tokens, each the same in both themes: `onChrome` / `onChromeSecondary`
  for text on the navy brand chrome (BetslipBar, Paywall, SignIn, SignalLockCard,
  the DeleteAccount toast, the Settings manage button, the Scoreboard team
  discs) and `onBright` for text on bright fills (the DraftKings chips and
  hand-off, the SportToggle count, the player hit-rate badge). In Light,
  `onChrome` equals the old `textInverse` and `onBright` equals the old
  `textPrimary`, so Light renders the same as before.
- The Scoreboard empty-state and lock buttons now use `tint` instead of
  `brandNavy` (#0B1320 vs #0B1220 in Light). On the navy Dark background they
  would otherwise have disappeared.
- Settings: an Appearance tile and drill-in with three radio rows. The footnote
  says "Close and reopen Signalbase to switch to Dark." while a change is
  pending, and the tile reads "· reopen to apply".
- `App.tsx`: the navigation theme is based on DarkTheme in Dark, and the status
  bar style follows the scheme.

**Verified.** `verify_contrast_tokens.ts` now measures every Dark text token on
its Dark backgrounds (≥4.5:1). It also checks that the grade ramp's lightness
still falls good → mid → bad (0.499 / 0.476 / 0.334) and that the bright fills
did not change: ALL PASS. The new `verify_appearance.ts` and
`tests/test_mobile_appearance.py` were checked by mutation. Moving

## 2026-11-16 — Why the women's models trail the men's; in-play bets away from DraftKings' number were never stored

**Asked.** mike: *"Why isn't the ncaabw models as successful as the ncaab?
What are we missing"*.

**Found.** The full measurement is `docs/sports/ncaabw.md`, "Why the women's
record trails the men's". In short: four fifths of the men's units are the two
first-half models, and no book in the feed prices a women's first half;
DraftKings prices about one women's game in ten (69 of 701, men's 519 of 681);
the totals model at other books' first totals does not clear; this season's
books hang halftime totals closer to the model (the replay bets 25.5% of
2026-27's games against 60-64% of the stored seasons', whichever season the
model is fitted on). And a bug: the men's and women's in-play loops wrote
`dk_implied_prob` NULL for a bet at a book whose number DraftKings did not
hang, `picks` refuses that, and the loops logged "write failed (non-fatal)":
20 of the men's 24 in-play bets (game dates 2026-11-09 to 11-14) and 13 of the
women's 22 halftime bets (11-11 to 11-15) were never stored or posted. Graded
on the finals: men's 14-6, +6.93 units; women's 6-7, -1.69.

**What changed.**
- `models/ncaabw_live_loop.py`, `models/euroleague_live_loop.py`
  (`build_pick`): with no DraftKings quote at the bet's number,
  `dk_implied_prob` is 0.0, as the first-half cards and the NBA loop already
  write; `dk_odds` stays NULL, `edge` 0.0, `line_book` names the book. The
  EuroLeague loop had the same line and no failure yet. **The men's loop
  (`models/ncaab_live_loop.py`) is left as it is**: mike, 2026-11-16, *"I do
  not want anything changed in the mens model that would change the model or
  picks, it's been winning"*. It was fixed in the branch's first commit and
  taken back out.
- `tests/test_inplay_pick_without_draftkings.py`: each fixed loop's row has
  every NOT NULL column of production `picks` filled when DraftKings is absent
  or at another number (seen failing on the old code), and no
  `models/*live*.py` writes `"dk_implied_prob": None` except the men's loop,
  named in `HELD`.
- `scripts/ncaabw_research/totals_other_books.py`,
  `scripts/ncaabw_research/ht_model_vs_market.py`: the two research runs
  behind the doc's tables, print-only, on the local cache.
- Docs: the section above in `docs/sports/ncaabw.md`, two rows of its market
  grid, a paragraph in `docs/sports/ncaab.md`, "In play".

**Verified.** The halftime build replays its pinned numbers (689/398/291/
+66.59 and 669/398/271/+89.96 for 2025-26, 303 and 298 for 2024-25). The
other-books script reproduces `totals_b` on DraftKings' games to the bet
(402 bets, 225-177, +29.96 units in 2025-26). The feed's DraftKings halftime
total matched DraftKings' own board on all 11 games both covered.

**Not done.** The 33 bets the loops decided and never stored are not
inserted into `picks` (mike's call, #1406: it adds settled bets to each
model's own record). The resolver on DraftKings' men's board missed 10 games on 11-14/15
("Nicholls State", "Saint Bonaventure", "Queens NC", "CSU Bakersfield" and six
more); tracked as #1404, not fixed here. The halftime re-check is #1405.

## 2026-11-16 — Comped accounts could not sign in: NULL `email_change` (OTP 500)

**Reported.** McCarthy (mccarthy040@aol.com) could not get an email code; the
app showed a database error.

**Cause (measured).** Supabase auth logs: `POST /otp` → 500, `error finding
user: sql: Scan error on column index 8, name "email_change": converting NULL
to string is unsupported` (14:47 and 15:05 UTC). The hand-written
`INSERT INTO auth.users` in `scripts/grant_*_comped_*.sql` set five token
columns to `''` but not `email_change`, which has no default. Query of
`auth.users` for any NULL token column returned 9 rows, all pre-created comp
accounts (2026-11-12, -14, -16), none ever signed in; only `email_change` was
NULL.

**Fixed.** `UPDATE auth.users SET email_change = '' WHERE email_change IS
NULL` — 9 rows. Both grant scripts now set `email_change`. New
`tests/test_auth_user_insert_columns.py` fails on any `INSERT INTO auth.users`
missing a scanned token column (watched it fail against the unfixed scripts).

**Not done.** Did not trigger a real OTP for McCarthy (it would email him);
he needs to retry.

## 2026-11-16 — Sport chips: sports with a signal move to the far left

**Asked.** Matt (screenshot of Picks → Signals): any sport with a signal should
move to the left of the sport chip row, dynamically.

**Done.** `orderSportChips` (`mobile/src/lib/loadState.ts`) puts every sport
with a signal count > 0 first, then the rest; SPORTS order is kept within each
group, so the row only changes when a sport gains or loses its last signal,
never because one count passed another. `SportToggle` renders that order;
the Models screen passes no counts, so its row is unchanged. After the UX
designer review: PicksHome withholds counts until today's board is known (no
draw-then-reshuffle on first load), the scroll-to-active runs once on first
layout only, and a reorder scrolls the row back to the start.

**Verified.** New checks in `scripts/verify_error_states.ts` (ordering, zero =
no signal, unknown = SPORTS order, the wiring); the wiring check FAILED against
the old SportToggle. `verify_a11y.ts` slop regex updated to `ordered.length`
(86/86). tsc: 0 diagnostics not in baseline. pytest `-k "mobile or
context_budget or sport"`: failure set identical to master (6 pre-existing).

**Not done.** Sports with only a live in-play pick (red dot, no badge) do not
move forward — left for Matt to decide. No `LayoutAnimation` on reorder.
## 2026-11-16 — The archive move stays off Supabase in the daytime (#1345, #1419)

**What happened.** The prepare pass for the prop repeats ran 09:00-13:50 ET.
Besides writing each date's file from the PC copy, it picked every game again
in Postgres. Two things went wrong.
- **It slowed the app.** The project logged 178 statement timeouts in those
  hours, 79 of them reads from the app. In the 18 hours before there were at
  most 5 an hour, and there were none from 18:56 to 19:11 UTC, after it stopped
  (Supabase `postgres_logs`, by hour and by application).
- **It stopped on 2026-09-10.** Three games in one statement ran past 300 s.
  psycopg2 makes a timeout an `OperationalError`, so the run took it for a
  dropped connection and read the same date twice more before it gave up.

**Measured.** 256 MB of shared buffers and a 60-connection limit for a
101.75 GB database. The 60-connection limit belongs only to Supabase's two
smallest sizes (0.5 and 1 GB). One game's rows came off disk at 650-2,300 rows
a second: 45,000 rows in 70 s, 340,000 in 148 s. The PC copy was also reading
`player_prop_odds` all day: it started at 00:49 ET and was 15% through at
13:41 ET.

**What changed** (`scripts/archive_rows.py`, on #1419):
- Preparing reads the PC copy only. The Postgres check is opt-in
  (`--check`); the removal makes the same check inside its own transaction.
- A statement timeout is never retried as a dropped connection.
- Removals run only from 01:30 to 05:30 ET. Inside that window they pause for
  the :17 refresh pass and while more than 6 other statements run.
- A game that runs past 900 s is noted in the ledger and tried again the next
  night. A game Postgres disagrees on is noted and refused until it is
  prepared again.
- `remove --pending` works through every stored date.
- A file over 40 MB goes to Storage in parts. Storage refused the
  2026-09-13 (70.5 MB) and 2026-09-20 (52.9 MB) files whole with HTTP 413.
  With the key in place, all 166 dates are stored and read back: 168
  objects, 473.4 MB.
- `reclaim` (`VACUUM FULL`) is marked unmeasured: it locks the props table
  for the whole rewrite, and no rewrite of 40 GB has been timed here. The
  docs no longer say it fits 03:00-05:00.

**Verified.** The 6 new tests fail on the previous version and pass on this
one; the archive tests are 17 of 17. Up to 2026-09-09, 1,572 games were
prepared with the Postgres check and 0 were refused.

**Not done.** Nothing has been removed: the upload needs
`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in mike's `.env`. The rewrite is not scheduled.

## 2026-11-16 — The copy on mike's PC, recorded nightly and shown on the Database tab

**Asked.** mike (in a question dialog, relayed by the session that started
this one): "locals" is the copy on his PC (`Documents\betting_local_copies`),
and he wants the PC copy section: the last nightly copy's time and duration,
the tables that do not match Supabase, each copy's size over time, and the
free disk, from one small step at the end of the nightly copy that writes one
row to Supabase ("Yes, my PC copy").

**What changed.**
- `scripts/local_mirror_report.py` (new): reads only the copy's own files and
  writes one row to `public.pc_copy_snapshots`. Insert only: a pass reported
  twice keeps its first row. It imports nothing from the repo, because the
  task runs it with `python -I` from `bin\`.
- `scripts/local_mirror_run.ps1`: runs it last, after every copy has ended,
  with each step's exit code, and first with `--catch-up` for a previous pass
  whose runner stopped before its last step; refreshes it from
  `origin/master` like the rest. The task's own result fails on any non-zero
  step code, crashes included.
- `data/migrations/pc_copy_snapshots_2026_11_16.sql` (new): the table, RLS on,
  PUBLIC, anon and authenticated revoked. Its skip reads the access lists, so
  a right a role only inherits cannot make every pass re-run it (the database
  health file's gap, #1410).
- The Database tab: a PC copy tile (five cards, the differing tables, each
  copy's size over time) through a new read-only function, `getPcCopy`.
- `bin\local_mirror_run.ps1` and `bin\local_mirror_report.py` on the PC were
  replaced by hand (the wrapper is never refreshed from master); the old
  wrapper is kept as `local_mirror_run.ps1.before_report_20261116`.

**Found on the way.**
- **An exit code the wrapper cannot read stopped the report and passed as
  success.** With the GitHub step still running, `$gh.ExitCode` was `$null`:
  `Start-Process` refused the report's arguments, and `[Math]::Max` read the
  null as 0, so the task exited 0 (measured with the wait removed). Unknown
  codes are now 255.
- **The GitHub copy failed on the 00:30 pass of 2026-11-16**: `gh auth token`
  timed out after 30 seconds (`logs\github_20261116T054324Z.err`). It had
  succeeded every night from 2026-11-09 to 2026-11-15. Flagged; #1411 checks
  the next pass.
- **Nightly copies take 7 to 9 hours since 2026-11-12** (00:30 to 09:05,
  09:33, 09:35, 07:50), against about 75 minutes before. The pass that started
  00:30 ET on 2026-11-16 was still running at 13:30 UTC. Flagged only.
- `STATE.json` is rewritten by any run, a one-table run by hand included (one
  ran at 02:35 UTC on 2026-11-16), so the row keeps how many tables the run
  covered and the tile's nightly card reads only nightly rows.
- **The wrapper running the 2026-11-16 pass ended about nine hours in** (Last
  Run Result 0x8007042B, "the process terminated unexpectedly") while its copy
  kept running. Why is not known: Task Scheduler's history is off. Under the
  new wrapper such a pass would leave no row, hence the catch-up.
- **An independent review of the first commit confirmed 18 findings.** Fixed
  here: a failed write printed the driver's message (host, port, user, and a
  malformed `DATABASE_URL` whole); a one-table run by hand could pass for the
  night's copy (a pass's copy must now cover 90% of the tables on disk); a
  crashed step's negative code counted as success in the task's result; the
  runner that dies before reporting (the catch-up); the tile's gaps; two
  missing migration tests. The rest, and two task settings for mike (the
  task's 12-hour limit, its history), are #1418.

**Verified.**
- Tests: `tests/test_local_mirror_report.py` (36, including the real nightly
  script run in PowerShell with stand-in scripts) and
  `tests/test_local_mirror_report_pg.py` (17, on a throwaway Postgres with
  Supabase's default grants). With the copy's other tests, the Retool byte
  check and the size budget: 134 passed under the connection guard (its only
  refusals were the tests' own malformed addresses). Every new behaviour's
  test failed on the previous code, and 20 deliberate breaks each failed a
  test.
- The migration was applied to production with the worker's own helper at
  13:15 UTC, and a second apply returned on the marker. Grants: only postgres
  and service_role. `get_advisors(security)` lists the table only as "RLS
  enabled, no policy" (INFO); the other five notices are unchanged.
- Row 1, written by hand at 13:16 UTC and read back: 316 tables and
  258,582,043 rows on disk, 11,083,908,744 bytes, 531,451,416,576 bytes free
  of 999,012,954,112. One table differs: `latest_odds`, 44 rows at the
  09:31 UTC check of a table that changes all day.
- Published 13:32 UTC (tag `rr-publish-1794835927360-5f021e8`), then again
  at 15:43 UTC with the review's tile fixes (`rr-publish-1794843829257-ceaa439`;
  the import before it reported TS2532 in the tile, fixed before publishing).
  The live app and the repo hold the same 81 files, byte for byte. The tile
  loaded live with no console errors: the GitHub card orange ("None has
  finished since Nov 15, 05:34 UTC"), `latest_odds` orange.

**Not done here.** The first nightly row comes from the pass after the one
running now (#1411).

## 2026-11-16 — The archive move starts: prop repeats leave Supabase for mike's PC (#1345)

**Asked.** mike: *"move all the historical data past a certain point to my
laptop and just keep what was needed in Supabase"*; the exact repeats too, kept
in the archive on his computer.

**Measured.** The database is 101.75 GB. 66.0% of prop rows and 53.8% of
game-odds rows repeat the row before; a 90-day cutoff alone moves about 30 GB.
The PC copy and Postgres give the same fingerprint for the same rows (199,991
`picks_log` rows; and 3 NBA games of 2026-11-08, 8,735 / 9,329 / 6,316 rows).

**Built.** `scripts/archive_rows.py`: prepare (file from the PC copy, checked
against Postgres per game), upload (Storage, read back), remove (in one
transaction, only when Postgres picks the same rows), run (every date),
reclaim (`VACUUM FULL`). Only prop repeats may move: the reader audit found
production readers of game-odds repeats and of every row older than 90 days
(docs/data_archive.md, section 8). The re-buy planner now counts archived rows.

**Verified.** 2026-11-08: 958,454 prop repeats in 23 games, all 23 matching
Postgres, file 5.95 MB. `tests/test_archive_rows.py` (10) and the planner test
fail when the opener, close, rung, age, gate or archive counting is removed.

**Not done.** No row has left Supabase: the Storage copy needs
`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` in the PC's `.env`.

## 2026-11-16 — Stats boards: one price column on every board, Anytime TD included

**Asked.** Matt: the Rush Yards and Anytime TD boards put the line in
different places. Make the UI the same, and check every sport for the same
problem.

**What was found.** One board was different: NFL Anytime TD
(`isAnytimeTdBoard`, NFL + `rush_rec_tds`). It showed HIT % → MATCHUP →
BEST, with a grey price pill and the book name under it. Every other prop
board in every sport (NFL, NCAAF, NBA, WNBA, MLB, NHL and the rest) showed
HIT → book/weekday → MATCHUP, with the book badge inside the pill and
DraftKings filled.

**Decided (Matt, in chat).** The standard layout everywhere. DraftKings stays
green on Anytime TD too.

**What changed.** `StatsScreen.tsx`: the Anytime TD header and its three row
types use the standard order and the standard `OddsCell`. The price header is
the book label and is dated the same as the other boards. Anytime TD keeps its
defense-rank `AnytimeMatchupCell` and the MATCHUP sort, now in the last
column. At large text the matchup still gets its own line. The `bestPrice`
branch of `OddsCell`, its styles, `ATD_ODDS_W` and `ATD_COPY.priceHeader` were
removed, because nothing used them anymore. `verify_anytime_matchup.ts`
now checks that there is no `priceHeader`.

in the files this change touched. `verify_anytime_matchup.ts` and
`verify_stat_segments.ts` pass. `ux_scan --changed` found 0 problems. pytest
`-k "mobile or stats or anytime"` gave the same 4 failed and 3 errors as
master. `verify_a11y.ts` passes all 86 checks. The board was not rendered on a
device.

**UX review (frontend-ux-designer).** Fixed: at large text the standard
pill's 12pt vertical hitSlop reached into the Anytime TD matchup line 2pt
above. A stacked pill now has no vertical slop and is a 44pt box itself
(`oddsWrapStacked`). The stacked price header gets `minWidth: ODDS_W`. Two
stale comments were fixed. Not changed: the price column is now flexible on
Anytime TD (62 to 93pt, the same as every board), so with 2+ books a wide pill
can move HIT % left of its header. This is not measured. It is the same
behaviour as every other board. Matt decided (in chat, 2026-11-16) not to
give Anytime TD its own fixed width. If the drift shows on a device, fix it for
every board at once.
## 2026-11-16 — Signals card: one line-movement row, shown only when the move is against the pick

**Asked.** Matt, from a screenshot (Abilene Christian @ Missouri State, Under
145.5): the card showed two red movement marks, "↓ DK line now 142.5 (−108)"
under the hero and "⚠ Line 145.5 → 142.5" near the bottom. *"We should only
have one … only display it when the line or the price is changing against what
the model picked for that specific sports book."* Keep the bottom spot, use the
line change with the price change in parentheses.

**Why there were two.** The bottom chip (`summarizeMovement`) is the older
movement warning; the top "Now" line (`signalsNowLine`) was added on review of
#965 and toned green/red on 2026-11-15. On a line move both fired.

**What changed.**
- `signalsCard.signalsMoveLine`: "DK line 145.5 → 142.5 (−105 → −108)";
  same price → "(−105)"; price only → "DK line 9 (−110 → −120)";
  moneyline → "DK +120 → +105".
- `PickCard` (Signals layout only): the row shows only when `nowLineTone` is
  `worse` at the deciding book (or, on an "Old price" card, the book the line
  was scored at). A favourable move shows nothing. The Now line under the hero
  and its styles are gone; the extras-row chip is the one row (warning icon,
  `avoidInk`). VoiceOver reads the same text with ", moved against this pick".
  The board layout's movement chip and the books sheet's line note are
  unchanged.

**UX review (frontend-ux-designer): Do not ship → fixed.**
- Blocker, fixed: on an "Old price" card the row is read at the scored book,
  which is not the deciding book; the first build put the deciding book's
  price under the scored book's name. That row is now the line only
  ("DK line 145.5 → 142.5"). The logic moved into a pure
  `signalsCard.signalsMoveRow`, behaviour-tested with the FD-decided /
  DK-scored pair.
- Should fix, taken: the row is pre-game only for a pre-game pick (CLV takes
  over at the start, as the old chip did); live picks keep it.
- Consider, taken: VoiceOver reads the row after the stake, where it sits;
  moneyline reads "DK price +120 → +105"; unused `nowLineToneSpeech` deleted.

**Verified.** `verify_pick_card`, `verify_pick_cta`, `verify_contrast_tokens`
ALL PASS, with behaviour checks on `signalsMoveRow` (the screenshot case,
favourable move, price-only, Old price card, moneyline, away spread). Watched
fail: letting a favourable move through fails two of them. tsc error set identical to master (17, 0 in touched files). Mobile pytest
(-k mobile/signals/...): 613 pass, 3 fail — the same 3 fail on origin/master
(`test_mobile_board_season`, `test_mobile_nhl_skater_stats`,
`test_mobile_player_stat_groups`), pre-existing.


## 2026-11-16 — Scoreboard splits switch to DraftKings' own numbers, with an opening-move arrow

**Asked.** Matt: screenshot of PIT @ CIN on our Scoreboard (PIT +3.5, 49% bets
/ 55% money) beside a competitor's DraftKings splits for the same game, which
read very differently. "The numbers look way off", and he likes the
competitor's arrow when a number is moving. Approved: DraftKings as the
source, the arrow measured against the opening split, a history table.

**Measured.** `scoreboard_splits` for `NFL_2026_10_PIT_CIN`: spreads away
49/55, home 51/45, `line_value` +4/-4, source `action_network`, last written
2026-11-16 01:10Z (kickoff 01:20Z). That is Action Network's consensus
pseudo-book (id 15), not DraftKings. It was measured at +4 while the card
showed DK +3.5, which the app's line-match gate (`splitsFor`) should have
blanked. Why it showed is not established: the row is overwritten each pass,
so the value at screenshot time is gone (an older app bundle is the other
candidate). The same finding was made on 2026-11-03 (CHI @ SEA), and that
session's worker probe (`worker_jobs` 936774) had already measured that DK
Network serves DraftKings' Handle % / Bets % in its server-rendered HTML;
nothing was built on it until now.

**What changed.**
- `data/ingestors/dk_splits.py`: fetches DK Network's splits page per event
  group (NFL 88808 measured; other sports' ids read from the page's own
  links each pass), parses the stripped text (`Selection Odds Handle Bets`,
  then Moneyline / Spread / Total rows), matches teams by nickname (city
  words collide: LA Rams / LA Chargers) and NCAAF through the odds feed's
  school resolver, skips started games, upserts `scoreboard_splits` with
  `source='dk_network'` and keeps the first DK split as `open_*`.
- `data/ingestors/scoreboard_splits.py` (Action Network consensus) no longer
  writes `scoreboard_splits`; it appends to the history only.
- `scoreboard_splits_history` (append-only, worker-only, RLS on, anon
  revoked) gets every split each source returned that differs from the last
  one stored. Migration `scoreboard_splits_dk_history_2026_11_16.sql`.
- App: the read filters `source = 'dk_network'` and selects `open_*`; a %
  cell shows a small neutral ▲/▼ when the split is 3+ points from its
  opening (`SPLIT_MOVE_POINTS`), spoken as ", up" / ", down".

**Verified.** `tests/test_dk_splits.py` (11) on the probe's captured page
text, each guard watched failing under a mutation (handle/bets swapped, city
matching, opening overwritten, history not de-duplicated).
`verify_scoreboard.ts` 85 pass, the arrow checks watched failing. tsc error
set identical to origin/master. Nickname matching is unique for every NFL,
NBA, NHL and MLB team in the odds maps.

**Not done / not measured.** No live fetch: this sandbox cannot reach DK
Network (or Action Network). Whether DK Network serves NBA, NHL, MLB and
NCAAF, their event-group ids, and NCAAF's team-name format are unmeasured;
the first worker pass after deploy returns per-sport parsed / matched /
unmatched counts. Until a sport has DK rows its % columns are dashes.
## 2026-11-16 — A Database tab and the SignalBase redesign of the ops dashboard

**Asked.** mike: *"the UX and UI are very poor - please modernize (super modern
best practice) and brand the design with signalbase"*, and a Database tab
that shows the hourly database health (recorded by the entry below).

**What changed.**
- The dashboard (`retool/models-ops/`): a Database tab (three new read-only
  backend functions over the snapshot tables) and a SignalBase redesign of
  every tab (navy header with the amber stepped-S mark, tab headings, compact
  layout controls, refreshed cards, badges and tables, orange warnings).
  Presentation only: no existing backend query changed.

**Found on the way.**
- Retool's database driver binds `$1, $2…` in the order the placeholders
  appear in the text. Two of the new functions had a join's `$2` before the
  `WHERE`'s `$1` and returned nothing through the Retool connection; fixed
  before any push. No existing function has the pattern (all checked).
- Retool refuses `*.test.ts` files while the app has testing off, so the
  dashboard holds no test file.

**Verified.** Scratch-app import: typecheck clean, generated hooks for the
three functions, 75 files byte-identical, the rest explained; every overview
card matched the stored snapshot in the browser.

**Published** 2026-11-16 12:49 UTC, after mike's "Yes, publish it" (tag
`rr-publish-1794833342231-a352318`). No function approval was asked for.
Read back with no draft named, the live app and `retool/models-ops/` hold
the same 79 files, byte for byte, once Retool's own `package.json` (which
now names the three new functions' database connection) was copied back.
The live app loaded with live data on the Models and Database tabs and no
console errors; the Database tab showed the worker's first hourly snapshot
(12:50 UTC, snapshot 5).

## 2026-11-16 — The odds re-buy covers every missing piece (#1384)

**Asked.** mike, to the pieces the first re-buy plan left out: *"Yes, add
them"*.

**Changed** (`data/ingestors/odds_rebuy.py`, `docs/odds_rebuy.md`).
- **Extra markets**, per game at the refresh moments: MLB first five innings,

  `NFL_<date>_...` where the schedule's id is `NFL_<season>_<week>_...`. It

rows. Every row has its game; ladders keep their rungs; in-play rows are
stamped after the start; `latest_odds` for the slice's games had the same
2,599 rows and hash before and after. Per-piece table: `docs/odds_rebuy.md`.
`tests/test_odds_rebuy.py`: 18 pass, and each new test fails when its fix is
removed (schedule ids, rungs, part-way props, in-play quotes changed before the
