in-play 347,320, props 2,693,801. December, on mike's yes.

## 2026-11-16 — Database health recorded every hour

**Asked.** mike: *"I want to add a section on database health, size and any
other metrics you think are cool and change over time. both locals and
supabase"*. This entry is the recording half; the ops dashboard's Database
tab that reads it lands separately, after mike's OK on the dashboard's
redesign.

**What changed.**
- `data/migrations/db_health_snapshots_2026_11_15.sql`: four insert-only
  tables (`db_health_snapshots`, `db_table_snapshots`, `db_query_snapshots`,
  `db_query_texts`), RLS on with no policy, `anon` / `authenticated`
  revoked, registered in `view_migrations`.
- `tracking/db_health.py` and the worker job `db_health_snapshot` (hourly at
  :50 ET, in `MAINTENANCE_JOBS`, so the morning disk window turns away its
  05:50 and 06:50 ticks, and before noon ET any tick while the daily still
  holds its lock; heartbeat bound 180 minutes, pipeline role).
  Each run: database size split into table, index and toast bytes; row
  estimates; client connections; the oldest open transaction;
  transaction-ID age; unused indexes; Storage per bucket; Postgres's running
  counters, stored as reported. Every six hours: every table's size, and the
  100 statements with the most time plus the 25 slowest per call.

**Found on the way.** `pg_stat_statements`' total time fell between the
first two snapshots with no reset: `dealloc` went from 436 to 437, because
Postgres drops the least-used statements when its 5,000-entry list is full.
So load is read from `pg_stat_database.active_time`, which only rises until
a reset, and idle-in-transaction time is stored beside it. Since the
2026-03-30 reset Postgres has counted 9,459,708,927 ms idle in a
transaction against 2,568,771,649 ms active (read 2026-11-16 04:2x UTC).

**Verified.** Migration applied to production with the worker's own helper
(`view_migrations._apply_one`), a second apply returned on the marker, and
the edited version re-applied once and added its eight columns.
`get_advisors(security)` lists the four tables only as "RLS enabled, no
policy" (INFO), the same as `dashboard_layouts`. Four snapshots taken by hand
and read back (source `manual`): snapshot 1 at 03:41:53 UTC,
109,078,711,443 bytes, 316 tables, 117 statements, 1,352 ms of reads;
snapshots 2–4 wrote no table rows, as the six-hour rule says. Between
snapshots 3 and 4 (7 minutes): 0.333 statements running on average, 1.094
sessions idle in a transaction, 93.85% of reads from memory.
`tests/test_db_health.py`: 15 passed; the no-delete test was watched failing
with a DELETE added to the job.

**Independent review, and what it changed.** Four reviewers each took one
area, and a second agent re-checked every finding. All eight held.
- The oldest open transaction is now aged against `clock_timestamp()`, the
  time of the read. `now()` is when the run's own transaction began, which
  is before `pg_stat_activity` is read. A read-only replay in the job's order
  (80 runs, 09:40 UTC) found a transaction that began after the run's own
  start in 18 runs. Each of those came out negative against `now()` (down to
  -0.43 s) and positive against `clock_timestamp()`.
- The migration's skip check re-checks everything the body sets: RLS on all
  four tables, and no privilege for `anon` or `authenticated` on any table,
  column or the sequence. Before, it re-checked only table-level SELECT, so
  any other drift stayed until the file next changed.
  `tests/test_db_health_pg.py` runs the file through `_apply_one` on a
  scratch Postgres and checks the repair: seven kinds of drift are undone by
  the new file and left in place by the old one. The marker went from
  `0e8c3b995b8d` to `3a59b4f67fe8`, so the file applies once more.
- Every column the snapshot selects now carries the name of the column it
  is inserted into. Two tests hold the select and insert lists together:
  one by name, one by running the real `take_snapshot`. A real-Postgres test
  checks that the live and dead row estimates land in their own columns.
  Before, each of the reviewers' four column-swap edits and the due check
  without its filter passed every test. Each now fails one.
- The schedule test stubs the heartbeat writes. Before, running it from a
  checkout with a `.env` wrote 31 rows to production `job_heartbeats`.
- Two texts said only the 05:50 and 06:50 ticks are lost. Before noon ET, a
  daily that still holds its lock turns ticks away too.

**Production writes from test runs.** Because of that hole, this session's
own test runs wrote heartbeat rows to production: the full-suite run
(04:56–07:24 UTC), a run here at 07:36 UTC, and a reviewer's run at
07:47 UTC. Each row is overwritten by its job's next real run. Whether the
failure alerter posted a false "Resolved" meanwhile is not known, because a
successful ops post is not logged. The same hole exists in older tests: #1396.

**Not done here.** The worker's own first tick happens after this merge
deploys. It is read back then: a `source = 'worker'` row and a heartbeat
stamped after the deploy. Two older problems are tracked instead of fixed:
the suite-wide guard (#1396), and a turned-away morning tick that clears a
job's `error` and posts a false "Resolved" (#1397, which `job_queue` already
has on master).

## 2026-11-16 — Horse racing: the worker stores each new Betfair month

**Asked.** mike: *"Build it"*, the daily job for issue #1371. Betfair
Australia posts one free CSV a month of every Australian and New Zealand
thoroughbred market; the racing audit had stored 2020-01 to 2026-10 by hand.

**Built.** `scripts/horse/betfair_monthly.py`, run daily at 10:43 ET by the
worker (`scheduler.run_betfair_monthly_store`, its own process), and as the
job type `betfair_monthly_store` for an on-demand run. It works by MONTH:
a month is held when any copy of it is stored, in its own folder or a zip's,
and the copy that counts is the one the study reads. A month with no copy is
stored as the original. A held month is re-checked by size every day, and
the two newest are downloaded and hashed; bytes different from the copy
read (a correction, or a return to an earlier version) are stored as a new
version (`<stem>.v<UTC stamp>-<sha 8>.csv`) in the month's own folder, never
over the old file, with a stamp after every stored one. A new annual zip
treats each member as its month by the same rules, keeps any non-month
member under `other/`, and records every member in a `_manifest.json` row.
Each file is written and read back in one transaction, so a failed run
leaves nothing half stored. `anz_study.load` now reads one file per month
(the newest version), and refuses two plain copies of one month.

**Reviewed.** Two reviewers (correctness; production safety) found, all
fixed: a half-stored file was never finished, and a row that failed its
read-back was already committed; a republished month's name was never read
by the study; a future 2026 zip would have stored every 2026 month twice; a
failed run beat "ok" and raised no alert (it now raises, and the job is in
the heartbeat's silence table); the web calls went through a library the
traffic probe does not record (now `requests`); the queued run relabelled
the scheduler's probe (it now calls the store directly). The live run then
caught one more: through `requests`, the server reports the gzipped size
(1,204,719 for a 4,535,977-byte month), so every month looked changed. The
size request now asks for the raw size, and a run where every month looks
changed but none is now fails. A second review confirmed those fixed and
found the folder-by-folder logic could still store one month twice (a
month arriving with or after its year's zip), give two versions one stamp,
raise a false size alarm on a year re-exported through its zip, and miss a
return to an earlier version; the month-by-month rules above close all four.

**Verified.** 27 tests. Seventeen deliberate breaks of the final code,
each caught by a test (among them: a month held only by its own folder, an
identical check against any copy instead of the one read, no stamp bump, a
false size alarm counted against the run's own new rows, a commit per row,
no rollback, no daily hash, no size check, one part's size for a split file,
no no-size guard, no every-size guard, a silent empty listing, no read-back,
a dry run that downloads, the reader keeping the first version, a failed run
not raising). A per-run stamp counter that no break could expose was
removed as redundant. The study reader's output on today's files is unchanged:
1,303,266 rows, 132,750 races, 82 files, the same fingerprint. Against
production, after the fixes: 16 files listed, 82 rows stored, the server's
sizes equal to the stored ones (October 4,535,977 bytes), the two newest
months downloaded and found identical, 0 written. The traffic probe records
the job's calls.

**Not done here.** The first run on the worker itself is a declared job
that runs after the merge; the first new month (2026-11) is expected in
early December.

## 2026-11-16 — Horse racing: does a US outlet still pool with the Hong Kong Jockey Club?

**Asked.** mike: *"whats next"*, after both racing studies merged (#1399).
The one test left needs the Club's odds archive (about US$499), which tests
a bet a US user can place only if a US outlet's Hong Kong bets join the
Club's own pools and are taken in the last minute or two.

**Found** (three searches and a verifier who reopened the sources;
`docs/horse_racing_scout_us_outlets.md`, follow-up). Xpressbet, AmWager and
NYRA Bets offer Hong Kong in 2026; Xpressbet blocks it in Arizona. **Not
established**: that any US outlet pools with the Club after 2023 (the Club
names more than 60 partners in 26 jurisdictions for 2025/26, none by name;
the last named US evidence is from 2014 and 2019), any US charge on top of
the 17.5% take, and how late a US outlet takes the bet (the Club cuts its
own computer bettors off about 15 seconds out). The measured test is a US
outlet's Hong Kong $2 payouts against the Club's dividends; no public US
payout page was found.

**Not done.** No purchase. Nothing in the code changed.

## 2026-11-16 — Horse racing: Hong Kong pools before the off, and UK/Ireland at starting price

**Asked.** mike, 2026-11-15: *"lets use the free hong kong history, and what
do you need from me for uk irel;and"* (licences are never a blocker), then
*"Merge it"* for the data PR.

**Stored first (PR #1387, merged 2026-11-15 22:08 ET).** nch015's Hong Kong
set (6 gzipped CSVs, 40.6 MB) and deltaromeo's UK/Ireland zip (1.12 GB, 34
parts of 32 MiB) into `nfl_research_data_backup`, by
`scripts/horse/store_files.py`. Rows that large timed out the PC mirror, so
`scripts/local_mirror.py` now sizes each page from the rows it just read and
halves down to one row before it fails (`MIN_BATCH_ROWS = 1`; two tests,
both watched failing). The worker and the pollers redeployed at 03:16 and
03:12 UTC; the scheduler's first odds pull and threshold sync ran within 30
seconds.

**Hong Kong before the off** (audit §7; `scripts/horse/hk_preoff_*.py`).
Bets decided on the pool 2 to 33 minutes out and paid at the final dividend:
183 bet sets, none with an interval above zero. 84% of what the pool learns
in its last 30 minutes arrives after the last snapshot a bettor can use,
about 5.6 minutes out. Benter's form model holds a little the pool lacks
5–12 minutes out (`b` +0.14 to +0.21) and none against the close; its bets
lose. A reviewer caught two lead pairs dropped after their results were
seen; they are restored and counted. `tests/test_hk_preoff_data.py` pins
the clock and the snapshot a decision may use (watched failing with a
planted look-ahead).

**UK and Ireland at starting price** (audit §8; `scripts/horse/uk_sp_*.py`).
5,112,965 runners in 506,076 races, 409,872 British and Irish races
gradeable. Market structure: no rule decided before the off was positive on
1988–2004 and then held up in both later halves; every cell that cleared
chose its runner on the SP, so each is an upper bound, and the whole
each-way bet that carries them loses at a flat stake. Fundamentals then SP:
backing a form model's top-rated runner at SP loses in 2011–2026 at every
cut (Britain −4.28% on units with no cut, −0.92% [−2.21, +0.36] at the
strictest); the model's gain over SP has been within ±0.7 per 1000 races
every year since 2022. The 1995–2010 profit is not taken: most of what the
model knows beyond SP comes from earlier-run figures whose timing is unknown. Irish chases where the model
gives its top horse over a 50% chance (872 bets, +5.55% [+0.79, +10.32] on
units) and the same with a 0.2 lead over the next (838 bets) are the two
late cells, out of 96, with an interval above zero; both were found after
looking, and they are a peak whose halves span zero.

**Reviewed.** Two reviewers per study (look-ahead; grading and data). Both
structure reviewers reran the whole script, with identical output. The
fundamentals reviewers reran the bet and sections 0–6, and compared the
leak check and the remove-one-group table with the saved output only. In
each study the grading reviewer re-graded from the raw rows with its own
SP parser: 0 mismatches. Their fixes, applied: the loader's year filter
crashed on the 1988–2004 file (the dropped header rows left `date` as a
category; fixed, `tests/test_uk_sp_data.py`, watched failing); the
fundamentals study now prints all four figure-based picks, every cut in
each era with flat intervals, and race type by era in halves; the
structure study labels the units stake as relying on the SP, counts
positive years from unrounded returns and marks which calibration rows are
in-sample. Then three independent checkers read the write-up against the
outputs (numbers, claims and wording, code): 83 findings, all applied,
including a test assertion that could never fail and two figure-pick
variants mislabelled as "last run". After the last code change the loader checks, the structure study and the
figure picks were rerun from the committed scripts and matched the earlier
runs exactly (the picks under their corrected names); the fundamentals
study's full rerun from the
committed script matched the checked run in all 29 result sections.

**Not done.** No model, no `picks` row, no config. The pool outlets found
do not offer SP; whether bet365 in Colorado or New Jersey takes a bet at SP
was not established.

**What changed.** `completed_day_burn` is yesterday’s counter minus the day before, when the counter did not reset. The page and the “days left” line both use that. A partial morning with a full day under 5 days of runway pages. A billing-period reset does not invent a burn. The 50,000 floor is unchanged.

## 2026-11-16 — Ops digest review fixes

**Asked.** Review on the ops PR: stranded NBA prop bets had gone silent, Monday calibration deltas were a week late, and `aborted` was treated as a clean deploy.

**What changed.** `stranded:nba_prop:*` pages, on the 6-hour repeat. Other failure-alerter WARNs (a start-time gap, a watch that could not read, an undecided prop) are lines in the 7:15 digest. The calibration watch posts again at 9:00 ET, after the 8:30 sweep. `deploy-cutoff` stays out of the clean rate; `aborted` counts again. NBA and college finals page only when two or more are older than 36 hours, and that page repeats every 6 hours. A saved alert stamp from before the two-tick fields is treated as already paged. An unwritable state file pages on that run. `/data` is used only when it is already a directory. The MLB in-season check looks at the past 14 days, not the next 14, so the fortnight before Opening Day stays quiet. A `worker_jobs` failure on the last attempt still pages. A watched job whose last heartbeat is `error` is a digest line.

**Asked.** Mike approved the ops-alert review: keep real outages immediate,
and put the rest in one 7:15 ET digest. Credits page only under 5 days of

`settlement_stranded`) only after the condition is still true on the next
run. Clean rate waits an hour and ignores `aborted` and `deploy-cutoff`.

page that lasted 30 minutes. WARN does not page. The heartbeat watchdog is
unchanged and still immediate. `tracking/pipeline_watch.py` is the digest:
SKIPPED health rows are not findings, model-quality FLAGGEDs are,
Monday calibration deltas are, finished worker jobs are a count. The
calibration sweep and the calibration watch no longer post. A successful
worker job does not post. A starting daily retry does not post.
`announce_daily_cap` pages once per ET day when the catch-up has stopped.
MLB offseason checks use the 190-day skip budget and stay SKIPPED when no
MLB game is inside 14 days. UFC rows with no final are not `final_scores`.

still prefers `RAILWAY_VOLUME_MOUNT_PATH`, and `/data` when Railway set
`RAILWAY_ENVIRONMENT` and that directory exists.

**Verified.** The ops-alert test files, the health skip budget, the failure
alerter, the pipeline watch, calibration watch, daily retry, job queue,
injury feed, heartbeat watchdog, and the system-health meta-step tests.

**Not done.** No threshold, stake, pause, grading, or member `push_sent`
change. NFL live `_alert_ops` stays unwired while that job is disabled.

## 2026-11-16 — Discord pick posts link the matchup to the score page

**Asked.** Mike: when a pick posts to Discord, the matchup in the pick should
be a markdown link to that game's live score and final result. Every pick
post (pre-game channels, live channels, free pick, void notice). Eggz and
ParlayMatt, and the results recap, only where a matchup is actually written.

**What changed.** `tracking/score_links.py` builds the URL.
`tracking/discord_notifier.py` puts `[matchup](url)` on the context line of
the embed field value. The field name stays the pick label, word for word:
Discord does not render a masked link in a field name or an embed title.
The post body is still one embed and no message content, so the URL is not
a bare link Discord would unfurl.

An ESPN id already on the signal, or one read from `ncaab_events`,
`ncaabw_events`, `live_scoreboard`, or `tennis_moneyline_decisions`, becomes
`https://www.espn.com/<sport>/game/_/gameId/<id>` (tennis:
`/tennis/match/_/gameId/<id>`). EuroLeague uses the game-center query built
from `EUROLEAGUE_<season>_<code>`. Anything else is one ESPN scoreboard read
per sport and date, cached six hours, inside a one-second budget for the
batch. A timeout, a non-200, or two games that both match falls back to that
sport's scoreboard for the date. UFC falls back to the ufcstats upcoming
list (fight ids are not stored). A lookup error does not drop the pick and
does not touch `push_sent`.

**Verified.** `tests/test_discord_score_links.py` plus the Discord notifier,
live-field, void, publisher-lock, pick-integrity, best-price, stake, price
bound, and restate-price tests: 283 passed, 5 skipped, then the link file
again at 32 passed after the example ids were sample numbers. A slug-less
ESPN gamecast URL returned HTTP 200
(`…/mens-college-basketball/game/_/gameId/401918949`, measured 2026-11-16).
The NFL scoreboard for 20261116 returned 200.

**Not done.** No live Discord post from here. euroleaguebasketball.net is
the host the repo already records as 429 to automated fetches, so that
game-center URL was not fetched. Eggz, ParlayMatt, and the results recap
list sports and units, not matchups, so they gained no link. Pick
selection, grading, units, and the `push_sent` ledger are unchanged.

## 2026-11-15 — Back button: the React pill on every pushed screen

**Why.** Matt, 2026-11-15, with a screenshot: he tapped a push notification, it opened Pick Detail (UConn @
Louisville Under 122.5, live), and Back did nothing. He force-quit. This is the fourth report of a dead
native Back on iOS 27.x (Settings twice, Player Stats, now Pick Detail). The screenshot shows the
arrow-only system Back from 2026-11-08, so that workaround did not end it. Matt: "Just fix it however you
think."

**What the freeze trail showed.** Nothing yet. `app_errors` at 23:22Z and again after the force-quit had
no row after 18:14Z. A `restart` row is written on the next launch, so the trail for this freeze arrives
when the app is reopened. Pick Detail had one row in 30 days (2026-11-14, an ordinary relaunch).

**What changed.** `mobile/App.tsx`: the navigator's `screenOptions` now set `headerBackVisible: false`
and `headerLeft: () => <StackBackButton />`. That is the React "< Back" pill Player Stats and Settings
sections already carry, and neither has frozen since. Minimal display mode stays. `StackBackButton` pops,
or resets to Tabs when the stack is empty (a deep link that landed alone). `DeleteAccountScreen` drew
UIKit's Back with `headerBackVisible: !locked`; it now sets the pill, or `() => null` while a delete
runs. That screen still has its `beforeRemove` block and gesture lock. ByomBuilder's "Cancel" still
overrides the pill.

**Verified.** `mobile/scripts/verify_stack_back.ts` gained four checks: the default hides the system Back,
the default draws the pill, the pill's fallback is a reset to Tabs, and no screen sets
`headerBackVisible` to anything but `false`. All four FAIL on origin/master and pass here.
`verify_settings_home.ts` counted exactly two pills, so it now names the three buttons. tsc error set
byte-identical to origin/master (26, 0 in touched files). The 124 pytest files that name the app gave
the same result here as on origin/master: 6 failed and 3 errors on both, all in other areas.
UX review: ship with fixes. Its check break and stale comments are fixed. ux_scan found 0 issues.

**Not verified / not done.** Whether this ends the freeze. That cannot be checked without iOS 27 on a
device. Long titles beside the wider pill (BuiltInModelDetail, a long BYOM name, NCAAF TeamStats) were
not checked on a device either. They are plain UIKit titles, which UIKit lays out around bar items;
Player Stats overlapped because its title was a React view. iOS's long-press Back history menu is gone
on every screen. Android now draws the same pill as iOS, as Player Stats already did. ByomBuilder's
Cancel still has no empty-stack fallback; that predates this change.

**Follow-up, same PR (the trails arrived).** `app_errors` 85 (23:28Z) and 87 (01:52Z), iOS 27.3, both
bundles before this PR: each push-opened Pick Detail logged `screen PickDetail` about 0.2 s BEFORE
`app_state active`, then 10 and 3 taps with no screen change and no open pop-up, then the app left.
Earlier the same evening Pick Detail opened from the list while active and popped normally.
`usePushDeepLink` now holds a route until AppState is active (a ref, re-read after subscribing, so a
cold-start change cannot be missed) and replays it from the AppState listener. The freeze log gained
`push held` / `push replay` crumbs, and the Back crumb is written before navigating, so the next trail
shows whether each fix fired. That the active-state timing CAUSES the freeze is a hypothesis from two
trails; it is not reproduced. Row 54 (2026-11-14) opened Pick Detail from the list and ended with 4 taps
and no screen change, which may be reading or may be the same freeze.

## 2026-11-15 — The Retool ops dashboard's source moves into the repo

**Asked.** Mike: *"move the betting model retool application to the github
repo so I can make edits there, protect the app in retool if needed"*.

**What changed.** `retool/models-ops/` holds the source of the Retool app
"Betting Model — Models & Ops" (69 of its 70 files). `retool/README.md` says
which copy runs and how a change moves each way. `scripts/retool_app_files.py`
writes a Retool read into the folder (`write`), builds the upload for Retool's
import tool (`zip`), and compares the folder with Retool byte for byte
(`check`). `.gitattributes` keeps `retool/**` LF so that comparison holds on
Windows (`core.autocrlf=true` here). `tests/test_retool_app_files.py`, 11
tests; the byte-compare, CRLF, key-order and leftover-plan tests were each
watched failing with the logic removed.

**Measured.**
- **Protection is not available.** Retool's Source Control page shows GitHub
  as an Enterprise feature; Plans & billing says the account is on Team; the
  protect call answered `source_control_not_configured`. Retool's docs (read
  through a summarizer) also say Source Control writes a fixed `/apps-v2`
  layout and can fail on unknown files, so even on Enterprise it would want
  its own repo, not this one.
- **The copy is exact.** Published main and the newest thread listed the same
  70 files at the same sizes and line counts, so there was no unpublished work.
  All 69 files match Retool's sizes and line counts with no CR bytes, and
  `raw.githubusercontent.com` serves the same bytes for all 69 at `f892ed72`.
  `pnpm-lock.yaml` (201,834 bytes) is left out: the read tool truncates at
  64 KB.
- **The way back works as far as the import, on a throwaway app.** Retool's
  builder cannot download (shell limited to a few `git` commands,
  `pnpm install`, `vitest`; 2.05 credits to find out). Retool's import tool,
  aimed at a new app "SCRATCH - GitHub copy test (safe to delete)"
  (`50223a88-…`, Drafts, never published), accepted the zip (68 files, 0
  dropped) for 0.002 credits. The builder reported 63 files placed with its
  `copy_file` tool (the event log shows it in use) and `frontend/package.json`
  written by an edit. Read back: 66 of 66 comparable files identical;
  `package.json` differs only by the app's ID (Retool writes its own, keys
  re-ordered). Four files were refused as Retool's read-only files
  (`vitest.config.mjs`, `backend/package.json`, `frontend/vitest.config.ts`,
  `frontend/vitest.setup.ts`), so an edit to them here cannot reach Retool;
  they already matched. `backend/package.json` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` could
  not be read back from that app (reported missing; reason unknown). The
  import leaves `/plans/IMPORT_PLAN.md` and `/plans/import-build-plan.json`,
  which the builder refuses to delete ("Cannot delete protected file");
  `check` now skips them. Deleting an ordinary app file by thread message
  worked, confirmed in the file list. Python's default HTTP client is refused
  by Retool's firewall (error 1010); `curl` works.
- **The throwaway app was not published.** It is blocked until its two layout
  functions are approved; that approval is the owner's call and was not given.
  (The live app needed none; see the live push below.) Retool's write check
  does not flag `setModelVolume` (it writes through
  `SELECT * FROM set_model_volume(...)`).
- **No database writes from the test.** `model_volume_settings_log` holds four
  rows, all 2026-11-11 23:50–23:57 UTC, which account for every insert and
  delete the table's counters show. `dashboard_layouts` is empty; its counters
  (2 inserts, 2 deletes since 2026-03-30) carry no dates.
- **Credits.** Org Retool AI balance 992.07 of 1,000 after all the tests
  (resets 2026-12-14). For scale: the 11-13 redesign thread used 452 credits,
  the 11-12 Volume-tab thread 47.
- **Who can edit in Retool.** Two accounts, both admins (the SignalBase account
  and Matt's). The admin group owns every app and that entry is not editable,
  so nothing on this plan stops an admin editing in Retool.
- The source holds no credential: the database is reached through Retool's
  saved `supabase` connection by ID. The repo is public, so the dashboard's
  queries now are too; every table and view they name is already named
  elsewhere in the repo (all but `api_call_daily` in `data/migrations/`).

**The live push (mike: "Yes, push it").** A no-change push to the live app at
2026-11-16 01:46 UTC: import 0.0023 credits; 67 files matched on the draft
(the same two unreadable from a draft); no function approval pending, so
published commit `9275c647` (tag `rr-publish-1794793612838-9275c64`,
behaviour "latest"). The published app read back with no draft named: 69 of
69 files byte-identical, `check` exit 0, the two `/plans` files skipped (72
files listed now, was 70). Loaded in Chrome: Models tab 87 of 87 models,
Volume tab 59 of 87 with paused hidden, no console errors. Issue #1382 closed.

**Not done.** No change to app access or roles. No function approval given. The throwaway app is not deleted (its draft is left
with one file deliberately removed by the delete test): the Retool tools here
have no delete, and deleting it in the browser needs mike's yes.
## 2026-11-15 — Model building runs on the PC; the archive gets a backup copy in Storage (#1345)

**Asked.** mike: why put model builds in the cloud when they are occasional,
not continuous? Then, on keeping a second copy of the archive in Supabase
Storage: *"Yes, keep a copy."*

**Decided.** Model building runs on mike's PC: retrains, sweeps and
full-history BYOM backfills. It reads the monthly archive files there. The same
files also go to Supabase Storage as the backup. The cloud worker keeps making
picks. Its history reader raises for dates that have moved out.

**Measured.**
- On the PC (32 GB of memory, pyarrow): all 83,872,923 prop rows in 13.6 s, and
  all 57,362,605 `odds` rows in 12.0 s.
- One day of props (65,774 rows): 1.63 s from the files, 3.97 s from Supabase.
- Worker use of model building (`worker_jobs`): 7 retrains (09-01), 13 sweeps
  (09-16 to 10-28), 8 BYOM backfills (10-28 to 11-05).
- The worker's recurring jobs read the current season (`byom_refresh`) or
  graded picks (weekly calibration).
- Supabase Pro keeps 7 days of daily backups. That is why the archive needs a
  second copy.
- Plan prices are from Supabase's docs. Compute sizes are add-ons on Pro, and
  nothing needs the Team plan.

**Changed.** `docs/data_archive.md` sections 3c and 4. Still open on #1345: the
90-day window.

## 2026-11-15 — A restart relaunches every in-play loop at boot (#1359)

**What happened.** A merge restarted the in-play service at 23:59 ET on
2026-11-14. The college football supervisor fires only 11:00-23:50 ET, so the
new container's first tick was 11:00 ET the next morning. North Dakota State
at Hawai'i had no in-play model from the 2nd quarter on (last stored state
23:56:09 ET). Inside a window, a restart still left up to 10 minutes uncovered.

**What changed.** `scheduler.schedule_boot_relaunch`, called from `main()`
after the boot catch-ups, gives each in-play supervisor's own job a first run
60 s after boot, 5 s apart: college football, MLB, NFL leader, the retired NFL
worker if switched on, NBA, NCAAB, NCAABW, EuroLeague, then the pre-game
poller. It is the same job, not a second one, so `max_instances=1` still stops
a second copy of a running loop. After the boot run each job goes back to its
own schedule. The startup banner now prints that first run time.

**Why 60 s.** Railway stops the old container once the new one is active, the
old scheduler passes the signal to its loops, and none handles it. Measured: on
11-14 the old college football loop's heartbeat ended in the same second the
new scheduler started (23:59:17 ET). On 11-15 the old in-play container was
removed 3 s after the new one went live (deployments 775b46cb and ce76a1ff).
Nothing outside one process stops two copies of a loop.

**Left out.** The DraftKings and Bovada in-play feeds: off unless switched on,
and they run their full 15 minutes whether or not a game is live.

**Verified.** `tests/test_inplay_boot_relaunch.py`, 6 tests. The `main()` test
fails with "ncaaf_live_loop has no boot run" when the one call is removed. One
test starts the real APScheduler with stub loops and sees each run once, then
return to its cron.

## 2026-11-15 — NCAAB: a bet on a postponed game (Washington @ Utah); moved games found and settled

mike: *"Ncaab pick Utah v Washington but is that correct ? I can't find the game"*.

**What happened (measured).**
- The FAA ground stop of 2026-11-13 postponed Washington @ Utah twice. It moved
  from Sat Nov 14 5pm MT to Sun Nov 15 noon MT, then to Sat Dec 5. Utah posted
  the Dec 5 date at 4:04 PM on Nov 14. ESPN kept event 401921760 and now has it
  at 2026-12-06T00:00Z.
- Our row caught the first move (updated 2026-11-14 11:03Z) and not the second.
  Dec 5 was past the 14-day schedule refresh, and the 15th's board no longer
  listed the game.
- `ncaab_moneyline_own_spread` bet **Utah ML at Fanatics −135** at 15:05Z and
  posted it to Discord and push at 10:05 ET. By then DraftKings (06:17Z),
  FanDuel (07:19Z), Caesars (00:17Z), BetRivers and Hard Rock (08:19Z) had
  pulled the game. Only BetMGM and Fanatics still quoted it.
- Left alone, the row would have moved around Nov 21, and the Dec 5 final would
  have graded the pick (`_game_settle_window_days` reaches back to it).
- Book rules: Fanatics (MA v.10) basketball "must start before midnight local
  time"; postponed events void "unless ... within 48 hours". BetMGM (MA,
  2026-02-26) basketball "not continued within 48 hours after initial start
  date, betting will be cancelled".

**What changed (PR #1375).**
- `ncaab_espn_ingestor`: a stored, unscored event missing from its date's board
  is read from `summary?event=<id>`, and its new date, tip and status are
  applied. A tip before 06:00 ET is skipped, because ESPN files it on the
  previous board.
- `paper_tracker._void_rescheduled_picks`: an unsettled BET whose game now
  starts more than 48h from its `game_time` settles NO_ACTION. It runs before
  every grader, for every sport.

**Verified.**
- 9 new tests fail on master and pass. Master's code on this game leaves the row
  on the 15th and keeps the date due on every pass.
- On the 80 test files that touch either module, branch and master have the same
  single failure (`test_multi_book_odds::test_closing_line_reads_name_the_book`,
  pre-existing). Branch passes 2,330, master 2,321.
- Production: the fixed results step moved the row to 2026-12-05 /
  2026-12-06T00:00:00Z. The dry run read 28 unsettled BETs and found 1 over 48h.
- Pick 4498275 was settled NO_ACTION at 2026-11-15T17:46:21-05:00 on mike's
  "yes". The first hand write was refused by the auto-mode classifier.
- No BET since 2026-08-01 had moved more than 24h, so the rule touched no
  history.
- The only other open BET on a started game (Ole Miss @ Oklahoma, NCAAF) was
  played, 24-17.

**Not done.**
- No Discord note about the move (mike: no).
- Moves under 48h still grade on the rescheduled game, because the books differ
  there.
- Whether the Dec 5 make-up game can post its own moneyline pick under the old
  publish key: #1374.
- The code graph is not rebuilt for these two files: #1317.
- ESPN also 404s event 401928387 (Jarvis Christian @ Grambling, Nov 5, no
  picks). That costs one free summary call per pass until it leaves the 14-day
  look-back on Nov 19.

## 2026-11-15 — Horse racing: the first market audit on the free files

mike: *"pickup the pr that was created and merged on horse racing. do
additional research, invoke model skill"* (PR #1357, the scope).

**Stored first.** `scripts/horse/store_files.py` put every downloaded CSV in
Supabase before any table was read: 86 files (82 Betfair ANZ monthly CSVs,
four Hong Kong CSVs), 360.5 MB raw, 97.4 MB gzipped, in
`nfl_research_data_backup` under `source = 'horse_racing|…'`. The existing
per-file table was used so no DDL (and no PostgREST reload) ran during the
NFL Sunday slate. `--verify` restored every row and matched its sha256; the
PC mirror then held 3,780 rows against Supabase's 3,780.

  track size, field size and a logit on the scheduled-start prices all null.

  Churchill 17.5%), Hong Kong offered at FanDuel Racing and Xpressbet
  (commingled with the HKJC as of 2014; 2026 not confirmed), fixed odds only in New Jersey and Colorado, no exchange.

## 2026-11-15 — The sportsbook sheet no longer lists the deciding book at its lock once its line has moved

Matt, from two screenshots of the Signals card "GB −3.5" (`nfl_opener_spread`,
pick 4375914, decided at FanDuel −110): the card read "FD line now −2.5 (−120)"
and the sheet listed "FanDuel −110" with no note, while Caesars read
"GB −2.5 · different line". *"We should call out the current line on the bet
line like we do for Caesars."*

**Measured** (Supabase, 2026-11-15 14:17 UTC, `NFL_2026_10_MIN_GB`):
`v_latest_odds_all_books` FanDuel spreads −2.5 at −120; `alt_game_lines`
FanDuel `alternate_spreads` −3.5 at +108. Caesars has no −3.5 row. So FanDuel
−110 at −3.5 was on offer nowhere: it was the record chip, which
`pickLineQuotes` keeps ahead of any fresher row.

**Changed** (hand-off and sheet only; the pick, hero, edge, stake and record
are untouched, §1c):

- `resolveBoardHandoff`: when `hero.movedLine` is set, the deciding book's lock
  chip becomes that book's current row at the pick's number when it has one
  (the ladder rung: FanDuel +108, opening the rung), the way every other book's
  row is built. With no such row the chip leaves the sheet.
- `otherLineQuotesForPick`: the deciding book is no longer same-line by
  construction. With priced rows and none at the pick's number it is listed at
  its line now ("GB −2.5 · different line", −120). With no row at all it keeps
  its chip (missing is not moved).
- The same-book Now override on the hand-off is skipped on a moved line; it
  would have put the −2.5 price on a −3.5 quote.
- `PickCard`: the sheet's line note shows only when the sheet's Bet is the
  moved-line quote.

**Verified**: `verify_other_lines.ts` section 6 is the production fixture; 5 of
its checks failed before the fix (FanDuel −110, `otherLine=false`) and pass
after. The failing set of all `verify_*.ts` scripts and the `tsc` error set are
byte-identical to `origin/master`. 40 Python-side mobile tests pass.

**UX review** (`frontend-ux-designer`, scan 0 findings, ship with fixes). Taken:
the sheet's line note keys on `decidingBookOnlyAtMovedLine`, not on two prices
being equal; the lock chip is dropped only when a priced row for that book is
held, so a line moved on the latest-DK row alone never leaves a dash (check
watched failing without the guard). Left for Matt: the Pick Detail chips, the
sheet when the deciding book is the member's only book and has no rung, and a
"Prices now at GB −3.5" list heading.

**Not done**: Pick Detail's "Betting lines" chips still show the record chip at

## 2026-11-15 — Signals "now" line: green when the number got better, red when worse

Matt, from a screenshot of the Signals board ("FD line now −2.5 (−120)", "DK
now (−105)" under the hero): *"I like when a line goes down it shows red or if
the line improves in your favor it goes green."*

**What changed.** `nowLineTone` in `mobile/src/lib/signalsCard.ts` compares
the number on the board now with the pick's, from the view of someone betting
it now. It uses the same rule as `movementFromLatest`'s `lineMovedAgainst`. A
spread is better when the picked side gets more points. An Over is better when
the total falls, and an Under when it rises. A moved line decides; when the
line is unchanged, the price decides (longer payout is better). PickCard draws
the Now line in `betInk` / `avoidInk` with an up / down arrow beside it. The
spoken Now line ends "better than the pick" or "worse than the pick", so colour
is not the only carrier. Nothing moved, or nothing readable, stays grey.

**Judgement call.** When the line and price move in opposite directions, the
line decides. On the screenshot, GB −3.5 −110 → −2.5 −120 shows green.

**Verified.** 14 new checks in `scripts/verify_pick_card.ts` cover both
screenshot cards, spreads home / away (home-relative `scored_line`), totals,
props, moneyline, even money (+100 = −100) and the PickCard wiring. Flipping
the home-spread rule fails two of them. `tsc` error set identical to master;
`ux_scan --changed` 0; `verify_a11y` ALL PASS; the mobile test set has
master's failure set. **Not done:** the designer-agent review stopped on the
account's usage limit (resets 04:30 UTC), so the PR waits for it; not seen on
a device.

## 2026-11-15 — all-time average odds is the implied-probability mean

Mike, on the review of #1356: "Yes" to switching the Average odds line from the mean of the decimal prices to the implied-probability mean, and to merging it.

**What changed.** `average_taken_american` converts each taken price to implied probability (1/decimal), averages those with equal weight, and converts the mean back to the nearest American price. A pick with no taken price stays out and is not filled with -110. The line label and its place under the win rate are unchanged. One +2500 among fifty -110s is -106 this way; the decimal-payout mean of the same prices is +138. Thresholds, stakes, and pause state were not changed.

## 2026-11-15 — NCAAF identity: drop the minted LSU @ Ole Miss row

`ncaaf_game_identity` went STALE on `2026-11-21 Ole Miss x2`. The schedule
row is `NCAAF_2026-11-21_wofford_ole-miss` (CFBD, week 12, commence
`2026-11-21T17:00:00Z`). ESPN's 2026 Ole Miss schedule has Wofford at that
kickoff and LSU at Ole Miss on 2026-09-19, final. LSU's 2026-11-21 game is
at Tennessee. The second row, `NCAAF_2026-11-21_lsu_ole-miss`, was minted
by `run_historical_odds_range` from The Odds API snapshot
`2025-12-01T16:55:37Z` (ledger: snapshot_date 2025-12-01, hour 17, pulled
2026-11-14 21:58:14Z), which contained `LSU Tigers @ Ole Miss Rebels` at
`2026-11-21T17:00:00Z`. No odds, picks, or other `game_id` children were on
it. The check is a true positive. Odds do not replace Wofford.

**What changed.** `drop_ncaaf_unscheduled_opponents` runs before every
games upsert in the odds ingestor. It drops an NCAAF mint whose kickoff is
more than 120 days after the snapshot, and a mint whose home team and date
already have a different canonical opponent. An unresolved spelling
(`Citadel Bulldogs`) still mints. The worker migration
`ncaaf_drop_phantom_lsu_ole_miss_2026_11_21.sql` deletes the phantom only
while it is the unscored live LSU row and the Wofford row is still there,
and it returns without deleting if any child table has a row.
`tests/test_ncaaf_unscheduled_opponent.py` and
`tests/test_ncaaf_phantom_lsu_ole_miss_migration.py` passed (30 tests with
the existing identity and dateless-event tests).

Review on the PR: a stored live mint must not count as the schedule.
`week` is the marker, because the odds upsert sets `data_source='live'`
and does not clear `week`. Measured the same day: Wofford is in
`ncaaf_teams` (fcs); on the forward slate, 23 rows are `live` with week
set and 6 are `live` with week NULL (the mints). The lead window stays on
the historical paths only. A failed schedule read rolls back its
savepoint. The migration skips a child table that has no `game_id` column.

**What was deliberately not done.** No pause, threshold, or stake change.
LSU prices are not moved onto the Wofford row. Five forward-slate pairs
that share a kickoff and differ by one calendar day (ET odds id vs the UTC
CFBD id kept from 2026-08-29: Miami (OH) @ Kent State, Ball State @ Ohio,
Clemson @ Duke, SMU @ Notre Dame, Texas @ Texas A&M) are a different split.
`ncaaf_game_identity` groups on `game_date`, so it does not see them.

## 2026-11-15 — all-time header gains the average CLV

The all-time field of the daily results recap now has a third line under the win rate and the average odds: `Avg CLV: +1.8% (N measured)`. The number is the mean of `picks.clv_pct`, which is already percent (`(taken decimal / close decimal − 1) × 100`), and only where `clv_method` is `standard_v2`. A pick with no closing line is left out. An in-play pick is left out. A 0% close is in the mean as 0, and it is inside the measured count. One decimal, with the sign. Thresholds, stakes, and pause state were not changed. `tests/test_discord_notifier.py` passed, 134 tests.

## 2026-11-15 — daily results all-time block: units order, win rate, average odds

Mike: in the all-time section of the daily results recap, sort the sports by units (best first) and, before those lines, show the all-time win rate and the all-time average odds.

**What changed.** `render_all_time_fields` orders sports by all-time units, highest first. An exact tie breaks on the sport name. The day's own sports, in `results_embed`, stay alphabetical. The overall all-time field keeps the record line, then two lines: `Win rate` is wins over wins+losses, one decimal, with that fraction (a push is graded and is not a decision), and `Average odds` is American. The average is the unweighted arithmetic mean of the decimal odds of the graded picks that have a taken price, converted back to the nearest American price. `-110` and `+150` average to `+120`, not `+20`. Even money (decimal 2.0) is `+100`. A missing, zero, or non-finite price is left out and is not filled with `-110`. When that drops a row, the line says how many prices were in the mean. Eggz and the standalone all-time embed use the same renderer, so they get the same block. Thresholds, stakes, and pause state were not changed.

**Verified.** `tests/test_discord_notifier.py` (the new cases plus the file) and `tests/test_eggz_picks.py`. The two Eggz schedule tests import `apscheduler`; they passed once that package was installed.

**Not done.** The staff test post did not go out. `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_OPS` is set on the Railway worker. This environment's Railway connection returns variable names only, and `DATABASE_URL` and the webhook are not in the environment. No Railway CLI. Nothing was posted to `#results` or any other channel.

## 2026-11-15 — Picks filter: a Final chip for today's completed games, with a graded result chip

Matt, on the Signals filter sheet: *"On this filter it would be great to filter
on completed games from the day."* Asked two questions; he chose finished games
**hidden unless Final is picked** (counts, sport badges and Bet all stay on
bettable games) and **final score + W/L/P** on the card once graded.

and `gameStateOf` mapped `final` / `ended` to it, but `readTodayBoard` drops
every `isGameOver` row before the screen sees it, so the chip could only ever
appear for the rare game the live poll called final before the score landed.

  returns `finishedData` (today's finished games, retired and VOID-hidden rows
  still out) apart from `data` / `pausedData`. No new query: these rows were
  already fetched and discarded on the phone. Every other consumer still reads
  the open board.
- `lib/gameStateFilter.ts`: empty selection = Not started + Live (Final
  opt-in); `toggleGameState` starts a tap from that default, so Final ADDS
  finished games rather than replacing the board; summary reads "Final hidden"
  by default and names every state when Final is in (never a bare "All" pill).
- `screens/PicksHomeScreen.tsx`: `boardItems` = open board + this view's
  finished picks (Signals: graded → `passesRecordFilter`, ungraded →
  `passesActionFilter`), and every cut runs on it. Header counts, sport badges,
  Bet all and `todayStats` are untouched. An empty board whose games are all

- `components/PickResultChip.tsx` + `lib/pickResult.ts`: "W +0.91u" beside the
  FINAL pill on a graded BET, units from `postedPickUnits` (the recap's and
  Record tab's number), spoken right after the verdict. `RESULT_TONE` is now
  the one W/L/P table, shared with `DailyResultsModal`.
- Chips draw lit from the effective selection.

## 2026-11-14: Decision timing is a per-model parameter; the NCAAF moneyline model replayed at every hour (PR #1339)

From a claude.ai session the same day (mike). `ncaaf_moneyline_market` fired 6
underdog BETs at its 14:00 UTC check. mike's rule: when a model decides is a
per-model parameter, chosen by out-of-sample expected units; about 17 rule
models said "decided once (CLAUDE.md §1c)", a misreading, since §1c locks a
BET and never freezes a game that did not bet.

**The rule, written down.** CLAUDE.md §1c: the lock is on a BET, not a game;
when and how often a model decides is its own measured parameter (the
sentence "game-level at the first scoring run" was the one being read as
"decide once"; the §7 encoding rule lost its story, already in
`docs/rules_evidence.md`, to stay under the size cap). The league-models
skill: "When the model decides" in `references/porting-traps.md` replaces two
cadence notes and keeps the hockey evidence (18% of games fired live against
3.5% in the backtest, 5-18, -9.10 units); `references/shipping.md` and the
ship step say the timing is measured. Four NCAAB and NCAAF doc lines no
longer cite §1c for "never decided again". The card docstrings that say
"EACH GAME DECIDED ONCE (CLAUDE.md 1c)" were left as they are; each is
corrected when its model's timing is replayed (issue #1342).

**The purchase (mike: "Buy all of it now").** Worker jobs 1316678-1316680,
`requested_by` mike, keys `ncaaf-hourly-history-2023/-2024/-2025` (also in
`jobs/declared_jobs.json`, inserting nothing): every hour 00-23 UTC, h2h and
spreads, ten books, 2023-08-17..12-09, 2024-08-15..12-07, 2025-08-14..12-06.
Measured cost 20 credits a snapshot (`api_call_log`). 2023 finished 19:03 UTC
(the worker restarted about 18:15; the job resumed on its ledger without
re-buying): 1,724 calls in its second attempt, 34,480 credits, 1,124,086 rows,
3 HTTP 502s from the feed (unbilled), so 3 hours (2023-12-04 19Z and 20Z,
2023-12-05 05Z) are unledgered and need a fill job. Historical rows are
protected from the odds clean-up (`data/prune_odds.PROTECTED_SOURCES`).

**Reading the rows without loading production.** Today's earlier entry
measured the nightly copy as the cause of most 11-12/13 timeouts, and a
by-hand copy of `odds` re-counts all 50M rows. `scripts/ncaaf_hourly_extract.py`
reads only the new rows by primary-key range: 3 seconds a 50,000-id slice,
waiting while more than 6 other queries run and through minutes :15-:27 ET.

**The replay.** `scripts/ncaaf_moneyline_timing.py` runs the live card's own
`quotes_at` and `decide` at every whole UTC hour, then grades 26 timings
fixed before any result: each fixed hour on the game's Eastern date, every
hour until it bets (the first BET locks), and the last read before kickoff;
chosen on 2023-2024, confirmed on 2025, BH-FDR at q 0.10 over all 26. Checked:
on the stored 14Z/23Z pulls its 14:00 timing holds all 311 research bets
(same game, side, book, price; +14.70 units) and its 23:00 timing 110 of the
research's 114 evening bets (the other 4 kick off 23:00-23:03 UTC, inside the
card's five-minute rule). On the bought 2023 hours every row's own stamp is
within a minute of its pull at every UTC hour (median 0.4-0.8 minutes, 100%
inside the card's 60-minute window), so a quiet hour is not thinned by stale
stamps. Games outside the purchase's windows (bowls) are left out of scope.
Tests: `tests/test_ncaaf_moneyline_timing.py` (fail with the first-bet lock
keeping the last bet, with the FDR not dividing by the timings searched, and
with the five-minute rule removed).

**Result: no timing clears** (`docs/decision_timing.md` has every timing's
table). 14:00 UTC, the live check, is the timing chosen on 2023-2024 (+13.88
units, one-sided p 0.097); no timing survives BH-FDR at q 0.10; on 2025 alone
14:00 made +0.81 units on 93 bets (+1.4%, interval -21.0% to +23.0%, p 0.46).
Over the three seasons 14:00 holds the research's 311 bets game for game
(+14.70 units). "Every hour until it bets" made 951 bets, +8.32 units (+1.6%),
-3.52 in 2025; the last read before kickoff -1.71 units. At every fixed hour
the bet price averages 3-4% below Pinnacle's no-vig close (14:00 -3.83%,
beating it on 22.9%). The live timing is unchanged.

**The purchase overwrote stored kickoffs; repaired.** The historical job
upserts every snapshot's games, and its snapshots do not arrive in time order:
1,690 rows of 2023-2025 games changed `commence_time` (most by minutes from
post-start snapshots; some by hours, Florida at Miami 2025-09-20 from 23:30 to
16:00 UTC, from a fill job's 2025-09-11 snapshot written last), and CFBD's
scored rows among them had `data_source` set to `live`. Fixed in the ingestor
(`_history_game_rows`: a snapshot sets a stored kickoff only inside the 24
hours before it, before the start; otherwise it only inserts a missing game).
Restored from the local copy taken 05:43 UTC that morning (saved aside before

values kept in `games_ncaaf_kickoff_restore_20261114` (row security on, no

**Three replay fixes from the first full run**, each with a test: read only

## 2026-11-14 — Crash log: send expo-updates' own error log

Matt sent a TestFlight crash (1.3.0 (14101), 07:08 ET): the app died 0.24 s
after launch in expo-updates' `ErrorRecovery.crash()`. The bundle it picked
failed, the relaunch from cache failed too, and `UpdatesReaper` was deleting
old update files on another thread at the same moment. No JS ran, so
`app_errors` held no row for that launch, and Apple's report leaves out the
exception text. The cause is unconfirmed. On that device the app ran the
11-13 02:59Z bundle all day, through eight newer publishes.

Matt approved logging it ("Yes for #2"). `beginLaunch` (`mobile/src/lib/crashLog.ts`)
now reads expo-updates' own log file (`Updates.readLogEntriesAsync`, 24 h,
read capped at 3 s). When the new error entries include a failed launch, one
`emergency_launch` row goes with `extra.source = 'updates_log'`. Offline
download errors alone are not a row: expo-updates' iOS source logs those at
error level too (`FileDownloader.swift`). No new server kind, so no migration, and the
queue cannot be stuck behind a kind the RPC refuses. Verified:
in `mobile/scripts/verify_crash_log.ts`: 14 new checks, all pass, each watched
failing with its code broken. The UX review (ship with fixes) found two real
defects in the first draft, both fixed: a hung native read held the `running`
marker, and entries were marked sent before their row was stored. tsc: 26
errors, all in the OTA workflow's tolerated baseline, none in touched files.
Not verified on a device: whether the log write survives a real abort
(`docs/app_errors.md`, "Native aborts before JS").

## 2026-11-14 — No data is ever deleted: the rule, what still deletes, and the archive design

mike: *"the supabase DB is getting too large. Can we store the majority of
historical data past X days … on my machine … We should not be deleting any
data, that needs to be a hard rule that only mike can override … nightly
backups should only be a diff … optimize for speed, performance AND results."*

**What changed.** The rule, in CLAUDE.md §1b ("NO DATA IS EVER DELETED"),
made room for by moving two passages of evidence out (the 655 MB story to
`docs/rules_evidence.md`; the encoding trap's detail was already there) so
the always-loaded layer stays at its cap. The design and every measurement:
`docs/data_archive.md`.

**Measured.** 98 GB on Micro (1 GB memory; Supabase sizes it for 10 GB).
Ninety percent of `player_prop_odds` is under 90 days old, so a 90-day archive
moves about 17 GB, not the majority. 61% of the prop rows on three NBA games of
11-13 repeat the row before. The pruner deleted 2.50M `odds` + 6.27M prop rows
in seven morning runs (11-08 to 11-14), and the PC mirror drops pruned rows from
its own copy. Last night's mirror pulled 67.6M rows (27% of what it holds),
and its range counts have read about 792 GB since 11-03.

**Then stopped the deletes** (mike: *"Yes, stop tonight"*). One switch,
`config.DATA_DELETION_APPROVALS` (empty), is asked by the odds prune, the API
call log prune (its daily rollup still runs), `app_timings` and `player_news`.
The PC copy (`scripts/local_mirror.py`) keeps every row whose key is in none
of its chunk's replacements in `<table>/vanished/`, and every removed bucket
file in `storage_vanished/`; the latest-line caches are left out. Tests:
`tests/test_no_data_deleted.py` (6 of 8 failed before the gate) and four new
cases in `tests/test_local_mirror.py` (all four failed on an untouched copy).
The mechanism tests of the four jobs now approve their own job explicitly.
The merge waits for the night's in-play games to finish (a merge redeploys the
in-play service too), so the 00:30 ET PC run of 11-15 still uses the old script.
The whole PC copy was snapshotted first, at 17:15 ET, to
`C:\Users\micha\Documents\betting_local_copies\snapshots\supabase_mirror_20261114_before_stop`
(6,086 files, 3.47 GB, every file SHA-256 identical to the source).

**Deliberately not done.** `lineup_slots`, `capper_picks` and the stat-table
rewrites still replace rows; the PC copy now keeps what they remove. Nothing
was archived. The other chat's five index fixes were checked, not built.
Full suite: 17 failed, 12,785 passed on the branch; 17 failed, 12,773 passed
on an untouched master copy; the two failure sets are identical, so none is
new and none was touched.

## 2026-11-14 — Bet all tracked one of three placed bets on the phone

Matt, with a screenshot of NCAAB Signals: *"I placed all three of these picks
but when I came back only one was showing tracking even though I said track
all."*

**Measured.** `tracked_bets` holds all three for his device, written in the
same second (21:16:23 UTC): Charleston Southern ML (4486048), Saint Joseph's ML
(4486005), Oakland -1.5 (4486000). So the server write worked and the phone's
own list (AsyncStorage `trackedBets.pickIds.v1`) lost two.

**Cause.** `useTrackedBets.track` wrote `[...ids, id]` from the render's `ids`.
Bet all's Placed called it once per leg in one tap, so each write replaced the
one before it and only the last pick stayed. A single Track tap never hit it.

**Fix.** `trackPicks` / `untrackPicks` in `hooks/useTrackedBets.ts` wait for the
stored list to load, then read the module's latest list and persist with no
await between, so concurrent calls build on each other. The hook's `track`,
`untrack` and `untrackPick` go through them; `trackMany` is new, and Bet all
tracks a book's legs in one call.

**Verified.** `mobile/scripts/verify_tracked_batch.ts` runs the real store on an
in-memory AsyncStorage shim (`scripts/shims/async-storage.mjs`); with the list
read before the await it fails exactly as on the phone (only 9 of 7, 8, 9
kept). tsc error set unchanged; mobile pytest failures identical to master.

**Not done.** The two picks missing on Matt's phone are not restored by this:
the app reads tracked picks from the phone only. Tapping Track on each puts
them back (the server row already exists, and a repeat insert is ignored).

## 2026-11-14 — Bet all: every pre-game signal for a sport, as single bets

Matt: *"the ability for a user to place all single bets on all the signal bets
for a particular sport … on either one sportsbook or the sportsbook with the
best odds."* Then: *"They are all singles and should not be considered for a
parlay"*, *"Default to best line"*, *"Yes ask if they placed the bet"*, and
*"don't cover live bets but we should call out it doesn't."*

**What changed.** A row at the top of the Signals board, "N ready to bet as
singles", with the line "Pre-game signals only. Live bets aren't included" and
a "Bet all N" button. It opens `BetAllSheet`. **Best line** (the default) puts
each pick at its best price among the member's books, grouped by book. **One
sportsbook** shows each of the member's books with how many picks it has, and
names the picks the chosen book does not have. Every pick starts ticked. Start
turns the books into a list opened one at a time; each leg shows the stake to
enter (a link carries none), sized at that book's price with `stakeFor`, as
the card sizes it. Coming back from a book asks "Did you place these N bets as
singles?" — Placed adds them to tracked bets (`useTrackedBets.track`), Not yet
keeps the step open, Skip closes it. Signals not included are listed with the
reason (Game started, Price check, Old price, Not at your books, No price).

**Eligibility is the card's.** The block in `PickCard` that works out the price
check, price age, the model's price and the member's books moved unchanged to
`lib/pickPriceView.ts`, and `lib/betAll.ts` reads the same function, so Bet all
offers a pick exactly when its card offers the bet. Live picks, non-BET rows,
paused, preview and settled picks never reach it.

**Singles, never a parlay.** `combineSinglesLinks` (`lib/betslipLinks.ts`) is a
separate builder from the parlay hand-off's `combineBetslipLinks`: BetMGM goes
out `type=Single` (the parlay one sends `type=combo`), and Caesars keeps the
stored `&dl=betslip`, which the parlay combiner drops. Hard Rock and BetRivers
have no multi-pick form, so their step offers one "Add pick i of n" per pick.
Whether a book's slip opens on Straight or Parlay when several picks arrive is
the book's choice, not the link's; the sheet tells the member to choose
Straight. `BET_ALL_ONE_LINK_BOOKS` switches a book to one link per pick if a
phone shows it opening a parlay.

**Measured before building** (Supabase, read-only): betslip links on
2026-11-14/15 rows — DraftKings 99.9% of 9,970 game lines, FanDuel, BetMGM,

## 2026-11-14 — Picks filter: a Status section (Not started / Live / Final)

able to filter on games that are live or not played yet."*

**What changed.** A Status section in the Picks filter sheet, between Date and
Games: multi-select chips Not started / Live / Final, each with its pick count.
Empty = every state; the section hides when the board holds only one state;
while it narrows it shows a removable pill, counts in the Filters badge, and
Clear all clears it. Not persisted, and dropped on a sport switch (Date's
contract). A selection the board cannot honour reads as All rather than
emptying the board. The Games list below follows it.

LIVE / FINAL with — against the same live-state poll. `ended` (clock window run
out, no score yet) is grouped as Final, which is what `TeamsBoard` already
labels it. `mobile/src/lib/gameStateFilter.ts`.

it fail with `ended` mapped to Live. `tsc --noEmit` error set identical to
origin/master, 0 in touched files. pytest `-k "mobile or filter or
context_budget or anon or picks_home"`: 714 passed, the same 15
failures/errors with and without the change (scratch-database tests).
UX review: ux_scan 0 findings; empty-board copy now names Status (fixed);
"Final" for `ended` kept (matches TeamsBoard); chip counts taken before the
Date cut, as Date's are (not changed); a one-tap "Bettable" chip on the bar
left as a decision for Matt.
## 2026-11-14 — Sportsbook hand-off: open the book's app, or the App Store

Matt, after the 2026-11-12 probe: FanDuel "worked"; the BetMGM `/sportsapp/`
test links "still took me to the website" (how they were opened was not
said); "Yes I have the app"; then *"You need to fix this. Make it open the App
Store if I dont have the app or open the app if I have it."* Branch
`claude/busy-faraday-4u5o6o`.

**Why it could not be done in JS.** `Linking.openURL` opens Safari when no app
claims an https link and reports success either way, and `canOpenURL` only
answers for a custom scheme. Only DraftKings' scheme is verified. No public
source names BetMGM's (web search, 2026-11-14); `itunes.apple.com` is not
reachable from the sandbox (proxy 403, WebFetch ENOTFOUND).

**What changed.**
- `mobile/modules/open-in-app` (new local Expo module, iOS): one call,
  `UIApplication.open(url, options: [.universalLinksOnly: true])`. True when an
  installed app took the link, false when none did and nothing opened.
  Autolinking resolves it (`expo-modules-autolinking resolve --platform apple`
  lists pod `OpenInApp`, module `OpenInAppModule`).
- `mobile/src/lib/openInApp.ts`: `requireOptionalNativeModule`, so Android and
  a binary built before the module answer null and keep the old route.
- `appClaimedLink` (`betslipLinks.ts`): the link each book's app claims, from
  the probe. DraftKings and FanDuel: the stored link. Caesars: the filled
  `/us/<state>/bet/` link. BetMGM: the stored query moved from `/en/sports` to
  `/en/sportsapp/sports`, the only path its app claims. No link: the claimed
  root.
- `openBookBetslip`: unless the app is known to be missing, iOS is asked to
  open the claimed link in the app only. Yes: done. No, with no verified
  scheme saying it is installed: the App Store. Cannot ask: the old chain.
- `pr-ci.yml` now runs `verify_book_links.ts` (it was in no CI job).

**Verified.** `verify_book_links.ts` ALL PASS (new checks included). Watched
fail: with the BetMGM rewrite removed, the rewrite check failed; with the
store branch keyed on the wrong answer, the order check failed. tsc baseline
check exit 0. `ux_scan --changed` 0 findings.

**UX review** (frontend-ux-designer): no blockers. Fixed: the BetMGM claimed
link is now tried when a candidate scheme says installed too; an unparseable
URL is an error (cannot ask), never "not installed". Declined: on a `false`,
asking "App Store / Open in Safari" instead of going to the store. Matt asked
for the store.

**NOT verified, and only a phone can.** The Swift has not been compiled (EAS
does that). Whether BetMGM's app puts the selection on the slip from the
`/sportsapp/` link. What `universalLinksOnly` returns when the member has set
the domain to open in Safari (expected false, so the store page with "Open").

**Ships how.** `app.json` and `package.json` are unchanged, so the OTA guard
does not refuse and the JS goes out over the air, where it does nothing on
today's binary (module absent, null). The fix reaches a phone only with a new
TestFlight build (`mobile-build.yml`). Version left at 1.3.0 on purpose: a
bump would stop every 1.3.0 binary receiving updates until it is replaced, and
the JS is safe on both.

**Not done.** BetRivers (outside NJ), Hard Rock, Fanatics, Bally, betPARX and
Rebet: their apps claim no link we hold, so they keep link → store → site.
The two `NaN` betslip links are still flagged, not fixed.

## 2026-11-14 — Production database timeouts: the cause measured, four fixes, the instance size (issue #1328)

mike: *"Find the cause of the timeouts and fix."* 39 statement timeouts or
aborted transactions in `pipeline_log` from 2026-11-12, none from 11-05 to 11-11.

**The cause, measured.** The database is 97 GB (about 38 GB on 2026-10-01;
`odds` 20 GB, `player_prop_odds` 40 GB) on an instance with 256 MB of
shared buffers and 60 connections. Supabase's compute table matches that to
Micro: 1 GB, 11 MB/s and 500 IOPS baseline disk, bursting to 261 MB/s
"drawing on a disk IO budget. Once the budget is exhausted, performance
returns to baseline."
- 35 of the 39 failures fell inside the nightly copy to mike's PC. From
  11-12 that copy ran about 9 hours (05:30-14:35 UTC), against about 75
  minutes before, for the same rows (40.1M on 11-13, 38.9M on 11-10).
- Its read rate starts fast and then drops: 11-12 12,500 rows a second for
  25 minutes, then about 5,000, then about 1,300; 11-10 held 9,000-10,500
  for the whole run. That is the burst budget running out.
- The 15:17 UTC pass on 11-14, with no copy running, finished clean (49 of
  49 steps, 10m50s) while reading about 7-8 MB/s, with 46-75% of active
  statements waiting on disk. Load-dependent, on a disk held near baseline.
- The other 4 failures (03:22-04:36 UTC) fell inside the NCAAB in-play
  purchase, the live-board reads and the UFC prop-history stores.

**Fixed (branch `claude/db-timeouts`):**
- The aborted-transaction cascade. A timed-out read inside a larger
  transaction aborted it, and the next statement failed "current transaction
  is aborted": the injuries run lost every sport's rows (9 runs), the NFL
  prop scorer lost whole dates, player news lost a run. The injuries return
  ramp and player news's best-effort reads now run in a savepoint
  (`data.db.call_in_savepoint`). The NFL prop injury veto rolls back to its
  savepoint and, on a timeout, fails CLOSED (re-raises): an empty veto index
  would have let the card bet a player who is Out. Other errors still fail
  open.
- Three `injuries` indexes, built on production CONCURRENTLY at 15:28 UTC
  (17 s, all valid), with a recoverable migration. The latest-report read
  went from up to 119.7 s to 0.065 ms; the refresh gate's MAX(created_at)
  from up to 115 s to 0.054 ms; the return ramp from up to 118.7 s to
  0.044 ms. The return-ramp index is empty: no injuries row has an
  activation date.
- NBA first-basket stats (failing 3 of 3 runs today): the four statements
  read `nba_pbp` twice each; now one period-1 copy per rebuild, and last
  season only while it has no rows. Rolled-back comparison on production:
  identical rows for 2027 (366 and 4,126) and for 2026-27 (2,828 and 30,784).
- The copy's pause windows were in UTC (10:00-10:50, and :15-:27 of hours
  11-22), so from the 11-01 clock change it read straight through the 6am
  run and never paused for the overnight passes. Now Eastern: 05:40-07:15 ET
  and :15-:27 of every hour. The copy's launcher refreshes the script from
  master before each run, so a merge reaches the next 00:30 run.

**Not lost.** NHL shots-on-goal scoring failed at 06:45 and 07:29 and
succeeded at 08:26; NFL props failed at 07:53 and 09:54 and succeeded at 08:35
and 10:31. Games were hours away.

**Also merged the same morning, by another session:** #1255 runs the heavy
worker jobs one at a time in 05:40-07:15 ET. It does not cover the copy.

## 2026-11-14 — UFC: the fight-winner model's first betting run, no bet; five fighters it could not rate

The first 12:25 UTC run of `ufc_open_moneyline` since it went live beat OK
and placed no bet. A read-only replay of the card at the same moment (no
`--publish`) gave: 41 fights, 13 already decided, 18 with no fresh Pinnacle
quote, 10 with a fighter it could not rate.

**Pinnacle has not priced the 2026-11-21 card.** Read three ways: no
Pinnacle row in `odds` for any of its 12 fight ids (DraftKings and Caesars
8 each); the odds feed's live MMA board (1 credit) prices 13 events with
Pinnacle, all on the 2026-11-14 card, none of 16 on 2026-11-21; and
Pinnacle's own site lists only the 2026-11-14 card. The model needs
Pinnacle's price a week out, and Pinnacle posts later each year: in the
bought history its first price came a median 17.4 days out in 2021, 9.5 in
2025 and 7.6 in 2026 (to June); 87%, 73% and 62% of fights at least 7 days
out.

**Five veterans were unrated** because the feed spells them otherwise:
Sergey/Sergei Pavlovich, Joseph/Joe Pyfer, Daniel/Dan Hooker,
Charlie/Charles Radtke, Heili Alateng/Alatengheili. Each confirmed against
`fighters` with its UFC fight count and added to `config.UFC_NAME_ALIASES`.
The other four (Nik Bagley, Connor Hughes, Ernesto Rodriguez, Valeriu
Mircea) have no UFC record: debuts, which the live card cannot rate (the
backtest without debuts is +13.21% on 775).

**A double decision, found and closed.** With an alias in config, the name
lookup also resolves the old rows' spelling, and the ingest writes new pulls
under a new id, so one fight would have two fight keys and could be bet
twice. The card's fight key now maps both slugs through the aliases (test
fails without it). The card also logs each unrated fighter's name. Replayed
on the fix: Pavlovich, Pyfer and Hooker's fights reach the price check
under one key each; 4 unrated, all debuts.

**Also.** Two merges cut the 6am pipeline: #1324 (11:24 UTC, merged without
checking the clock) and #1323 (12:19 UTC, another session); each run closed
itself as `deploy-cutoff` and the new worker restarted it. A stray
`config.py` (dated 2026-10-13) in the temp folder shadows the repo's for any
script run from that folder; checks are now run from a folder without one.

## 2026-11-14 — EuroLeague: five pre-game rules live, and the period feed they decide on

mike, 2026-11-14: "I told you to build all the models", knowing each of the
five frozen EuroLeague leads missed part of the research bar. Branch
`claude/euroleague-pregame-models`, cut from `claude/euroleague-live-ht`
(#1314, not merged yet); pushed, not merged. `Updated-By: mike`.

**Built.** One rules module (`models/euroleague_pregame.py`) holding the five
research rules as written: the home moneyline at -200 or longer
(`euroleague_home_ml`), the favourite at -500 or shorter
(`euroleague_heavy_fav_ml`), the home underdog against the spread on
own-country games (`euroleague_home_dog_spread`), the first-half moneyline
underdog (`euroleague_h1_dog_ml`), and the first-quarter total from each club's
prior first-quarter share at margin 1.0 (`euroleague_q1_club_share_total`).
One card (`scripts/euroleague_pregame_card.py`, ledger
`euroleague_pregame_decisions`), one build (`scripts/euroleague_pregame_build.py`,
artifact `models/saved/euroleague_pregame_2027.json`), the live period feed
(`data/ingestors/euroleague_period_odds.py`: one per-event call per game 60, 10
and 2 minutes before tip, the period markets and the full game at the ten
period books, ledger `euroleague_period_live_fetches`, source
`odds_api_euroleague_period_live` protected from the prune, a balance floor),
and the window job that runs both every minute
(`scripts/euroleague_pregame_window.py`, `scheduler.run_euroleague_pregame_window`,
heartbeat 20 minutes). Config: each model's cut, EV floor, price floor (the big
favourites -4000), own probability, rule. Settlement: the first half on
quarters 1-2 and the first quarter on quarter 1 of `euroleague_period_scores`,
a tied half a push; the closing-line value of a period pick read from a pull
after it. The app: the five names (refined by the UX review), their page text,
probability notes, market mappings and the first-half and first-quarter wording
on the boards; the open-picks view and the dashboard names re-hashed.

**Verified.** The build's replay matches the research's own code bet for bet
on all eight reads (no difference either way, no tie broken differently): the
home moneyline 972 / +59.64 (DraftKings) and 984 / +64.80 (best book), the big
favourite 188 / +34.59 and 182 / +38.74, the home underdog spread 454 / +48.34
and 455 / +54.54, the first-half underdog 465 / +19.14, the first-quarter share
63 / +17.71. The tests (rules, card, feed, settlement, artifact, a real-Postgres
run of the card) were each watched failing under single breaks of the code
(the mutation list in the commit message's count). The touched app files
type-check with an error set identical to master's; every mobile verify script
was run on both trees.

**Found.**
- The brief said the home moneyline and the big favourite could not conflict.
  They do: when the favourite at -500 or shorter is the away club, the home
  club is the underdog the home moneyline bets. 6 games in the replay; both are
  written NONE (the same-pass rule), costing -1.00 and -0.69 units.
- Three settlement gaps for any EuroLeague period pick: no `h2h_h1` result arm,
  `totals_q1` in no totals arm, and every non-NCAAB period read
  `nba_period_scores` (pending forever). Fixed, with tests.
- The research cache gained one game (2026-11-13) after the research read it,
  and its full-game "close" is a stale board (the purchase ended 05:45 UTC that
  day). The replay compares on the research's own data state.
- The halftime loop checked its spread only against other spread bets; it now
  reads the pre-game rules' standing moneylines and spread too.
- `tests/test_prune_bounded.py`'s fixture changed shape with every new
  protected source (seed 11 lost its tie groups); its source draw has its own
  stream now.

**Not done.** No Discord channel or Railway variable (the pre-game channel is
being created; the card posts nothing to Discord until
`DISCORD_CHANNEL_EUROLEAGUE` or `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_EUROLEAGUE` is set). No paid
call: the feed's cost (about 15 credits a call, 45 a game) is derived from the
code's billing model.

## 2026-11-13 — Why the NHL moneyline model keeps betting Vancouver (no change)

mike: *"Why does the nhl model keep betting Canucks ML ... they suck and always
get killed"*. Branch `claude/nhl-moneyline-bad-team-check`. Investigation
only: no model, cut, stake or publishing change.

**Found.** Five of the model's 52 bets since the rebuild went live (10-16) were
VAN ML, +118 to +185: one win, three losses, 11-13 open. The model starts from
DraftKings' price and moves it. The rating lifted VAN in all five bets and the
penalty kill in four; those inputs raise a team when its numbers are worse.
The opponent's back-to-back, travel or goalie workload did the rest. Shots
against pushed VAN down, by less. VAN won 3 of 20 games that DraftKings priced
at 6.66 wins (chance of 3 or fewer: 5.9%).

**Graded.** The shipped walk-forward, re-run from the local mirror with no
database read, reproduced the shipped grade exactly (1,326 bets, +83.2 units
at EV 0.06). Without penalty kill, goals against and rating it makes +21.3
units on 1,062. The bets those inputs mostly made returned +6.0% on 235 but
beat Pinnacle's close 55.8% of the time, against 65.3% for the rest. Detail:
`docs/nhl_moneyline_rebuild.md`, "Why it keeps betting bad teams".

**Not done.** No input removed: taking them out loses about 62 units over five
seasons. The live split waits for ~250 settled bets (issue #1307, about
2027-03-06).
